- [ ] **The Pi is a single point of failure for every write, on both environments, and the Worker fallback only covers reads.** Found live on 2026-10-02: the Pi went off the network mid-release and **nobody could sign in or save anything on production**, while every public page still rendered. Three paths confirmed it was the Pi and not name resolution — mDNS (`lanna-pi.local`, `lanna-pi`) silent, the cached LAN address `192.168.68.102` (a Raspberry Pi Foundation MAC) answering neither ping nor port 22, and `POST https://lannacare.org/login` returning `503 x-lanna-served-by: pi-timeout` while `GET` returned 200. The Supabase auth config was checked and was correct, so this was not an auth fault: **"Continue with Google" is a server action, so a POST** — the flow dies before the redirect to Google is issued and the user sees a bare `503` body ("The shelter's server did not answer in time"), which reads as a dead page. Email-and-password sign-in fails identically. **Since this release, test is on the same Pi** (`test-pi.lannacare.org`, same box, same tunnel), so one machine going down now takes writes out on **production and test at once — including the environment you would use to verify a fix.** The cloudflared config anticipates this (`# A second Pi later: run cloudflared there with this same tunnel's credentials and Cloudflare balances between the two`), but there is one Pi. **Three separable pieces, smallest first.** (a) **Distinguish a connect-time failure from an in-flight one in `worker/origin.mjs`.** It splits on method alone — `const idempotent = IDEMPOTENT_METHODS.has(request.method)` — and every failure path returns `idempotent ? null : writeUnanswered()`, so a write is refused whether the Pi timed out mid-request or the fetch never left the Worker. **Keep the refusal where it is right:** a 502/504 can be cloudflared losing the origin *after* the Pi has acted, and replaying would record a second intake (#250, and that file's own comment). But a connection refused or a DNS failure means the request provably never arrived, so replaying locally cannot duplicate anything. Today's outage was entirely the second kind, and **one condition would have meant no outage at all** — writes would have fallen through to the Worker exactly as reads did. Check what the Workers runtime actually surfaces for a connect failure versus a timeout before relying on the distinction; if `fetch` throws indistinguishably, a short pre-flight (a `HEAD` with a one-second timeout, or the status the `scheduled` handler already collects) is the fallback plan. (b) **Nothing said the Pi was down.** It was found because a person could not sign in. Settings → System status has a Pi tile — behind a login that stops working when the Pi does, so the one place that would say so is unreachable exactly when it matters. The Worker stays up in that state and already runs a cron every fifteen minutes (`wrangler.jsonc`, status alerts), so it is the natural place to notice and send the mail `0098` built. (c) **The weekly backup runs on the Pi**, so a Pi down over a Sunday means no backup that week, silently — a larger exposure than the outage. Either run it somewhere that is not the same single point of failure, or alert when a run is missed. **Also worth deciding, separately:** whether emptying `ORIGIN_HOST` should be a documented emergency lever. Setting it to `""` in an environment's block and deploying makes the Worker serve everything including writes, restoring sign-in within a deploy, at the cost of the CPU limits the Pi was adopted to avoid (`Error 1102`). It worked that way until 2026-09-30 and is currently folklore rather than a runbook step. **Status 2026-10-02: (a) is done** (`claude/origin-write-fallback`; docs/decisions/2026-10-02-origin-write-fallback.md). A thrown fetch turned out not to distinguish the two cases — workerd words a refused connect and a connection dropped after the write arrived identically — so the split is on the edge's status instead: a write is rendered by the Worker on a **530** (no tunnel connector, error 1033) and refused as before on everything else. **Status, `claude/pi-down-alert`: (b) is done** — the alert run now treats a dead Pi as red (the tile stays amber), so the existing two-consecutive-runs mail and recovery mail fire for it, with what to check in the body (docs/decisions/2026-10-02-pi-down-alert.md). **Still open: (c) (the existing backup tile alerts only after two missed Sundays; one constant changes that), the `ORIGIN_HOST` lever, and the drill that proves (a) on the real tunnel** (stop `cloudflared` on the Pi, sign in).

- [ ] **Rethink Pi failover: today a power cut still refuses writes. Make the fallback cover the outages that actually happen — options paper, then build.** Lutan, 2026-10-02: not happy with where the write fallback (`claude/origin-write-fallback`, docs/decisions/2026-10-02-origin-write-fallback.md) leaves us. The 2026-10-02 production release notes set it out: **`cloudflared` stopped → 530 at once → writes fall back and work**; **power cut, first seconds → 502/504 or a 20 s timeout → writes refused**; **power cut, once the edge notices → 530 → fall back**; **Pi up but sick, or `lanna-care` stopped → 502/503 from cloudflared → refused**. So the one case that falls back cleanly is the drill, and a power cut (the likeliest outage for a box in a house) starts with a window of refused sign-ins and saves, and a sick-but-up Pi never falls back at all. "If it's not going to fail over properly, we need a different solution." **Why it is built this way, so the replacement keeps the guarantee:** a 502/504 can arrive *after* the Pi has written, so replaying it on the Worker could record a second intake (#250), and a thrown fetch cannot tell "never arrived" from "arrived, then dropped" (the decision file's probe table). Any new design must still never apply a write twice. **The key fact to build on: the data is not on the Pi.** Both the Pi and the Worker write to the same Supabase database, so the duplicate risk can be closed *at the database* rather than by guessing at the edge, after which retrying on the Worker is always safe. **Options to weigh, not mutually exclusive:** (1) **Idempotent writes.** Every server action carries a client-generated request id, and the database records it under a unique key in the same transaction as the write (one shared wrapper around `runAction()` plus a `write_requests` table, or a parameter on the RPCs that matter: intake, placements, weights, deliveries, stocktake, uploads' attachment rows). A replay then returns the first result instead of writing again, and **every** failure (502, 504, timeout, thrown fetch) can fall back to the Worker. It removes the "refused by design" rows outright, but it touches every write path, so it needs its own schema PR and a sweep like #441's. (2) **Route on health, not on each request's outcome.** The Pi sends a heartbeat every few seconds (or the Worker's cron probes it) into Workers KV or a Durable Object; when it is stale the Worker serves *everything* itself before trying the Pi, so a new request after a power cut never waits for the edge to notice. Covers the "Pi up but sick" row too if the heartbeat comes from the app rather than the OS. It does not cover a request already in flight when the power goes, which is what (1) is for. (3) **Keep the power on.** A UPS HAT or small UPS for the Pi *and* the router/ONT (the tunnel needs both), sized for a typical Suphan Buri outage, with a clean shutdown on low battery. Cheapest and most direct for the power-cut row; does nothing for a crash or a broken deploy. (4) **A second connector on the same tunnel**, off-site: cloudflared already supports replicas ("A second Pi later" in `scripts/pi/cloudflared-config.yml`), and a free-tier cloud VM (e.g. an always-free ARM instance) running the same build would keep the origin up when the house goes dark. Real redundancy at near-zero cost, but a second machine to deploy to, patch and keep the secrets on, and each replica serves a share of traffic, not only the failover. (5) **Flip it: writes always on the Worker, the Pi only for page renders.** The 1102s came from rendering, not from server actions, so writes might fit the Worker's CPU budget. Then a Pi outage costs reads (which already fall back) and never writes. It hinges on a Pi-rendered form posting to a Worker build, which needs the same server-action ids and a shared `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (the open Deployment item), so verify that first. Paid Cloudflare Load Balancing was considered and is out: it costs every month and cannot target a Worker. **Deliverable:** a short paper (`docs/pi-failover.md`) that measures before it argues: on the real tunnel, time how long a hard power-off takes to become a 530 (pull the plug, POST every second), and what a stopped `lanna-care` returns. Then a recommendation of the cheapest combination that makes every row of the table "falls back and works" without ever risking a double write. My expectation is (1) + (2), with (3) as cheap insurance, but let the measurements decide. Agree it with Lutan, record it in `docs/decisions/`, then build (schema PR first if (1)). **Done when** the drill passes for all four rows: stop `cloudflared`, pull the power, stop `lanna-care`, and wedge the app, and in each case a sign-in and a save made during the outage succeed exactly once. Supersedes the `ORIGIN_HOST=""` emergency-lever question in the item above if (2) lands, and pairs with that item's (b), the alert when the Pi goes down. **Status 2026-10-03: the options paper is written and proposed ([#310](https://github.com/lutanbennett/Animal_Shelter_App/pull/310), `docs/pi-failover.md`); it is not agreed and no drill has been run. Remaining: Lutan reads it and answers its two questions; the drill (recipe in the paper) is run on the Pi; option 5 waits only on a CPU measurement (the form-key question is answered: a shared key is required and works on both the Pi’s and the Worker’s build, per `server-actions-encryption-key`, 2026-10-03, and it is live only once #312 is merged and both builds are redeployed); then the agreed recommendation goes in `docs/decisions/` and the build is planned.**

**Role walkthrough, Pass 1 (Vet) — 2026-09-27. Work these first, then re-run the
walkthrough from the top** (Lutan). The twelve items live under their own headings
rather than being copied here; this is the order to take them in and what blocks
what. Record is `docs/uat/2026-09-27.md`; the script is `docs/role-walkthrough.md`.

| Order | Item | Where | Why here |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A refused page and a booked vet visit dump you on the public website | Quick wins | Smallest of the twelve, and it blocks re-testing: it ejects the tester mid-checklist on every refused route. Also cuts load on the CPU path the 1102s are hitting |
| 2 | Doctors belong to a vet — managed list rather than free text | Medical records | **The real gate.** A vet account has no link to a clinic at all today, so items 4 and 8 cannot be built until this exists. Schema PR of its own, ahead of both |
| 3 | Cut the vet's world down to residents and their own tasks | Auth | Independent of the schema, and it is most of what Lutan wants the vet role to feel like. Menu is easy; the five unguarded route trees are the work |
| 4 | A vet should see only the residents their own clinic treats | Auth | Largest item from Pass 1. Needs 2 first, and needs the rule **decided with Lutan before anyone builds it** — a scope that hides a resident from the vet about to treat them is worse than one that shows too much |
| 5 | Recurring jobs: only offer people who can actually do the job | Management | Independent. Stops the Pass 0 situation arising rather than handling it |
| 6 | Prescription should not attach to a future vet visit | Medical records | Small, independent, correctness |
| 7 | One weight per vet visit | Medical records | Small, independent, correctness |
| 8 | Vet visit form: clinic picker and the Status dropdown | Medical records | The clinic half needs 2; the Status question is a decision, not code |
| 9 | A vet's photo upload limited to the Medical folder | Medical records | **Do with the Medical-folder item already at the top of this section** — both are about what Medical means as a destination, and splitting them means touching the same upload path twice |
| 10 | A date field cannot be cleared once set, on mobile | Mobile | Small, and it affects every optional date in the app rather than the one it was found on |
| 11 | A role-based manual | Documentation | Pairs with 3: as the vet's menu shrinks, a manual still explaining Enclosures is actively wrong |
| 12 | File-type icon for non-images; release notes by role | Quick wins, Documentation | Nice to haves. Last |

**Fold the schema into one PR first, or the migration rule will be the bottleneck.**
Four of the twelve want a migration — 2 (a `vet_doctors` table, and the link from a
vet *account* to a clinic that does not exist today), 4 (the RLS policies that scope
residents to a clinic, which need that same link), 7 (a unique index behind one
weight per visit) and 6 (a check against the appointment date). CLAUDE.md allows
**only one in-flight branch carrying a migration at a time**, and says that where two
streams both need schema their migrations go in one PR. Done separately these four
serialise behind each other no matter how many worktrees are open; done as a single
additive schema PR up front, every feature half that follows can run in parallel.
2 and 4 need the same link anyway, so they are not really two decisions.

**Item 4 is gated on a decision, not on throughput.** It is the one thing here that
should not be picked up by a session and built: the rule for what a vet may see
wants settling with Lutan first, because a scope that hides a resident from the vet
about to treat them is worse than the current one that shows too much.
Not on this list and not forgotten: **Cloudflare 1102 on the test site**, expected
to clear when the Pi becomes the origin (see Deployment), now deferred to
Friday 2026-10-02, so expect it to persist until then. It is the environment,
not a defect in the app.

The plumbing (starter text from the dictionaries, admin editor prefill, translation queue) is exactly what the new page needs and only the words change. A new page with a fresh slug was the alternative and was turned down. The schema PR comes first. **Where it links from:** under **Adopt** in the header and footer (it is adoption, not a service), and the `/adopt` listing's line for adopters abroad. **Content:** generic starter text in English and Thai in our own words, same rule as relocation: what an international adoption involves in outline (meeting the animal, the paperwork and health requirements an overseas home usually needs, travel being arranged with the adopter) — **no fees, timelines, countries served, partner names or licence claims**; those come from the shelter. `generateMetadata` with OG tags, a releases line for the new page. **3. The illustration: sketch C, "Over the globe" — with the pyjamas.** Of the three caped-puppy sketches drawn for relocation (A straight-out flight, B carrying a crate — the one used, C over the globe), Lutan chose **C** for International adoptions (confirmed 2026-09-27): the puppy flying up and out over the curve of the planet, a dotted route trailing back to a pin. **But always in its orange pyjamas** — sketch C was drawn in plain fur; use the pyjama body from sketch A / the current `PuppyFlying`. The sketch lived only in the relocation session's scratchpad, so its globe is recorded here: a 240×160 viewBox, a planet `circle cx=120 cy=330 r=190` in soft forest green with two darker land shapes clipped to it (`M20 150 q20 -14 44 -6 q18 6 12 20 h-60z` and `M130 142 q24 -8 40 2 q14 10 34 4 l10 20 h-90z`), a terracotta pin at (44, 147), a dashed terracotta route `M44 146 C40 120 52 96 70 84` (dash `5 3`), a cloud top right, and the puppy at `translate(40 -8) scale(.82)`; in C the cape was forest green with a terracotta collar. Add it to `src/components/PuppyLoader.tsx` beside `PuppyFlying` (e.g. `PuppyOverGlobe`), same approach: plain SVG, colours from `--site-action` / `--site-accent` so it follows the dev recolour, animated in `globals.css` (cape ripple, gentle bob, and the route could draw itself in) with a still pose under `prefers-reduced-motion`, decorative (`aria-hidden`). Show Lutan the pyjama version before wiring it in. Decide what happens to `PuppyFlying` (the crate): keep it if anything else uses it, otherwise delete it with the page. Record the reversal in `docs/decisions.md` (the relocation entry of 2026-09-27 stays as history), and update the Settings → Website tabs item (Admin), which still mentions a Pet relocation page.
- [x] **Keep Medical-folder photos off the public website.** Lutan, 2026-09-27. **Done 2026-09-27:** the gallery and the photo proxy by `0101` (#170); the profile photo by `claude/medical-photos-profile` — a Medical photo can no longer be *chosen* (hub viewer, edit forms, `setResidentProfilePhoto`). A Medical photo that becomes the profile photo on its own (first upload, delete fallback) is the separate follow-up "Public views show no profile photo when it is in Medical" (docs/decisions.md, 2026-09-27). "Move to folder" not built. **Moved to Next up the same day (Lutan):** it is live today — every photo of a public resident shows on `/adopt/[id]`, Medical ones included. Photos filed under **Medical** when uploaded to a resident (operations, bad teeth, wounds) must never appear on the public site. Today they can: `public_resident_photos` (0017, last redefined in 0025) takes every `attachments` row with `owner_type = 'resident'` for a publicly visible resident, whatever its folder, and `/adopt/[id]`'s `PhotoGallery` shows them all. The folder is already recorded: the upload route (`src/app/api/residents/[id]/photos/route.ts`) stores the category from `PHOTO_CATEGORIES` (Shelter / Medical / Foster / Adoption, `src/lib/google/drive-client.ts`) in `attachments.sub_folder`. **Fix in the database, not the page:** a schema PR that redefines `public_resident_photos` with `and coalesce(a.sub_folder, '') <> 'Medical'` (same column list, so the anon grants carry over), because that view also feeds `is_public_drive_file()` (0084) — so the photo proxy stops serving a medical image to a signed-out visitor even if someone has its link, not only the gallery. **Profile photos too:** a medical photo can still be made the resident's profile photo, which appears on `/adopt`, the home page cards, recent adoptions and link previews through the other public views. Either block choosing a Medical photo as the profile photo on the resident hub (with a clear message), or have the public views fall back to no photo when the profile photo is in Medical — decide which and record it in `docs/decisions.md`; blocking is simpler to explain. **Check the data before relying on the folder:** imported (AppSheet) and older photos may have `sub_folder` empty or holding something other than a category, so run a count on dev (and read-only on production from the main checkout, when Lutan asks) of resident photos by `sub_folder`, and list any public ones that look medical so staff can refile them. Consider adding a "Move to folder" action on the resident's photos, so a photo uploaded to the wrong folder can be fixed without deleting it. - [x] **Bug: uploading a Shelter Friend logo fails [token fixed and retested 2026-09-25; code follow-ups done 2026-09-26] with "Google OAuth token refresh failed: invalid_grant: Token has been expired or revoked."** Reported by Lutan 2026-09-25 with a screenshot of the Shelter Friend card on a contact (Save pressed after choosing a logo; the address/map opt-ins visible above). **Environment: UAT, lannacare.org** (confirmed by Lutan). **Urgent: uploads on the site the customer is testing are down.** lannacare.org is served by the `production` block in `wrangler.jsonc`, whose Drive is still the dev Google account (lannaanimalfoundationbwm@gmail.com), so that account's Google Cloud project is where to look. Checked 2026-09-25: the local `.env.local` / `.dev.vars` credentials now fail with `invalid_client` ("The OAuth client was not found"), so they can't stand in for UAT's, and a new token has to be minted. **Cause is not the logo code:** the message comes from `src/lib/google/drive.ts` (token refresh, ~line 105): Google refused `GOOGLE_OAUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN` for that environment. So **every Drive upload on that environment is failing** (resident photos, hero, logos, project photos, backups), not just this one. Different from the dev-only `invalid_client` seen 2026-09-24 (a dead client); `invalid_grant` means the refresh token itself was revoked or expired. Usual reasons: the OAuth consent screen of the Drive client's project is in *Testing* (refresh tokens die after 7 days), the Google account's password was changed or access removed, or a newer refresh token was issued for the same client and account (Google keeps a limited number). **Fix:** (1) find the reason in the Drive client's Google Cloud project (under the account that owns the Drive; see the 90-day-token item too), publish the consent screen if it is in Testing, (2) mint a new refresh token and set it as the Worker secret for the affected environment (and `.dev.vars` / `.env.local` locally), (3) redeploy and retry the logo upload. **Code follow-ups, same PR or next:** show staff a plain message ("Photo storage is not connected — tell an admin") instead of Google's raw text, and make sure a failed logo upload doesn't throw away the rest of the Shelter Friend form; report it on the release/health check (e.g. a Drive token check in `scripts/` or the admin page) so an expired token shows up before a user hits it. **Fixed 2026-09-25 (token):** cause confirmed. The Drive client "Lanna Care Drive Access" (Desktop, created 2026-09-18) lives in the lanna account's project **Lanna Care - Dev** (`lanna-care-dev`), which was still in *Testing*, so its refresh token expired 7 days later, on 2026-09-25. The v0.5.0 deploy was not involved (no secret changes in `wrangler deployments list`). Published the project (logo removed, since a logo forces verification; home page `https://lannacare.org` and privacy `https://lannacare.org/privacy` added; `lannacare.org` added as an authorised domain), minted a new token, and set it in `.env.local` and as `GOOGLE_OAUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN` on the `production` and `test` Workers. Also removed a stray space after `=` on the Google keys in `.env.local`. Lutan retested the Shelter Friend logo upload on lannacare.org 2026-09-25: works. **Code follow-ups done 2026-09-26** (`claude/drive-upload-errors`): a dead token or client now raises `DriveNotConnectedError`, and every upload (logo, Website photos, resident/project/maintenance photos, attachments) and Drive folder sync says "Photo storage is not connected — tell an admin.", with Google's text going to the server log. The form itself was never lost (the logo uploads on its own, apart from Save, and the draft survives), but its message showed below Save, out of sight; it now sits by the logo. Settings (`/admin`) shows whether Drive is connected, and `scripts/check-drive-token.mjs` checks an env file.

It is small enough to fold into whichever schema PR is open. **Validation:** extend `src/lib/links/validate.ts` with Messenger and X host lists and a WhatsApp number check, with errors in en and th. **Where they show:** as icons in the public footer's follow/contact row (inline SVGs like `FacebookIcon.tsx`, shown only when set), on the About & contact page, and in the redesign's mobile-menu "Talk to us" panel next to LINE and Call (Messenger and WhatsApp as chat buttons; X belongs with Facebook and Instagram under "Follow us"). - [x] **Give the public site some life: spring motion on text and content blocks.** Lutan, 2026-09-26: some public websites have a "springy" feel to their text blocks, and the public pages should feel less static. **What it means here:** headings, text blocks and cards ease into place as they scroll into view (a short rise and fade with a slight spring overshoot, staggered within a group), and interactive cards (the "How you can help" cards, resident cards, Shelter Friend logos, buttons) give a small springy lift on hover and press. Subtle, not a show: short distances, around 300 to 600 ms, each block animates once. **How:** prefer plain CSS with the `linear()` easing function (it can express a real spring curve) and an `IntersectionObserver` that adds a class, so there is no animation library and almost no bundle cost. Reach for a library (e.g. `motion`) only if something needs physics CSS cannot fake, and say why in `docs/decisions.md`. **Must:** respect `prefers-reduced-motion` (no movement at all, content simply present), never hide content from anyone without JavaScript or from search engines (start visible and animate only once JS has run, or make the no-JS state the final one), no layout shift, and keep the staff app itself calm (public pages only). **When:** build it on the redesigned components, so after `public-site-shell` (batch 2 of the 2026-09-25 plan). It can ride with or follow `public-site-home`. First, agree the feel with Lutan on one section (e.g. the "How you can help" cards) with two or three variants in a preview before rolling it across the site. Reference sites Lutan likes would help; ask for a link.
- [x] **A puppy-at-a-laptop loading animation for page changes and slow loads.** [done 2026-09-26: Lutan chose sketch A (side-on, nose to the screen) for the public site and the one-colour glyph C for the staff app; `src/components/PuppyLoader.tsx`, root `src/app/loading.tsx`] Lutan, 2026-09-26: the RSPCA site shows a little running dog while a page changes or is waiting to load; the shelter's site should have its own. **Artwork must be our own.** Don't copy RSPCA's animation. Draw an original dog (ideally the shelter's logo dog, or a dog and a cat taking turns) as a looping run cycle. **Format:** an animated SVG (CSS keyframes on a few shapes) or a Lottie file rather than a GIF, so it is tiny, crisp at any size, recolourable to the redesign palette, and can be paused. **Where it shows:** the public pages' `loading.tsx` (Next's route-level loading UI) and client-side route changes, plus any wait over about a second such as a form submit or a photo upload. The staff app gets it too (Lutan, 2026-09-26): a small inline version for uploads and slow pages, not a full-screen overlay. **Behaviour:** appear only after a short delay (about 300 ms) so fast navigations don't flash it, and have an accessible label ("Loading", en and th) with `role="status"`. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` show a still dog with the label instead of the run cycle. **When:** with or after `public-site-shell` (redesign part 1), so it uses the new palette; it pairs naturally with the spring-motion item above. **Reference and concept (Lutan, 2026-09-26):** not a running dog after all, but **a puppy working on a laptop**, drawn from Lutan's own video (`C:UsersLeidosOneDrivePicturesIMG_0004 (2).MOV`, 2.6 s, portrait): a fluffy apricot poodle-cross in orange patterned pyjamas, leaning in and peering at an open laptop, nose close to the screen. Loop idea: the puppy leans in, looks at the screen, taps or paws at the keyboard, head bobs, with a small progress bar or dots on the laptop screen as the "loading" signal. Keep it a simple, flat illustration in the redesign palette, not a traced photo. Start from that, agree the drawing with him (two or three sketches or frames in a preview), then build.

## Security

**Moved above Deployment on 2026-09-30 (Lutan): severity governs here, not
file position.** The review appended this section at the end of the file,
which by this file's own convention ("ordered by suggested priority") read as
the lowest priority in the backlog while it held the only High finding and a
live open redirect. That was an artifact of how it was written, not a
judgement — so the section was moved rather than left to mislead the next
planner.

From the 2026-09-30 security assessment (`docs/security/security-assessment-2026-09-30.docx`, `.pdf`; commit d26103b, release 0.10.0). Finding IDs (DB-n, CODE-n, WEB-n) are the report's. No Critical finding; one High. Items are grouped so each is one stream; schema items are `claude/<name>-schema` PRs first, per the migrations rules. Effort is the report's. None of these makes the app harder for field staff.

- [ ] **Next 16.3.5 carries a Critical RCE advisory; the fix is a patch bump that is "outside the stated dependency range" (Critical).** Surfaced 2026-10-01 by the new `audit` job on PR #265, which is — as far as the run history shows — the first PR to execute it: `next 16.2.0 - 16.3.5`, **critical**, *"Next.js: Remote Code Execution in next/og ImageResponse"*, [GHSA-vcvr-r3jv-pc5j](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-vcvr-r3jv-pc5j), `fix available via npm audit fix --force`, `Will install next@16.3.8, which is outside the stated dependency range`. `package.json` pins `"next": "16.3.5"`, so **`main`, test and production are all affected equally** — this is not a property of the PR that found it. **Worth putting beside the 2026-09-30 assessment, which recorded "No Critical findings":** that was true of the code, and the first Critical to appear came from the supply chain instead, caught by a job the same security batch added. The chain is what the review could not see by reading the repo. **The mitigating fact, and it is a real one:** `next/og` and `ImageResponse` appear **nowhere** in `src/` or `worker/` (grepped 2026-10-01), so the vulnerable entry point is not reachable in this app — the dependency is vulnerable, the code path is unused. That is the difference between "patch on the next convenient day" and "stop and patch now", and it is why this is a backlog item rather than a hotfix. Do not treat it as safe indefinitely, though: a future feature that wants an Open Graph image would reach for exactly that API, and the person adding it will not know this. **Why it is not a one-line bump:** `AGENTS.md` opens by saying this is a modified Next whose APIs, conventions and file structure may differ from training data, and npm itself reports `16.3.8` as outside the declared range. So it needs its own PR: read `node_modules/next/dist/docs/` and the upstream notes for 16.3.6–16.3.8, bump, run the full gates, and **check the two things this project has already been bitten by around builds** — that `strip-baked-env` still finds and empties `next-env.mjs` (its shape is an OpenNext implementation detail), and that `getSiteOrigin()`'s header reading still behaves on the Pi behind cloudflared, since a sign-in outage on 30 September came from exactly that area. **Also fix the reporting gap this exposed:** `audit` is `if: github.event_name == 'pull_request'`, so a push to `main` skips it and nothing reports a new advisory between PRs — an advisory published on a quiet day is invisible until someone happens to open a PR. Its `continue-on-error: true` is correct and should stay (the job's own comment argues the case well, and contrasts it with `test-plan`), but "reported, not enforced" only works if it is actually reported: consider running it on a schedule as well, so a Critical lands somewhere a person sees without a PR to carry it. **Status 2026-10-02:** the bump landed (#268, Next 16.3.8) and the reporting gap is closed by `.github/workflows/advisories.yml` (daily, fails on purpose; docs/decisions/2026-10-02-scheduled-audit-fails-on-purpose.md). **Still open, so not ticked:** `getSiteOrigin()`/sign-in on the Pi behind cloudflared has not been confirmed on 16.3.8 (#268 verified sign-in on a local production build only, and was not deployed).

- [x] **Encrypt the weekly backup and keep session tables out of it (DB-1, High).** **Done 2026-10-02** (rollout in `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-backup-encryption-rollout.md`, code in #243): key pair made by Lutan, private key in two places only; first encrypted production run on the Pi (`.dump.age`, link not logged); a decrypt rehearsal gave a valid archive with the session and MFA tables absent; old plaintext dumps deleted on the Pi and in Drive, `backup.log` truncated; `Backups/` moved to `Admin/Backups`, shared with Lutan only; every production session and refresh token rotated. Left over, split out as its own item below: clearing the MFA factors. The restore itself belongs to DB-2 and the Deployment restore item. `scripts/backup.mjs:44` dumps `public` and `auth` with `pg_dump` and uploads the file to the shelter's Drive as-is; `auth` holds password hashes, TOTP secrets in plain text and live refresh tokens, and line 104 logs the shareable link to `backup.log`. Encrypt before upload with a key kept only by the administrator (age, gpg or `openssl enc -aes-256-cbc -pbkdf2`; key in a password manager, never the repo or `.env`), add `--exclude-table=auth.refresh_tokens --exclude-table=auth.sessions --exclude-table='auth.mfa_*'`, put `Backups/` in a folder shared with the admin only rather than the application root, and stop logging the web link. Then rehearse a restore. Half a day. **Do first.** **Update 2026-09-30:** the weekly backup now runs on the Raspberry Pi as well as the laptop (`--local-copy`, cron Sundays 03:00), so **the Pi's SSD also holds plaintext dumps**, and its `~/backups/backup.log` collects the Drive link every week. Encrypt with `age` public-key encryption so the decrypting key never sits on the Pi, and encrypt before both the upload and the local copy. **Status 2026-09-30:** code done in PR #243 (age public key, exclusions, link no longer logged, tile reads `.dump.age`); **left open until Lutan** generates the key pair, installs the public key on the Pi and laptop, does the first run and the restore rehearsal, deletes the old plaintext dumps and the Pi's `backup.log`, re-shares `Backups/` and rotates (test plan manual table).
- [ ] **Clear the production MFA factors left over from DB-1, and re-enrol.** From the DB-1 rollout (2026-10-02): Lutan's authenticator (TOTP) secret was in the plaintext dumps that were exposed before encryption, and the factors were not cleared when sessions were rotated. Lutan is the only person with one. In the production SQL editor: `delete from auth.mfa_factors;`, then sign in, open Settings → Security and enrol the authenticator app again. A few minutes, Lutan's to run.
- [x] **Backups from an always-on machine, and a rehearsed restore (DB-2).** **Always-on half done 2026-09-30, closed 2026-10-04:** the weekly backup runs from cron on the Pi (Sundays 03:00, `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-one-pi-hosting-and-backup.md`), and a red Weekly backup tile already emails admins (`src/lib/status/alerts.ts`). **The restore rehearsal was not done** and is carried by "Rehearse a restore from a production backup" (Deployment) rather than kept open in two places; retiring the laptop schedule is the "Confirm the Pi's first scheduled backup" item. The scheduled task runs only while the developer's Windows account is logged on (`scripts/backup-schedule.ps1:43-46`); `backup.log` shows two manual runs (21 and 26 Sep), and the restore procedure has never been tested. Move the schedule to a GitHub Actions workflow or the Pi in `docs/pi-hosting.md`, restore once into a scratch Supabase project and write the steps down, repeat quarterly, and have the "Weekly backup" status tile alert admins when it goes red. A day, including the rehearsal. Do together with the item above.
- [x] **HTTP security headers and Secure cookies (WEB-2).** *Done in the Worker; the CSP is report-only, tightening owed (see docs/decisions/2026-09-30-security-headers-in-the-worker.md).* Nothing sets CSP, X-Frame-Options, HSTS, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy or nosniff, and the Supabase cookie is created without `secure`. Add `headers()` in `next.config.ts` (or the Worker): `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `Permissions-Policy: camera=(self), geolocation=()`, HSTS if the Cloudflare zone does not already send it; CSP in report-only first (`default-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; frame-src https://www.google.com`), tighten after a quiet week; `cookieOptions: { secure: true }` in production on both Supabase client factories. Camera must stay allowed for photos and QR scanning. Also covers CODE-7's proxy half (`nosniff` and `Content-Disposition` on `src/app/api/photos/[fileId]/route.ts`).
- [ ] **Tighten the CSP (WEB-2 follow-up).** *Collection is done (2026-10-01, `claude/csp-tighten-and-collect`): the Worker answers `POST /api/csp-report` and logs a bounded one-line summary per violation, readable in Workers Logs (search `csp-report`); `docs/decisions/2026-10-01-csp-report-collection.md`. Enforcement is deliberately not on, because no reports existed before that change.* **Gate: after the collector has been deployed to production for a week**, read every `csp-report` line and explain or fix each distinct (directive, blocked) pair; if the week is thin or has unexplained hits, wait longer. Then: switch `content-security-policy-report-only` to `content-security-policy` in `worker/security-headers.mjs` (keep `frame-src https://www.google.com`, `img-src 'self' data: blob:` and `camera=(self)`); drop `'unsafe-inline'` from `script-src` with nonces only if they can reach every inline script on **both** render paths (the Pi's `next start` and the Worker's OpenNext render), otherwise leave it and say why; consider raising HSTS above six months once stable. A blocking CSP breaks public pages for visitors, so check `/`, `/adopt`, `/our-work`, a Shelter Friend page and the camera on a phone before merging.
- [x] **Check the row count before deleting Drive files (CODE-1, CODE-2).** Under RLS a refused delete affects zero rows without an error, and the code then deletes the Drive file anyway: `blood-tests/actions.ts:32-42`, `procedures/actions.ts:32-42`, `maintenance/actions.ts:361-369`, `projects/actions.ts:322-329`. A vet can select more attachments than `vet_delete_attachments` (0110) lets it delete, so an outside clinic can destroy another clinic's document while the row survives. Use `.delete().eq(...).select('id')` and abort unless exactly one row came back; better, a security-definer RPC like `delete_resident_photo`, and move Drive files to trash rather than deleting permanently (`drive.ts:408-412`). Separately `deleteMaintenanceJob` (`maintenance/actions.ts:308-323`) deletes the job's attachment rows before it learns the caller may not delete the job, so any volunteer can strip a job's photo records: delete the job first and let a foreign-key cascade take the attachments, or check the role up front as `deceased/actions.ts:92-95` does. A morning.

(DB-4) A `ChangeEnclosure` placement (volunteers can create one) can target the Deceased or Adopted pseudo-enclosure, which skips the deceased workflow, takes no cascade snapshot, engages every lock and defeats `undo_deceased_placement()`; add a before-insert trigger refusing a Lifecycle-zone target unless the placement type matches. One migration, no UI change.

Fixed-column views rather than base-table grants, per 0122's pattern. Move the PAT and DB password into Windows Credential Manager or a password-manager CLI and inject at run time (the scripts already read `process.env`), use a scoped token for backups and deploys, rotate the DB password and service-role key afterwards, confirm BitLocker is on, and have worktrees copy only the dev keys they need. Half a day plus rotation.
- [x] **Small code hardening batch (CODE-4, CODE-5, CODE-6, CODE-7, WEB-5, WEB-6, WEB-8, WEB-9).** One PR of trivial fixes: (CODE-4) `csvField` in `src/lib/csv.ts` must prefix values starting `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or CR with `'` unless they parse as a number; (CODE-5) about a dozen actions return Postgres `error.message` verbatim (`blood-tests/new`, `diets`, `immunizations/new`, `procedures/new`, `vet-visits/new`, `residents/new`) — map unique, check and RLS refusals to the translated sentences (`prescriptions/actions.ts:36-41`, `src/lib/action-result.ts:28-36`) and send the rest through `unexpectedFailure`, which also fits the #441 sweep; (CODE-6) escape backslashes before quotes in the three Drive `q=` names (`drive.ts:473,890,953`); (CODE-7) require `%PDF-` as the first non-whitespace bytes in `file-signature.ts:74`; (WEB-5) minimum password 10 to 12 (`password-change.ts:47`, now 8), no complexity rules, and turn on leaked-password protection in the Supabase dashboard; (WEB-6) run `contact_map_url` (`admin/website/actions.ts:144`) through `checkHttpsUrl` and add the same DB check constraint the social links have; (WEB-8) narrow the `src/proxy.ts:19` matcher to `/_next/static`, `/_next/image`, `/favicon.ico` and use segment-boundary matching in `isPublicPath` as `isPublicPage` does; (WEB-9) set `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` per environment so `x-forwarded-host` is never consulted (`src/lib/site-origin.ts`).
- [x] **Defence in depth and hygiene (WEB-7, CODE-9, CODE-10).** **Split 2026-10-01:** DB-11 is now its own item below (it needs a retention decision from Lutan before anything is built), and CODE-8 is a Cloudflare dashboard rule with no code — it belongs to Lutan and is listed with the other console work in the assessment appendix. What is left is three code and CI fixes that stand alone as one stream. (WEB-7) One shared helper refusing `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` or a mismatched `Origin` on the five multipart upload routes (`src/app/api/residents/[id]/photos/route.ts` and siblings). (CODE-9) Bump wrangler (fixes the undici chain), add `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` to CI as a non-blocking job, pin GitHub Actions to SHAs. (CODE-10) A `getUser()` check in `src/app/manual/pdf/route.ts:37-42`, and optionally a shared layout guard so new pages inherit it.
- [ ] **Retention period for what people typed at the assistant (DB-11) — decision first.** Split out of "Defence in depth and hygiene" on 2026-10-01 because it needs a decision and a migration, and bundling it meant a stream would either stall on the decision or invent a policy. `assistant_actions.request_text` (`0070`) keeps the **raw text a user typed** at the assistant, indefinitely. The assessment suggests stating a retention period (it guessed 90 days) and adding a scheduled delete, **or** storing only the parsed intent and never keeping the raw text at all. **The period is Lutan's to set and he has deferred it (2026-10-01): do not build against a guessed number.** The trade-off to put to him: keeping the raw text is what lets anyone see *why* the assistant misread a request, which is the main way the keyword assistant gets better; dropping it removes the retention question entirely but loses that. Once a period is set this is a small schema PR (a scheduled delete) plus a line in the privacy page if the text is user-supplied content being kept for a stated time.
- [x] **Confirm dialogs, offline notice and admin button size (WEB-10).** About 25 files use `window.confirm`, including archive, delete and two-step reset on Settings → Security (`UsersTable.tsx:114-145,222`); `ConfirmDialog` (cancel focused, consequences listed) is used in six. Migrate the Security page and every record-deletion confirm to it, naming the thing deleted; give admin table buttons a 44 px minimum height; add a "You're offline, changes will not save" banner from `navigator.onLine` plus a failed-fetch fallback. Improves field use as well as safety.
- [ ] **Session inactivity timeout (WEB-11, Info).** Sessions never expire by design (400-day cookie, no time-box). Keep that, but set a 60-day inactivity timeout in Supabase so a lost or sold phone eventually signs itself out; a phone used weekly never notices. Dashboard setting on dev and production; record it in `docs/decisions/`. *2026-10-01 (claude/session-inactivity-timeout): the expired-session path is handled — the proxy now sends a timed-out session to /login with a "you were signed out" message, keeps `?next=`, and clears the dead cookies — and the record is `docs/decisions/2026-10-01-session-inactivity-timeout.md`. **Still open, and Lutan's:** turn on Authentication → Sessions → Inactivity timeout = 60 days on dev, then on production (also the Supabase item in the assessment checklist — do it once).*
- [ ] **Supabase, Cloudflare and Google checklist (assessment appendix).** Things the code review could not see: sign-up setting, leaked-password protection, auth and MFA rate limits, redirect allow-list, session timeouts, TOTP enabled on the production project; Cloudflare HSTS, WAF and bot rules; Google OAuth consent and Drive folder sharing; the Pi origin and laptop disk encryption. Walk the appendix on both projects and tick what is set. Not code.
- [ ] **The database lets a volunteer read prices and delivery costs, and a vet read the other clinics.** Found 2026-10-03 by the roles-and-permissions audit (`docs/roles-and-permissions.md` §3, C9 and C10; read from `pg_policies` on dev). The pages refuse both, so nothing on screen shows it; a request sent by hand with the person's own login would be answered. **Volunteer:** the `volunteer_read_*` policies on `medication`, `diet_types`, `stock_receipts` and `stock_counts` return every row and every column, so `cost_per_unit`, a diet's cost and each delivery's `cost` are readable; Stocktake needs the names and the counts only. **Vet:** `vet_read_*` on `vets`, `enclosures`, `zones`, `diet_types`, `shelter_friends` and `bulk_appointments` are by role name with no clinic limit, so a clinic's login can list the other clinics the shelter uses and their bulk bookings; a resident's page needs the enclosure's name, and nothing a vet opens needs the clinic list. **Fix:** fixed-column views gated on role, as `0126` did for contacts (`vet_contacts`, `volunteer_contacts`), or narrower policies; schema PR, then a `scripts/check-contact-visibility.mjs`-style proof with each role's own JWT. **Check where the roles work stands before doing this separately:** if the paper's matrix is built, this is closed by converting the stock and clinic areas (its stage 4), and if volunteers become read-only the volunteer half goes with their stocktake right. **Same audit, lower priority, for the same PR or the conversion:** `resident_list_view` carries insert, update, delete and truncate grants to `authenticated` (C12; untested whether they can do anything); a vet may hard-delete their own clinic's visits, prescriptions, procedures, blood tests and immunizations although Remove is never offered to a vet (C4); `immunization_types` is fully writable by a vet (C3); `residents` has a delete policy for staff and management that no screen uses (C2).
- [ ] **A vet's "Medical folder only" for photos is enforced by the app, not the database.** Found 2026-10-03 while building the permission tables (`docs/decisions/2026-10-03-permission-tables.md`, scope table). `PHOTO_CATEGORIES` in `src/app/residents/[id]/photos/actions.ts` limits a vet to the Medical folder, but `record_attachment()` (`0097`) checks only the role and never the `p_sub_folder`, so a vet's own login used by hand could file a photo of a resident under any other folder, and `0101` publishes every non-Medical resident photo on the Adopt page. Same family as the C rows of the roles paper: reachable only by a hand-made request, not from any button. **Fix:** make `record_attachment()` and the move function refuse a non-Medical folder for a role whose `roles.scope_photos` is `medical_only` (or, until the conversion, for `vet`), with a harness that proves it under a vet's JWT. Best folded into the Vet and Staff conversion of the roles build rather than a PR of its own. Not urgent: vets are on hold and no vet login files photos outside Medical today.
- [ ] **Three Supabase console settings from the security assessment, in one sitting.** All three are in the same console and all three are still open. (a) **Turn off open sign-up, or restrict Google sign-in to the shelter's domain** (WEB-3) — on **dev and production**; written in `signup-bounds-and-mfa` 2026-10-01 and again in `signup-bounds-two-step` 2026-10-02, and done neither time. (b) **Turn on leaked-password protection** — dev and production (`code-hardening-batch`, 2026-10-01). (c) **Authentication → Sessions → inactivity timeout = 60 days** (`session-inactivity-timeout`, 2026-10-01, WEB-11). Lutan's console, not a session's: nothing in the repo can set or read these. The one way to tell they are done is the console itself, so tick this item with the date rather than leaving it to be inferred.
- [ ] **Backup key custody is not finished, though the feature shipped.** `0.14.0` encrypts the backups, but the private half still needs doing by hand: **generate the key pair on Lutan's own machine** (`age-keygen`), save the whole file in the password manager, delete the file — not on the Pi, not in chat (`backup-encryption`, 2026-09-30). Until this is done the encryption protects the backups from everyone including the shelter. The README restore recipe has never been exercised either, which is the natural second half of this item.

(b) **`GOOGLE_SIGNIN_CLIENT_SECRET` needs rotating in the Google Cloud console** — the QA session exposed its value in a transcript on 2026-10-01 and reported it. That one lived only in chat until now, which is exactly how it would have been lost. Both are Lutan's; neither is something a session should do.

(b) **Who opens 2-step set-up for a real admin who has no app yet** — `node scripts/bootstrap-admin.mjs --env production --allow-2step-setup <email>`, or another admin's **Allow set-up**. Written as "before the production deploy" in `signup-bounds-and-mfa` on 2026-10-01; four production releases have gone out since, so the deadline passed unnoticed. Admins enrolled before that date are grandfathered and unaffected.

## Deployment (before go-live)

- [x] **The Pi and the Worker are separate builds with no shared `NEXT_SERVER_ACTIONS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, so a form rendered by one and submitted to the other probably fails once.** Noticed 2026-10-02 while building the write fallback (`claude/origin-write-fallback`, docs/decisions/2026-10-02-origin-write-fallback.md): since that change a write falls through to the Worker when the tunnel answers 530, so a page the Pi rendered just before it dropped is submitted to the Worker's build — and the reverse when the Pi comes back. Next's self-hosting guide (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/self-hosting.md`, "Server Functions encryption key") says instances without a shared key answer "Failed to find Server Action". **Reasoned from that guide, not reproduced** — so step one is to see it: build twice locally, render a form with one build, post it to the other. If it is real, the fix is one secret set identically in `deploy.mjs`'s build and `scripts/pi/deploy-pi.sh`'s. The cost of leaving it: a reload fixes it for the user, and anyone who loads `/login` after the Pi is down is unaffected, which is why this is not part of that PR.
- [x] **Serve `test.lannacare.org` from the Pi too — it is the only environment still on the CPU-limited path, and it has now killed two role walkthroughs.** **Done 2026-10-02** with `0.14.0` (#293 set `ORIGIN_HOST: "test-pi.lannacare.org"`; the box side was completed the same day). Test now answers `x-lanna-served-by: pi` for **GET and POST**, and `/api/version` reports `0.14.0` with its sha, so the 1102s are gone and a walkthrough can be scheduled again. **The work was half-built for about an hour and test writes returned 503 in between** — the Worker was deployed with `ORIGIN_HOST` set while `test-pi.lannacare.org` had no DNS record and the Pi had no test service; reads fell back to the Worker, writes did not. The pieces, in the order `docs/pi-hosting.md` sets out: the test clone at `~/Animal_Shelter_App_test` and `cloudflared tunnel route dns … test-pi.lannacare.org` (Claude), `setup-test.sh` and the WAF rule (Lutan). **One real exposure was found and closed on the way:** with DNS live but the WAF rule still naming only `pi.lannacare.org`, `https://test-pi.lannacare.org/` answered **200 to the public internet** — the origin directly reachable, bypassing the Worker and its `x-origin-key` check. Production was checked at the same time and was correctly `403`. Extending the existing rule to `http.host in {"pi.lannacare.org" "test-pi.lannacare.org"}` closed it, verified four ways including that both Workers still reach their origins. **Lesson recorded in `docs/releases/2026-10-02.md`:** after any deploy where `ORIGIN_HOST` is set, check a POST, not a page — a GET and a version endpoint both looked healthy throughout. **Note the consequence for resilience**, now the first item under Next up: one Pi serves production and test, so a single box going down takes writes out on both at once.

- [ ] **The spare-clone timer was never installed on the Pi, so the USB-SSD spare does not exist.** Found 2026-10-02 while checking something else: `systemctl is-enabled spare-clone.timer` returns **`not-found`**, `is-active` is `inactive`, and `journalctl -u spare-clone.service` has **no entries at all**. #297 (`claude/pi-usb-ssd-spare`) merged the mechanism — `scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh`, `spare-clone.service`, `spare-clone.timer` — but the one-time `sudo scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh init /dev/disk/by-id/usb-…` that partitions the spare, takes the first clone and installs the timer has not been run. So the feature is in the repository and absent from the machine, which is the worst of both: it reads as done in the backlog and in `docs/pi-hosting.md`, and there is no spare. **Why it matters more since 2026-10-02:** the Pi now serves production *and* test, so it is the single point of failure for every write on both (see that item), and a bootable spare is the cheapest part of the answer — the box went off the network for about an hour today and the recovery was "Lutan restarted it", with no fallback had the SD or the USB SSD been the fault rather than the power. **Two things to do, in order:** run the `init` on the Pi against the spare's `by-id` path (not `/dev/sdX`, which moves between boots), then **rehearse the swap** — boot from the spare and confirm the app comes up, because an untested spare is a guess. **And fix the exec bit first** (see that item): `spare-clone.sh` is committed `100644` while `spare-clone.service` has `ExecStart=…/spare-clone.sh sync`, so systemd would refuse to run it even once the timer is installed. Installing the timer without that fix produces a timer that fires and fails silently, which is worse than no timer.



- [ ] **Let Claude run production deploys with fewer hand-offs: SSH from the laptop to the Pi, and a permission decision for the Worker — a guided session.** Lutan, 2026-10-02; facts from the release manager session the same day, which has run every release from 0.9.0 to 0.13.0. **Where each step of a production deploy stands:** (a) **Migrations** (`node scripts/apply-migrations.mjs --env production`) already run from a Claude session with no hand-off, using `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` from the gitignored `.env.deploy.production`. Leave them alone. (b) **The Worker** (`node scripts/deploy.mjs --env production >> deploy.log 2>&1`) is **not** blocked by credentials: wrangler is logged in through `npx wrangler login` (its own stored auth, `~/.wrangler/config/default.toml`), and the command would work. It is refused by Claude Code's auto-mode safety check (reason "Production Deploy"), consistently. **A token changes nothing here**; if Lutan wants Claude to run it, that is a Claude Code permission rule (an allow rule for that exact command in `.claude/settings.json`, via the `update-config` skill), and it is Lutan's decision, made in his own session, never at another session's request. Until then Lutan runs that one command himself. (c) **The Pi**, which is what users are served from (the Worker is the fallback, and both must be deployed or `/releases` shows the old version while the release mail announces the new one): `./scripts/pi/deploy-pi.sh` must **run** on the Pi but can be **started** from the laptop over SSH, which is not set up. Tested 2026-10-02: `lanna-pi` / `lanna-pi.local` answer and are in `known_hosts`, but authentication fails because **there is no private key on this laptop** (`~/.ssh` holds only `known_hosts`) and no ssh-agent running. `pi.lannacare.org` cannot be used for SSH: it is the cloudflared HTTP ingress, so SSH has to go to the LAN name. **This is the part the session fixes:** Lutan runs `ssh-keygen -t ed25519` and then `ssh-copy-id lutan@lanna-pi.local` (it asks for his Pi password, so it is his to run), add a `Host pi` entry in `~/.ssh/config`, and decide whether to restrict that key on the Pi to the deploy script only (`command=` in `authorized_keys`). Then a laptop-side wrapper, e.g. `node scripts/deploy-pi-remote.mjs --ref <sha>`, runs the deploy over SSH and appends its output to `deploy.log`, so a Pi deploy is one command from any session. **A Cloudflare API token is optional, not the fix:** nothing in the repo uses `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` today, and swapping the OAuth login for a scoped, long-lived token kept in a file is a different risk, not simply an upgrade; ask Lutan whether he wants it (its only gain is not having to repeat `wrangler login` when that lapses). **Things to write down while there:** on this laptop PowerShell's execution policy blocks plain `npm`, so every command must be `node scripts/…` or `npm.cmd`; keep the `>> deploy.log 2>&1` redirect on deploy commands (release records depend on it) and warn that the terminal looks empty while it runs; and the 90-day token set on 2026-09-22 is still unlocated, so if auth fails around 2026-12-21 check that first. Record each credential's name, location, scope and expiry (never its value) in a `docs/decisions/` file and the README deploy section. **Done when:** from a fresh Claude session, a Pi deploy of a named SHA runs over SSH without a password prompt and lands in `deploy.log`, and Lutan has decided, in writing, whether Claude may run the Worker deploy.
- [x] **Write the missing release record for `0.8.0`.** Done 2026-09-28 by the release manager, commit `84a8a31`: `docs/releases/2026-09-27.md` gains a `0.8.0` section, appended to the deploy date beside `0.7.0` as a day log. **Less was lost than feared** — the `>>` redirect added after `0.6.1` meant the whole sequence survived in `deploy.log`, so the drift result, the three applies, `strip-baked-env`, the Worker version and the mail counts are quoted rather than inferred. It also settled what the cut plan could not: drift reported 104 files / 101 applied, so `0096`-`0101` were already on production, every dependency was met, and `0102`-`0104` applied cleanly first. **What is genuinely unrecoverable:** nobody ran `check-public-views.mjs` or `check-app-access-gate.mjs` against production, and this was the release that redefined two public views; the record lists them as not run rather than ticking them because the deploy succeeded. **How it got past the release step:** there is no release step to get past. `deploy.mjs` guards the cut and CI guards test plans on PRs; neither knows about the record, because the record is written *after* the deploy *from* its output. It has no trigger of its own, so it happens only when whoever deploys hands the output to whoever writes records - which held for `0.5.0`, `0.6.1` and `0.7.0` because the session was in the conversation at the time, and did not here. It is the only release artifact with no automated trigger and the only one that cannot be reconstructed later. Two options are in the record, neither built: a nudge at the end of `deploy.mjs` naming the record now owed (the better trade - it fires while the output is still on screen), or a script comparing `latestRelease.version` against `docs/releases/`. Passed to Lutan by the `release-notes-tidy` stream; his call. **Not covered by this tick:** the release that is due (`unreleased` holds five lines) is still outstanding. Found 2026-09-28 by the `release-notes-tidy` stream: `/api/releases/current` reports `0.8.0` on both `test.lannacare.org` and `lannacare.org`, but `docs/releases/` stops at `2026-09-27.md`, which covers `0.7.0`. So 0.8.0 went out without the per-release pass from `docs/release-smoke-test.md`, and without the pre-deploy check that every PR in it had a completed checklist. The stream was dropped: the plan had been to strip the Pet relocation line from 0.8.0 on the grounds that it was never deployed. Since it was, the entry stays as history, and the `unreleased` line saying the page was replaced is correct (Lutan, 2026-09-28). The release manager should write `docs/releases/<deploy date>.md` for 0.8.0 from whatever the deploy output and the release mail still show, say plainly which checks can no longer be done after the fact, and find out how a production deploy got past the release step. Also: the 0.8.0 title says "two new website pages", and one of them has since been replaced. Leave it as history too, unless Lutan says otherwise. A release is also due: `unreleased` holds five lines.

Drive needs no move. Item closes when the sign-in client has moved.
Environments exist since 2026-09-21 (README "Environments"): `test.lannacare.org` runs `main` against the dev database; `lannacare.org` runs the production Supabase project (`dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu`, all 63 migrations applied, public views checked, Google provider and redirect URLs set, Lutan seeded as admin). Google Drive is the dev account in every environment for now. What still separates this from a live shelter system:

The customer bought `lannacareforanimals.org` on 2026-09-23 through **sav.com** — it matches the shelter's own Google account (`lannacareforanimals@gmail.com`), so it is also the right home for the shelter's email later. Not urgent, we are not ready to cut over, but it gates go-live, and the Pi origin item below is worth doing *after* it so the tunnel hostnames are created once, on the right zone. Order of work:
  1. **Host the zone on Cloudflare.** It was not registered there, so: add the site in the Cloudflare dashboard on the same account that holds `lannacare.org`, take the two assigned nameservers, set them in sav.com's domain manager, and wait for the zone to go Active (minutes to a couple of days, depending on the old TTLs). Nothing else can start until it is. Recreate the DNS that matters — the `google-site-verification` TXT record if Search Console is re-verified for the new domain (it is needed for the consent screen's brand verification), and Email Routing if the shelter wants `…@lannacareforanimals.org`.
  2. **Stand up a new Supabase project as production** and demote the existing one. Agreed 2026-09-23 and the right way round: the current production project (`dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu`) already holds the real AppSheet migration — realistic volumes and real edge cases, which is exactly what UAT sign-off needs — while the live database should start clean and be loaded at cutover from a fresh export, with the 2026-09-22 import as the rehearsal. New project → `node scripts/apply-migrations.mjs --env production` applies every migration from zero (no baseline needed; dev was baselined at 0027 only because it predated the runner) → Google provider, redirect URLs and SMTP → seed Lutan as admin → re-run `import-appsheet.mjs` against it at cutover. Then `.env.deploy.production` takes the new project's values and the old ones move to `.env.deploy.uat`.
  3. **Decide where the third project lives** — the open cost question. The free tier allows two projects per organisation and this makes three (dev, UAT, production). Recommended: put **production alone in its own organisation on the Pro plan**, which also buys the live system daily backups and PITR — worth having on its own merits, since `scripts/backup.mjs` exists precisely because the free tier has none, and it makes a UAT mistake unable to touch production's quota or settings. The free alternative is a second organisation for production; free projects pause after a week idle, which the live site never would be, but it leaves prod with no managed backups.
  4. **Point the domains.** Move the `lannacare.org` / `www.lannacare.org` custom-domain routes in `wrangler.jsonc`'s `production` environment to `lannacareforanimals.org` and `www.`, give the new `uat` environment the `lannacare.org` route, deploy both, and set the UAT project ref in `src/lib/app-env.ts` in the same change. No application code changes for the hostname itself: `src/lib/site-origin.ts` derives the origin from the request, so copied tag and QR links (`src/lib/tags/origin.ts`, `CopyTagLink`) follow the domain on their own — but the tags and cards already printed carry `lannacare.org`, so either reprint them at the end of the parallel run or leave `lannacare.org` redirecting the tag paths to production.
  5. **Follow the domain everywhere it is registered outside the repo:** Supabase Auth Site URL and redirect URLs on *both* projects; the Google sign-in client's authorised origins and redirect URIs in the `LCA App` Cloud project (endless-bonus-458210-d4), plus the consent screen's homepage, privacy-policy and authorised domains — the app is **published**, so expect a re-review, and both domains need to be listed while UAT still signs in with Google; the Cloudflare WAF rule and `ORIGIN_HOST` for the Pi tunnel; and the tunnel hostnames in `scripts/pi/cloudflared-config.yml` (`pi.lannacare.org`, `test-pi.lannacare.org` and both `httpHostHeader` lines), `scripts/pi/setup.sh` and `scripts/pi/deploy-pi.sh`.
  6. **Split the Drive trees at the same time.** Production moves to a copy of the folder tree in the shelter's own Google account and UAT keeps the existing one on `lannaanimalfoundationBWM` — see the Drive item below, which the cutover should carry out rather than leave for later. Until that is done, UAT and production would share every photo and archive PDF by file ID, and a tester deleting one would delete the customer's copy.
  7. **Treat UAT as holding real personal data**, because it will: adopter and carer names and contact details, not just animals. Same RLS, same role gating, same care with who gets a login as production — a lower environment is not a reason to be looser. Decide too how UAT stays realistic over time: restoring a recent production backup into it before each sign-off round is the obvious answer, and it doubles as the restore rehearsal the item below asks for.
  8. **Then the docs:** README "Environments" and "Deploying to Cloudflare", `docs/pi-hosting.md`, `docs/data-migration.md`, the usage comments at the top of `scripts/deploy.mjs`, and a `docs/decisions.md` entry for why the old production database became UAT rather than the other way round.
- [ ] **Send all auth mail from one sender domain — Resend's free tier holds one, and seven shelters do not fit.** From the seven-shelter discussion, Lutan 2026-09-28, and it lands at **shelter #2, not shelter #7**. Resend was chosen on 2026-09-23 on the explicit constraint that it "must cost nothing", and the free tier holds **one verified domain**. The cutover item above already notes the near version of this: production on `lannacareforanimals.org` needs a second free account or the paid plan. Seven shelters on seven domains makes that seven sender domains for Supabase Auth's password-reset and invite mail. **The free fix is to stop tying the sender to the shelter's domain**: send every shelter's auth mail from one shared sender — `noreply@lannacareforanimals.org` or similar — whatever domain the shelter's own site is on. One Resend domain, seven shelters, no cost, and it is arguably better anyway because there is one DNS setup to keep working rather than seven. The alternative, a free Resend account per shelter, leans on the free tier in a way not worth building on and multiplies the ways mail can quietly stop. **Decide it at the cutover**, because that is when the production sender domain gets configured and `docs/email-sending.md` §2 gets rewritten — doing it then costs nothing, doing it after shelter #2 means re-verifying DNS. Check what the mail actually says while there: a reset mail from `lannacareforanimals.org` arriving for a user of a different shelter's site needs wording that does not look like phishing. Also confirm whether Supabase Auth allows a per-project sender only (it does) and therefore whether a pooled database forces one sender regardless — if so this is not a choice, it is a consequence, and the wording work is the whole item.

Once, into a scratch Supabase project — the org's free tier allows two, so this waits for a moment when the dev project can be recreated from a restore, or for a third project elsewhere: `pg_restore` the `public` schema, then `auth` data-only, then check `apply-migrations.mjs --status` and sign in. Correct the README recipe with whatever it took.
- [ ] **A USB SSD on the Pi as a bootable spare and a second backup copy.** **Status 2026-10-02 (PR #297 merged): script, timer and runbook are in (`scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh`, `docs/pi-hosting.md` "Spare boot drive"); NOT done until a clone has been booted from. The first drive tried was a counterfeit (2 TB label, 40-50 kB/s, USB 2, `048d:1234`) and was rejected. Needs a branded 500 GB-1 TB SSD, `f3probe`, then `spare-clone.sh init` and the boot test. Encryption decision is proposed (unencrypted clone, drive kept secure), awaiting Lutan: `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-spare-boot-drive-not-encrypted.md`.**  Lutan, 2026-10-02, who has a spare external USB SSD. **Worth doing, for fast recovery rather than for the code** (the code is already on GitHub and the laptop). **Main use, a bootable clone of the Pi's NVMe:** OS, packages, `/etc` and the things that are *not* in git (the two env files, the `cloudflared` tunnel credentials and config, the `lanna-care` systemd unit, Glances/monitoring config). Use `rpi-clone` again (`docs/pi-hosting.md` already uses it to copy SD → NVMe): `sudo rpi-clone sda` onto the USB drive, then on a timer (weekly, and before any OS upgrade), so the clone stays recent. The Pi's boot order `0xf146` tries NVMe first and USB next, so **verify** that with the NVMe removed or disabled the Pi boots from the USB clone and serves the site; that test is the point of the item. **Second use:** a copy of the weekly database dumps, which today sit on the NVMe beside everything else (plus Drive), e.g. `backup.mjs`'s planned `--local-copy` pointed at the USB mount. **Limits to write down:** same box, same house, so theft, fire, flood or a power surge take both drives; Drive and GitHub stay the off-site copies, this is the quick way back from a dead NVMe. Because the clone holds secrets and a USB drive is easy to walk off with, encrypt it (LUKS on the backup partition; a bootable clone needs either an unencrypted boot partition with the secrets on an encrypted one, or accept the risk and keep the drive physically secure; decide with Lutan). Check the Pi 5's power supply can drive the USB SSD alongside the NVMe HAT (the official 27 W supply), and that the drive shows up as the same device after a reboot (mount by UUID). Deliverable: the timer and script in `scripts/pi/`, a "Spare boot drive" section in `docs/pi-hosting.md` with the recovery steps, and a monitoring tile (clone age) in the Home Assistant item. **An SD card is an accepted interim spare, decided 2026-10-02, and it does only the bootable half.** Lutan has spare cards, and this closes the recovery gap without waiting for a purchase — which matters while the Pi is the single point of failure for every write on both environments and the last recovery was "Lutan restarted it". It genuinely works on this box: the Pi was first flashed to SD and `rpi-clone`d to the NVMe (`docs/pi-hosting.md:152-158`), and `BOOT_ORDER=0xf146` already reads 6 NVMe, 4 USB, 1 SD, so SD needs no eeprom change. **Three caveats.** (1) **Use a USB card reader, not the built-in slot.** `spare-clone.sh:102` tests `lsblk -dno TRAN` for `usb`, so `mmcblk0` is refused outright; a card in a reader reports `usb`, passes unchanged, and is tried earlier in the boot order too. No code change needed, and the check should stay as it is. (2) **Size.** `init` writes 512 MB boot plus `ROOT_SIZE`, default **64G**, so a 16-32 GB card needs `ROOT_SIZE` lowered to fit the NVMe’s *used* space — read `df -h /` on the Pi first. (3) **Still `f3probe` it.** SD is the most counterfeited storage there is and the drive tried on 2026-10-02 was a fake. Endurance is a moderate worry rather than a blocker, because `spare-clone.timer` runs `rpi-clone -u` incrementally and not a full weekly rewrite — but SD has no SMART and tends to fail silently or go read-only, on the one device you only reach for on the worst day. So **re-run the boot test periodically** and treat the card as interim, not as the answer. A branded SSD is still wanted: for the archive half (the item below) and for speed.
- [ ] **Keep a second copy of the deceased residents' archive on the Pi's USB SSD.** Lutan, 2026-10-02 (the USB SSD is now plugged into the Pi). Today a deceased resident's whole record lives only in Google Drive, under `Residents/Deceased/<Name> (<ID>)/`: the summary PDF, the offline `index.html` and the photos and files beside them (`src/lib/archive/archive-deceased-resident.ts`). This is the record the shelter keeps for good, so a copy that does not depend on Google is worth having. **What "offsite" means here:** the copy is independent of Google (an account lockout, a deleted folder, a Drive outage), but it sits in Lutan's house next to the Pi, so it is not offsite from the Pi; Drive stays the primary and this is the second copy. **Shape:** a pull-only mirror run by a timer on the Pi (nightly is plenty): walk `Residents/Deceased/` through the Drive API with the Pi's existing credentials, download new or changed files (compare Drive file id and `modifiedTime`, so the regenerated PDF and index after a bio or photo edit, `refreshDeceasedArchiveIfNeeded()`, are picked up), and never write to Drive. The offline `index.html` was built to be opened without the app, so the mirror is browsable straight off the drive. **Deletions:** if a folder disappears from Drive, keep the local copy and log it rather than deleting it (the point is to survive a mistake in Drive); a death withdrawn in error (`undo_deceased_placement()` moves the folder back under `Residents/`) should be noted in the log too, not silently kept as if still deceased. **Sharing the drive with the spare boot clone:** give each its own partition (the clone needs the whole-disk layout `rpi-clone` writes; the archive needs a plain data partition mounted by UUID), or pick one job for this drive; decide alongside that item. **Privacy:** the archive holds medical history and may name carers and adopters, so encrypt the archive partition (LUKS) as the spare-drive item suggests for the clone. **Multi-shelter:** keep one folder per shelter on the drive, so it fits the per-shelter Drive item. **Done when:** the mirror script and timer are in `scripts/pi/`, a restore note in `docs/pi-hosting.md` (how to copy a folder back to Drive), a "last archive sync" tile in the Home Assistant item, and a test: mark a test resident deceased on dev, wait for the sync, edit its bio, sync again, and confirm the new PDF is on the drive. **This half needs the real SSD; the interim SD card does not cover it, decided 2026-10-02.** Capacity is the obvious reason — the clone takes the whole of a small card and leaves nothing over — but the stronger one is that the two jobs want opposite things from the drive. The clone is deliberately **unencrypted** (`docs/decisions/2026-10-02-spare-boot-drive-not-encrypted.md`: a Pi that must come back unattended after a power cut cannot wait for a LUKS passphrase), while this archive **must** be LUKS-encrypted, since it holds medical history and names carers and adopters. The clone is also rewritten weekly, so rot gets caught; an archive written once and then left on flash would quietly stop being a real copy, with no SMART to say so — the wrong medium for "the record the shelter keeps for good". Waiting costs little: Drive remains the primary copy, and the exposure this item addresses is an account lockout or a deleted folder, not a dead NVMe. This also settles the question the item raises above — *"give each its own partition ... or pick one job for this drive"* — as **two devices, one job each.**

- [ ] **Give production its own Drive tree on the shelter's account, and leave UAT on the current one.** Decided 2026-09-23, replacing the earlier "transfer ownership of the existing tree" plan: production gets a **copy** of the tree in the shelter's own Google account (`lannacareforanimals@gmail.com`), and the existing tree stays where it is on `lannaanimalfoundationBWM` for UAT, dev and test. The point is isolation — UAT is where a tester deletes a photo, re-runs an archive, or drops a resident to see what happens, and it must not be able to do any of that to the customer's real files. A copy is what buys that; an ownership *transfer* does not, because the old owner keeps edit access to what is now the customer's data, and removing that access breaks every legacy file ID in the UAT database. It also means the shelter owns its own files, which a developer's personal Drive account holding the only copy of the archive never did. Steps: copy `Residents/`, `Projects/` and the rest of the root into the new account (`files.copy` in a small script, structure and folder *names* preserved — the import matches on them); mint a refresh token for that account (`scripts/google-oauth-setup.mjs`); put the four `GOOGLE_*` values, including the new `GOOGLE_DRIVE_ROOT_FOLDER_ID`, in `.env.deploy.production`, and the current ones in `.env.deploy.uat`; `node scripts/deploy.mjs --env production --secrets`. **No remap script needed** — `scripts/import-appsheet.mjs` resolves attachments by walking the tree under `GOOGLE_DRIVE_ROOT_FOLDER_ID` and *recording* the file IDs it finds, so the cutover import against the copied tree records the copies' IDs by itself. **But rehearse it against the dev database first:** the 2026-09-22 import matched resident folders partly by the AppSheet id in their names, and those folders have since been renamed to R-codes, so the resolution that worked then may not work now. Two smaller things to check: the shelter's free 15 GB has to hold the tree and the archive PDFs it will keep generating, and the `1036…` Drive OAuth client lives in a project under the dev Google account — the new refresh token can keep using it, or it moves alongside.
