The new production project at the cutover needs the same on `lannacareforanimals.org` (cutover item step 2; `docs/email-sending.md` last section). (redirect URLs are done). Forgot password (done 2026-09-21) sends through Supabase Auth, whose built-in mailer is development-only. **Provider decided 2026-09-23: Resend free tier** (Lutan; must cost nothing). Lutan creates the account, adds the DNS on `send.lannacare.org` and fills Supabase SMTP. Every field is in `docs/email-sending.md` §2 (lands with PR #62). Resend free holds one domain, so Production on `lannacareforanimals.org` needs a second free account or the paid plan; decide at the cutover.
- [x] **Dev and Test look different from production.** Done 2026-09-22: anything built against the dev Supabase project is teal with a green-cast background and a **Dev** badge in the header; production keeps the orange (`src/lib/app-env.ts`, README "Environments", `docs/decisions.md`).

- [x] **Clear the stocktake counts out of the UAT database — no physical count has ever been done.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, during the role walkthrough. **Done the same day** by `scripts/throwaway-clear-uat-stock.mjs --env production --apply`, dry-run first and verified clear by an independent read afterwards. What was actually there, once looked at rather than assumed: **nine medications and two diets** carrying a `stock_on_hand` figure, and **seven `stock_counts` rows**. Two medications and both diets were typed that morning by accident while signed into UAT instead of test; the other seven medications were typed on 2026-09-25. The seven history rows were the `0093` back-fill, not a stocktake — `counted_by` null, each with its own `stocktake_id` holding one row, which is the back-fill's signature for items edited seconds apart. `record_stocktake()` has never run against UAT. `stock_receipts` was **empty**: the earlier draft of this item said the delivery rows were test data too, and that was wrong — no delivery has ever been recorded on UAT. Nulling `stock_on_hand` was enough for both tables, because `0083`'s trigger clears `stock_counted_at` with it and callers cannot set the stamp directly. Deliberately **not** a numbered migration: a `0101` that deletes stock rows would run against dev too and wipe the walkthrough's own test data. One-off correction to one environment, so a throwaway script through the same Management API endpoint, as `throwaway-utc-date-audit.mjs` was. Delete the script once this is read.

- [x] **Confirm the Pi's first scheduled backup (Sunday 2026-10-04, 03:00 Thailand), then retire the laptop's.** From the Pi setup (2026-09-30): the Pi's cron job was proven by hand and under `env -i` but has not fired on schedule. On Monday 5 Oct check `ls -l ~/backups/lannacare`, `tail ~/backups/backup.log` on the Pi and the newest dump in Drive `Backups/` (Settings → System status shows it). If it is there, run `scriptsackup-schedule.ps1 -Remove` on the laptop and drop the laptop paragraph from README "Backups". Two schedulers until then is deliberate. A few minutes. **Done 2026-10-04.** The Pi’s cron fired at 03:00 Thailand and uploaded `lannacare-production-2026-10-03T2000Z.dump.age` (the timestamp is UTC; 20:00Z on the 3rd is 03:00 ICT on the 4th). Confirmed three ways: the file in Drive `Backups/`, the Pi’s own local copy in `~/backups/lannacare` at 03:00 (only the Pi keeps local copies, so that is what proves the Pi ran rather than the laptop), and a decrypt with the administrator’s key — `pg_restore --list` reads 1392 entries, 71 tables of data, and **no rows** for `auth.refresh_tokens`, `sessions`, `mfa_*`, `one_time_tokens` or `flow_state`, so DB-1’s exclusions hold in the artefact and not just in the arguments. **Drive holds two files for that morning** because the laptop’s task ran too, which was the deliberate overlap; its scheduled task has now been unregistered and README “Backups” updated. **Found on the way:** the laptop’s `backup.log` still held live Drive links to the unencrypted dumps of 21 and 26 September — the Pi’s log was truncated during the DB-1 rollout but the laptop’s was missed. Log truncated and the Drive trash emptied 2026-10-04. The Pi was re-checked and is clean: 0 links, no plaintext `*.dump`, folders `700`, dumps `600`.
- [ ] **Run the app on its own user on the Pi, separate from the backup.** From the Pi setup (2026-09-30): `lanna-care.service` and the backup cron both run as `lutan`, so a compromised app process could read the dumps and both `.env` files. A dedicated service user for `next start` (owning only what it needs) closes the gap the single-Pi decision records as not done (`docs/decisions/2026-09-30-one-pi-hosting-and-backup.md`). Do it with the security assessment's deployment-surface findings, and after DB-1, which shrinks what a read of the dumps would give.
- [ ] **`setup-test.sh` run under sudo leaves the whole test clone root-owned, and every later deploy fails.** Found 2026-10-02 during the `0.15.0` release: `deploy-pi.sh --env test` died with `EACCES` writing `.env.production.local`, then with `EACCES` on `node_modules/.bin` from `npm ci`. `find ~/Animal_Shelter_App_test -user root` counted **57,249** paths; the production clone counted **0**, which is why production deploys have never needed a password. The script is written to run as your own user and uses `sudo` only for its three systemd lines (`tee`, `daemon-reload`, `enable`), so running the whole thing with `sudo` makes root the owner of `node_modules`, `.next`, `next-env.d.ts` and the env file. Recovery needs an interactive `sudo chown -R lutan:lutan ~/Animal_Shelter_App_test` — the husk cannot even be deleted without root, because the directories holding it are root's. Add a guard at the top of `scripts/pi/setup-test.sh` (and `setup.sh`, same shape): refuse when `EUID` is 0, saying it needs sudo only for the systemd steps. Probably worth the same guard in `deploy-pi.sh`. Note the exec bit on `setup-test.sh` was only added by #303 on the same day, so `sudo bash scripts/pi/setup-test.sh` was the natural way to run it before that.

## Quick wins


- [x] **Two shell scripts are committed without the executable bit, and one of them is invoked by systemd.** Found 2026-10-02 setting test up on the Pi: `bash: ./scripts/pi/setup-test.sh: Permission denied`. `git ls-files -s '*.sh'` shows `deploy-pi.sh` and `setup.sh` as `100755` but **`setup-test.sh` and `spare-clone.sh` as `100644`**, in the index, so every clone gets them non-executable. `docs/pi-hosting.md:122` tells you to run `./scripts/pi/setup-test.sh`, which cannot work as committed; the workaround is `bash scripts/pi/setup-test.sh`. **`spare-clone.sh` is the one that matters more**, because `scripts/pi/spare-clone.service` has `ExecStart=__REPO__/scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh sync` — systemd invokes the file directly and requires the bit, so the timer would fail with the same error, silently, somewhere nobody is watching. It has not bitten yet only because that timer is not installed (see the spare-clone item). **Fix:** `git update-index --chmod=+x scripts/pi/setup-test.sh scripts/pi/spare-clone.sh` and commit; two mode changes, no content. **Worth pairing with a guard**, since this is the second thing this week to reach `main` because nothing checks `scripts/` — a syntax error in `deploy.mjs` did the same on 2026-10-02 and CI was green on it (see that item). One CI step would catch both classes: `node --check` over `scripts/**/*.mjs`, and a check that any `*.sh` named in a `.service` file's `ExecStart` is mode `100755`. **Done 2026-10-02** (`claude/script-exec-bits-guard`): both files are `100755`, and CI job `script-integrity` (`scripts/check-script-integrity.mjs`) now parses every `scripts/**/*.mjs` and fails on a `.service` `ExecStart` `.sh` that is not `100755` in the index, printing the `git update-index --chmod=+x` fix. It states what it does not cover. Decision: `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-script-integrity-reads-the-index.md`.

- [x] **Replace the Lanna Care for Animals logo with Lutan's better-quality JPEG.** **Done 2026-10-02** (`claude/logo-replace`): the same artwork at 781 × 746 px, 33 KB, used as supplied (small enough already, and near-identical in shape, so every square `object-contain` box takes it unchanged); filename kept; favicon and Open Graph image left as they are. Lutan, 2026-10-02. Today's `public/lca-logo.jpg` is only **124 × 117 px** (8 KB), so it looks soft anywhere it is drawn larger than a thumbnail. It is one file used in 12 places: `AppHeader`, `PublicHeader`, `layout.tsx` (metadata), the sign-in, forgot-password and request-access pages, `LockedLanding` and `OfflineBanner`; `src/lib/public-paths.ts` lists `/lca-logo.jpg` as readable signed out (both lists), so **keep the filename** and nothing else changes. Get the new file from Lutan; resize it for the web (about 512 px on the long side is plenty for a 2× / 3× screen at today's display sizes), keep JPEG unless it has a transparent background (then PNG), compress it (aim well under 100 KB), and check the aspect ratio still suits each place it is drawn (the current one is near square, about 1.06 : 1; a different shape needs the `width`/`height` props adjusted so nothing stretches). While there, decide whether the favicon (`public/favicon.ico`) and the Open Graph image should come from the same artwork. Check the header, a sign-in page and the locked landing page on desktop and phone, light and dark, and that a hard refresh shows the new logo (browsers may hold the old one until their cache expires; a `?v=` query on the `src` is an easy fix if it lingers). A releases line (people will notice the sharper logo). No schema.
- [x] **26 check scripts replay a migration file to build their fixture; any that read a view or function a later migration changed carry the trap that broke `check-shelter-today.mjs`.** Found 2026-10-02 on `claude/check-shelter-today-fix`. `grep -l "supabase/migrations" scripts/check-*.mjs`. Most test one migration's own new objects, where replay is right; sweep for the ones that touch shared views (`public_*`, `resident_current_state`) and have them assert live instead. Done 2026-10-02: 36 scripts ran against dev, 14 were failing; 11 now assert live, 4 keep the replay with a stale-data fix, 12 were left alone (replayed objects not redefined) and 9 never replayed anything. Rule and per-script verdicts in `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-replay-or-assert-live.md`.
- [x] **`scripts/check-shelter-today.mjs` fails on dev: it replays `0073` and so restores a stale `public_shelter_stats`.** Found 2026-10-02 on `claude/schema-soft-delete`. The harness runs `0073_shelter_today.sql` inside its rollback transaction, which recreates `public_shelter_stats` reading the public `resident_current_state`; since `0108` anon cannot execute `current_vet_resident_ids()` behind that view, so the final anon step fails with `permission denied for function current_vet_resident_ids`. The migration is fine — the harness predates `0108`. Fix: have it assert against the live views instead of replaying `0073`, or replay only the functions it tests.
- [x] **Move the "Public website" link off the left nav and onto the header logo.** Done 2026-09-22: the roundel + wordmark in `AppHeader` is one `<Link href="/">` that opens in a new tab (`target="_blank"`, `rel="noopener"`), titled and labelled `t.nav.publicSite`; the nav entry is gone and the manual's navigation topic says so. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-22. `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` lists `{ href: "/", label: t.nav.publicSite }` as an ordinary nav entry; the logo roundel + "LCA" wordmark in `src/app/AppHeader.tsx` is plain markup that links nowhere. Make the logo and wordmark one `<Link href="/">` (with `title`/`aria-label` from `t.nav.publicSite` so it is discoverable and screen-reader friendly), drop the nav entry, and check the phone drawer still has nothing referring to it. Consider opening the public site in a new tab (`target="_blank"`) since staff are mid-task when they click it; decide with the user. Update the manual page that mentions the Public website link if there is one (`src/lib/manual/`).
- [x] **Accordion the Management and Admin nav sections, collapsed by default.** Done 2026-09-22: a chevron button (`aria-expanded` / `aria-controls`) beside the parent link toggles the group; the label still navigates to the landing page. Collapsed on load, auto-opens whenever the route is inside the group, and the user's choice is kept in `sessionStorage` (`nav-expanded`); sidebar and drawer share the state. Original brief: Customer request 2026-09-22. In `src/app/NavLinks.tsx` the two parent items with `children` (Management: dashboard, contacts, vets, medications, diets, translations; Admin: website, security, enclosures, zones, immunization/procedure/blood-test types) always render their children expanded, so an admin sees ~15 links before the fold. Make each parent a toggle (chevron, `aria-expanded`) that starts collapsed on app load, auto-expands when the current route is inside it (so a refresh on `/admin/zones` still shows where you are), and remembers the user's choice for the session (`sessionStorage`, wrapped in try/catch). Clicking the parent label should still navigate to its landing page (`/management`, `/admin`) — put the toggle on the chevron, or expand on first click and navigate on the second; decide with the user. Same behaviour in the phone drawer. Non-admin roles are unaffected (they never see these groups).
- [x] **Remove the Assistant link from the left nav — it lives in the header now.** Raised 2026-09-24 by Lutan. The header button (`AssistantPanel` in `src/app/AppHeader.tsx`, gated by `canUseAssistant`) opens the same conversation as a slide-over on every screen, so the sidebar entry (`src/app/NavLinks.tsx`, its own one-item group between the reference lists and Management/Settings) is a duplicate. Drop that group, the `t.nav.assistant` string if nothing else uses it, and `NAV_ICONS.assistant` likewise. **Decide with the user first:** the nav entry is the only link to the full-page `/assistant` — once it goes, that page can be reached only by typing the URL. Either keep the page as a deep link (and say so in the nav comment), add a "full page" link from the slide-over, or retire the route. Update the user manual topic that tells people to find the assistant in the left nav, and add a line to `unreleased` in `src/lib/releases.ts` — users will notice the entry disappear.
- [x] **A refused page and a booked vet visit both dump you onto the public website.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. `/` is the public home page, so every `redirect("/")` in the app throws a signed-in user out of the app entirely — no explanation, no way back except the menu they no longer have. Found when a vet tapped a stocktake recurring job. Four sites, and the last is the worst: `src/app/stocktake/page.tsx:37` and `src/app/deliveries/page.tsx:56` (role refusal), `src/lib/auth/require-management.ts` (every `/management/*` refusal), and **`src/app/vet-visits/new/actions.ts:71`, which is the success path** — *every* role that books a vet visit lands on the public website afterwards, not just vets. **Two different fixes.** The success path should go back to the resident or the visit it just created. The refusals should say "you do not have access to this" on a page inside the app, or fall back to `DEFAULT_SIGNED_IN_PATH` (`/my`) rather than `/` — `signedInLandingPath` in `src/lib/auth/app-access.ts` already knows the difference between an app user and a public viewer, and these guards do not use it. Worth a sweep for other `redirect("/")` calls added since. Small, and it makes every role-gating item below testable without the tester being ejected mid-pass.
- [x] **Show a file-type icon for attachments that are not images.** Done 2026-09-28 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `FileTypeIcon` (lucide `FileText` / `FileImage` / `File`, PDFs in red, an accessible name in both languages) replaces the 📄 glyph in procedures, blood tests, maintenance, project photos and the pending-files list; one `fileKind()` in `src/lib/uploads/file-kind.ts` replaces four copies of the image regex. Original item: Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough., noted while procedures passed. Uploading both an image and a PDF to a procedure worked, and both opened. The PDF just has nothing to look at in the list. A small type icon (PDF, doc, generic) where the thumbnail would be makes a list of mixed attachments readable at a glance. Nice to have, not blocking.
- [ ] **Contact page map preview is too wide on desktop.** Customer request 2026-09-24. On a contact's page (`/contacts/[id]`), the Google Maps preview under the address overflows the screen width in desktop layout. The iframe is in `src/app/contacts/[id]/ContactHub.tsx` (`h-[200px] w-full`, inside a `min-w-0 flex-1` column beside the address icon) — find which ancestor lets it grow past the viewport (a flex/grid child missing `min-w-0`, or the iframe's intrinsic width), and give it a sensible cap (e.g. `max-w-md`/`max-w-lg`) so it sits under the address rather than spanning the card. Check phone width too, and both themes.
- [ ] **`release-smoke-test.md` still says to commit the release record "on whatever branch is to hand".** Overridden 2026-10-03: Lutan ruled there are no exceptions to the branch-and-PR route, the release record included, after `0.15.0`'s record and `docs/release-procedure.md` were pushed straight to `main` and had to be reverted (`ac9b3b2`, `c00de0e`). `0.15.1`'s record went through PR #317 instead. Fix the line in `docs/release-smoke-test.md`, and check `docs/release-procedure.md` §8 says the same — it currently repeats the old wording too. A one-line docs change, deliberately kept out of the record's own PR so the record was not the vehicle for changing the rule it was subject to.

## Mobile

- [ ] **A 375 px overflow check, so a page that scrolls sideways fails before it ships.** Left over from F-06 (2026-10-03). The shared cause is fixed (a base-layer `.grid { grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) }` and `max-width: 100%` on form controls, `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-phone-width.md`), but nothing stops the next flex row or fixed-width box from overflowing again. A guard needs real rendering: `playwright-core` is already a dependency (`scripts/manual-screenshots.mjs`), but it needs a running server, a signed-in session per role and a page list with real data (an enclosure with a long name, a vet with a long clinic), so it is a project, not a one-liner, and was deliberately not half-built. **Shape:** a `scripts/check-phone-width.mjs` run by hand or in the release smoke test (not `npm run lint`) that opens each page at 375 px in English and Thai and asserts `scrollingElement.scrollWidth <= clientWidth`, printing the offending element. → *Mobile*
- [ ] **Turn word-only action links into icon buttons, so actions are easier to find and tap.** Lutan, 2026-10-02: there are a lot of word-based action links, and actions would be easier to navigate with icons and buttons. **What is there today (counted 2026-10-02):** about **95** links styled as plain text (`text-primary` + `hover:underline`) across roughly 45 files under `src/app`, most in Management (16 files), then residents, maintenance, weight, vets, prescriptions and diets. The building blocks for the better version already exist but are barely used: `src/components/ActionLink.tsx` (icon + label, primary/secondary variants) appears in only 3 files, `RecordRowActions.tsx` handles per-row actions, and `src/components/hub-icons.ts` (`NAV_ICONS` and the hub's lucide icons) is the shared icon vocabulary. **Not every text link should change**, so start with an audit, one line per link: **navigation** in a sentence or a list ("see the vet's page", a resident's name in a table) stays a text link; an **action** (Add, Edit, Record, Book, Move, Remove, Download, Publish) becomes a button. Then three patterns, used the same way everywhere: (1) the page's **main action** is a filled button with an icon and its word (`ActionLink` primary, e.g. "+ Record a vet visit"); (2) **secondary actions** are outlined icon + word buttons; (3) **actions repeated on every row** of a table or list become icon buttons (pencil, bin, download and so on) through `RecordRowActions`, each with a tooltip and an `aria-label` that names the row ("Edit Panda's weight on 2 Oct"), because an icon alone means nothing to a screen reader. Destructive actions keep their confirm dialog and a distinct (danger) style. **Rules:** one icon per meaning across the whole app (an icon map in `hub-icons.ts`, extended rather than chosen per page); tap targets at least 44 px on phones; Thai labels are often longer, so check every button in Thai at 375 px for wrapping; keep the word beside the icon wherever there is room, since icon-only is only for repeated row actions; and do not change what any action does, only how it is shown. **Split into PRs by area** (Management, resident hub and its tabs, maintenance, the rest), each with before/after screenshots on desktop and phone, in English and Thai, so Lutan can approve the look on the first area before the rest follow. Read with the Mobile responsiveness sweep (same section) and the public redesign's 44 px touch-target rule. Manual screenshots will change; follow the deferred full re-run rather than per PR. A releases line per area.
- [x] **Review which admin features (if any) belong on mobile.** *Done 2026-09-24: verdict table in decisions.md ("Admin on mobile"); desktop-only setup pages show a "Best on a larger screen" notice below `md`.* Intent: the phone experience stays a field tool — what a volunteer or staff member needs while standing in an enclosure (find a resident, log weight/immunization/vet visit/procedure, move or hospitalise, add photos, check enclosure occupancy). Admin-type work (Admin → Security, Website, Zones, Enclosures, Immunization types, future Vets / Contacts / Projects / Medications admin pages, and any bulk or reporting screens) most likely lives on desktop only, but decide that per screen rather than by assumption. Go through every route under `/admin` plus the desktop-only affordances elsewhere (residents table bulk selection, zone/enclosure filters, edit forms with many fields) and for each record: needed in the field / nice-to-have / desktop only, with a one-line reason. Output is a short table in `docs/decisions.md` and the resulting changes: hide desktop-only nav entries and pages below the `md` breakpoint (or show a "use a larger screen" notice rather than a broken layout), and make sure anything marked field-needed is covered by the Mobile responsiveness sweep below. Also check the role angle — volunteers never see admin pages anyway, so the question is mainly what admins/staff reach for on a phone.
- [x] **Inline-edited rows show stale data until reload.** *Closed 2026-09-24 as not reproducible on Next 16.3.5: with `refresh()` removed, button-driven rename and delete updated the row in place on zones and procedure-types (the page it was reported on). No code changed; see decisions.md, 2026-09-24.* Found 2026-09-21 while building `/admin/procedure-types`: a server action called from a button (`startTransition(() => updateX(...))`, as every admin/management table's Save / Delete / Merge does) revalidates the path but does *not* refresh the client router on Next 16, so the row keeps showing the old name until the next navigation — only `<form action>` submissions refresh on their own. The fix is `refresh()` from `next/cache` after the `revalidatePath` calls in the action (procedure-types does this; see its `revalidateProcedureTypePages`). Apply the same one-liner to the immunization-types, zones, enclosures, security, contacts, vets and medications actions.
- [ ] **Mobile responsiveness sweep.** **Scheduling decided 2026-10-02: it stays one whole item and Lutan schedules it deliberately — `/plan-day` should stop offering it as a candidate.** It had been proposed and declined nine times, once with a genuinely clear run (both sibling streams were `scripts/`-only, so the UI was uncontested). Splitting it into per-screen items and retiring the umbrella was considered and **turned down**; so was retiring it outright. It is not stalled and it is not a planning oversight — it is waiting for a batch Lutan clears for it, and he will say when. Until then, phone defects noticed in passing still get filed individually, as *drop the Resident ID column on phones* was (shipped #233). Go through every screen (residents list, resident hub, forms, admin pages, login) at phone width and fix layout, table overflow, and spacing so it feels professional in portrait. **Start from the admin-on-mobile list** (decisions.md, 2026-09-24, "Admin on mobile"): its field-needed rows (enclosure filters, resident edit, intake) and Security's users-table role column. **Known defect found there:** `/enclosures` scrolls the whole page sideways at 375px (`scrollWidth` 492 on dev data) — the per-zone card grids (`grid gap-3 sm:grid-cols-2 …`) have no column template below `sm`, so the single implicit column grows to the widest card's content (Main Zone section 468px). `grid-cols-1` (`minmax(0,1fr)`) is the likely fix, unverified; the `-mx-6` zone-chip strip also pushes its wrapper to 351px.

- [x] **A date field cannot be cleared once it has been set, on mobile.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough., found on a diet's end date. Tapping an optional date field commits a value, and there is then no way to get back to empty — so an optional date becomes mandatory the moment it is touched by accident, and the only escape is abandoning the form. Likely affects **every optional date in the app**, not just the diet end date, so fix it in the shared date input rather than one form: a visible Clear control beside a populated optional field, and make sure the empty value actually reaches the server as null. Check the desktop behaviour too — the native picker may allow clearing there, which is exactly how this stays invisible to whoever built it.
- [x] **Residents list on a phone: drop the Resident ID column.** Done 2026-09-30: the ID `<th>`/`<td>` are `hidden md:table-cell`; comment and manual caption updated. Lutan, 2026-09-29. Below `md`, `/residents` still shows two data columns, the R-code and the name (plus the copy-link and edit icons), and the ID pushes long names (with their Thai and other names) into wrapping, so each row is taller than it needs to be. Hide the ID `<th>` and `<td>` below `md` the same way the other columns are (`hidden md:table-cell` in `src/app/residents/ResidentsTable.tsx`, around lines 136 and 181), so the name gets the width; desktop keeps the column. Update the comment above the table ("Phones show only ID and name") and the manual caption in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` (~line 282: "Phones show just the ID and name"; there is no Thai manual file yet). The R-code is still on the resident hub, and search by R-code (the adopted-residents search item) is unaffected. Check at 375px with a long bilingual name, and that tapping a row still opens the resident. A releases line (staff will notice the tidier list). No schema.

## Resident operations

- [x] **Record updates from adopters on an adopted resident.** Lutan, 2026-09-26: adopters send news from time to time, and it should go on the animal's record. An adopted animal's hub gets an **Adoption updates** section: date, who sent it (the adopter contact from the Adopt placement's `carer_id`, preselected), how it came in (LINE, Facebook, email, visit), a note and photos. Photos go through the existing resident photo route into a `Residents/<Name> (<ID>)/Adoption updates/` folder. **Keep each photo tagged with its update** (Lutan, 2026-09-26): the photo's record links to the update, so wherever it appears (the update itself, the resident's Photos tab, a future public "Happy endings" card) it shows where it came from: sent by the adopter, on which date, via which channel (LINE, Facebook, email, visit). Photos taken by staff at the shelter stay untagged, so the two can be told apart and filtered. Adopted residents are not locked like the deceased, so no trigger change is needed; check RLS for staff and management. Probably its own table (`adoption_updates`) rather than notes on the placement, so there can be many, in a schema PR first. **Nice follow-on, ask first:** with the adopter's permission, a published update could feed the public "Happy endings" strip on `/adopt` (0061), a strong adoption story for the website. Manual (residents list search, adopted hub), both dictionaries, a releases line.
- [x] **My dashboard — "what I need to do today", starting with my maintenance jobs.** Lutan, 2026-09-25. A page for the signed-in user (e.g. `/my`, first entry in `NavLinks.tsx` for every role) listing the work assigned to *them*. **Version 1: maintenance only.** Every job assigned to the user that is not `Done` (user in `maintenance_assignees`, 0063; a job can go to a team, so show who else is on it), grouped by `due_date` (0033) — overdue, due today, later, no due date — with status, zone and a link to `/maintenance/[id]`, plus quick status changes through the existing maintenance actions — no new write path. Empty state that says there is nothing assigned. No schema needed: assignment and due date already exist (confirmed by Lutan, 2026-09-25). **Built to grow:** later sources plug into the same page as further sections, each a small loader returning a common task shape (title, what it is about, when, link, done action). Named so far: residents to take to the vet (vet visits already exist under `/vet-visits`), giving medications to a zone of animals (prescriptions, grouped by zone), and ordering stock (the stock-on-hand work in progress). Most of those need an *assignee* they do not have today, so each is its own later item with its own schema PR. Leave assignment of those out of v1. Also consider a count badge on the nav entry and whether this becomes the page staff land on after sign-in (today `/` is the public home for everyone). Manual topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts`.
- [x] **Recurring jobs for staff, feeding My dashboard.** [done 2026-09-26: schema `0095` (#155), then Management → Recurring jobs (`src/app/management/recurring-jobs`) and the Recurring jobs section of My tasks (`src/lib/my-tasks/recurring.ts`), including the sick-week hand-over form; see docs/decisions.md 2026-09-26.] Lutan, 2026-09-25. Let management set up jobs that repeat on a calendar and are assigned to a person (or a team, like maintenance, 0063). Examples: "stocktake of on-hand medication, every Monday morning, staff member X", then "order and buy medication, every Monday after the stocktake is done, staff member Y". **Templates vs occurrences:** a `recurring_jobs` template (title, description, assignee(s), recurrence rule, time of day such as morning/afternoon, optional link to the screen the job is done on, active flag) produces *occurrences* that are transient. Each one shows on the assignee's My dashboard (the item above) for its day and is marked done or skipped, and only that result is kept (who, when, a note), so there is a record that the stocktake happened. Decide whether occurrences are generated ahead (a cron in `worker/`, or on first read of the day) or computed from the rule and stored only when acted on; the second avoids a job table filling with future rows. **Recurrence:** a small, explicit subset rather than full iCal RRULE: every N weeks on chosen weekday(s) (weekly, fortnightly with a start date as the anchor), monthly on day N, monthly on the Nth weekday ("first Monday"), with a start date and an optional end date. Dates are in Asia/Bangkok. **Order between jobs:** "after the stocktake" means an occurrence can depend on another template's occurrence the same day, and shows as "waiting for stocktake" until that one is done. **Missed jobs:** decide whether an undone occurrence stays overdue on the dashboard until done or skipped (suggest yes, with how many days late) or quietly expires. **Admin:** a Management page to create, edit, pause and reassign templates, with a preview of the next few dates, and reassignment when a login is archived. The stocktake example links naturally to the "Stock on hand" item below; the job can deep-link to its count screen, `/stocktake` (`?tab=diets` opens the Diets tab), which staff and volunteers can use (0091). Needs its own schema PR first. **Reassigning one week** (Lutan, 2026-09-26): if someone is sick, management can hand that date's occurrence to someone else without changing the job, and the next week goes back to the usual person; the schema for it is in `0095` (cover team per date, `reassign_recurring_job()`, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-26), and the admin page needs the form, including "all of X's jobs for these dates → Y". Manual topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts`.
- [ ] **Deceased residents: confirm what happens to the archive when photos or the bio change after death.** Customer question 2026-09-22 — check the business logic and close the gaps. *How it works today:* the three edits still allowed on a deceased resident (0052: bio via `/residents/[id]/edit`, photo upload via `src/app/api/residents/[id]/photos/route.ts`, set-profile-photo and delete-photo in `src/app/residents/[id]/photos/actions.ts`) each call `refreshDeceasedArchiveIfNeeded()` (`src/lib/archive/refresh-deceased-archive.ts`), which re-runs `archiveDeceasedResident()` — the folder move no-ops and the summary PDF and `index.html` in `Residents/Deceased/<Name> (<ID>)/` are replaced in place, so the archive follows the record. *Gaps to decide on:* (1) it is **best effort and silent** — every caller ignores the returned `{ error }`, so if Drive is down the edit saves but the PDF/index stay stale and nobody is told; either surface it (hub banner "Archive out of date — Retry", reusing the existing Retry button) or record a `deceased_archive_stale_at` on `residents` that the hub shows. (2) It only runs when `deceased_archived_at` is set: a resident whose first archive never completed does not get one from an edit — fine, but the hub should say so. (3) A multi-photo upload regenerates the PDF once **per file** (sequential uploads); cheap for a handful, but debounce or run once after the batch if it turns out slow. (4) A HEIC profile photo is never embedded in the PDF (`EMBEDDABLE_IMAGE_TYPES`), so choosing a phone photo as profile after death gives a PDF without a picture — convert on upload or note it in the manual. (5) Verify in dev end to end: mark a test resident deceased, add a photo, change the bio, set a different profile photo, delete a photo — open the PDF and `index.html` in Drive after each step and confirm the change is there; write the outcome into the manual's deceased topic. *Status 2026-10-02 (`claude/deceased-archive-followups`, decisions `2026-10-02-deceased-archive-followups`):* gaps 1, 2, 3 and 5 are closed or consciously declined (walked end to end in dev; a simulated Drive outage shows the warning; persistent flag and debouncing declined). **Still open: gap 4 — upload a real iPhone HEIC after death, choose it as profile, and confirm the PDF has the picture.**
- [ ] **Regenerate the six production deceased-resident summary PDFs once the layout fix is live.** Filed 2026-09-24 from `claude/deceased-pdf`. Every archive PDF generated before that fix has the resident's name printed over the subtitle and no footer (name, ID, generated date, page N / M), and a deploy does not rewrite them — only a bio/photo edit on the deceased resident, or the hub's Retry for an archive that never completed, calls `archiveDeceasedResident()`. Six residents in production have one. Release-time work, after the deploy that carries the fix: regenerate each (re-saving the bio unchanged should do it via `refreshDeceasedArchiveIfNeeded()` — confirm that a no-op save actually triggers it, or add an admin "Regenerate archive" action if not) and open each PDF in Drive to check.

Needs a `user_id` on `contacts` (or a join table) to link a login to a carer, plus RLS policies keyed off it.
- [x] **Low priority: check the residents list's filter and search logic for adopted residents.** Done 2026-09-30 as a hardening, **not a reproduction**: the disappearance was not reproduced, and (a)–(c) are the code-explained causes; (d) was not checked against live data (see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-residents-list-adopted-search.md`). Search now matches the R-code, an adopted-matches pointer appears when a place, zone or enclosure filter hides them, and there is an Adopted chip. Lutan, 2026-09-26: a search for adopted Panda with Show all on once found nothing, but it could not be reproduced later the same day, so this is an investigation rather than a known bug. The code says adopted residents should appear. `resident_list_view` (0058) has no status filter, and `src/app/residents/page.tsx` hides only Deceased (`NOT_DECEASED`). Adopted is left out only by the On-site / Off-site place filter (`STATUSES_IN_PLACE` in `src/lib/residents/place.ts`: "Adopted and Deceased are history, not a place"). Likely causes to check: (a) the list was on **On-site** or **Off-site**, where adopted animals never show and the Show all toggle is ignored (`showAll` needs Everywhere), yet nothing says so; (b) a **zone chip** was selected, and adopted animals sit in the Lifecycle pseudo-zone; (c) the search was by **R-code**, but search only matches name, Thai name and other names; (d) RLS or data on the site used (the adopted row's `current_status`). **Improvements worth making anyway:** make search reach adopted (and deceased) animals from any place, the way a deceased name match already offers "show" (`deceasedMatchesHref`), e.g. "2 adopted residents match, show"; add R-code to the search; and consider an **Adopted** chip or "Past residents" view so staff can find them on purpose rather than by accident.

- [x] **Move a resident photo to a different folder when it was filed wrongly.** Lutan, 2026-09-27. A photo uploaded under the wrong category — typically recorded as **Shelter** when it is really a **Medical** photo — can today only be deleted and uploaded again, which loses its date taken and caption and is easy to skip. Add a **Move to folder** action in the photo viewer (`src/components/PhotoGallery.tsx`) offering the other `PHOTO_CATEGORIES` (Shelter / Medical / Foster / Adoption). **It matters for privacy, not just tidiness:** `0101` keeps Medical photos off the public site by `attachments.sub_folder`, and the migration's own note says it "cannot catch a medical photo filed in the wrong folder; that is for staff to refile" — this item is that refile. **Two halves, both needed:** update `attachments.sub_folder`, and move the Drive file to `Residents/<Name> (<ID>)/Photos/<Category>/<YYMM>/` (reuse `ensureResidentPhotosFolder` in `src/lib/google/drive.ts` with the photo's own date taken, then change the file's parents), so Drive and the database never disagree. Do the Drive move first and write the row only once it succeeds. **Rules to settle in the build:** moving a photo *into* Medical when it is the resident's profile photo must be refused or clear the profile photo, the same rule as `src/lib/residents/profile-photo.ts`; a role limited to one folder (a vet can only upload to Medical) should only be able to move photos into that folder, not out of it; adoption-update photos (dated folders, no category) are out of scope. Works for deceased residents too, since photos stay editable after death (decisions.md 2026-09-21) — check the archive path. Manual: a line in the photos topic in `en.ts` / `th.ts`; a releases line.
- [x] **Microchip number on a resident, searchable from a chip scanner.** Done 2026-09-30 across 0113, PR 221, 0116 and `claude/microchip-vet-feature` (0117); see the second-half item below and `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-microchip-second-half-vet-form-public-boolean.md`. Not built: a header search (the app has none) and the optional duplicate-scan box on the intake wizard's first step. Lutan, 2026-09-29. Two reasons: overseas adoptions need it (a microchip is the first thing an export permit and the destination country ask for, and `/adopt/international` already lists it), and the app should encourage shelters to chip their animals rather than treat it as optional. **Why now, when it isn't standard practice:** `0060_adoption_recommendation.sql` and `docs/decisions.md` (2026-09-21) left the field out because microchipping isn't done as a matter of course in Thailand. That is still true (Lutan, 2026-09-29); the field is wanted anyway, to support overseas adoptions, to make chipping easy for the shelters that do it, and as groundwork for the public register item under Public website. Add a `docs/decisions.md` entry saying so, which extends rather than contradicts the 0060 one. "Microchipping" already exists as a procedure type (0031), so the act of chipping can already be logged, just not the number. **Schema PR first:** `residents.microchip_number text` (nullable), stored as digits only (strip spaces and dashes on save), **unique** across residents where set (a partial unique index, so a mistyped duplicate is caught), with a check that allows the ISO 11784/11785 standard 15 digits and, if the shelter has older chips, 9/10-digit ones too (ask Lutan whether any exist before tightening it). Optional `microchip_implanted_on date`. Locked with the rest of the record on death (the 0026 trigger's column list). **Where it is entered:** Edit resident and the intake wizard (Health step), and offered straight after a "Microchipping" procedure is logged ("Record the chip number?"). Show it on the hub's details card, in the deceased archive PDF and index, and on the vet-visit and procedure views where a vet needs it. **Scanner search:** handheld and USB/Bluetooth microchip readers act as a keyboard: they type the 15 digits and press Enter. So (1) the `/residents` search and the header search match `microchip_number` exactly (after stripping non-digits), alongside name and R-code; an exact chip match jumps straight to that resident's hub, whatever the place/zone filters or deceased toggle say; (2) a small **Scan a chip** box (autofocused input, e.g. on `/residents` and the intake wizard's first step) so a staff member can scan a stray at the gate: a known chip opens the resident, an unknown one offers "New resident with this chip" prefilled. Phones cannot read the common 134.2 kHz (FDX-B) chips over NFC, so do not promise phone scanning; test with a real reader Lutan has, or by typing into the box. **Privacy and public site:** the number stays staff-only. Do not add it to any `public_*` view; the public resident page may show "Microchipped: yes" (a boolean derived from the column), which is the flag 0060's "Desexed / Vaccinated" ticks were meant to have. Vets see it for residents in their scope. **Encouraging chipping:** a count on the management dashboard (residents without a chip), a filter on `/residents` ("No microchip"), and a gentle prompt on the hub for a resident marked ready for adoption with no chip. Keep it a nudge, not a block. Manual (intake, resident hub, a short "Scanning a microchip" topic), both dictionaries, a releases line. Read it beside the multi-shelter spike (Architecture): a chip number is globally unique, so the same animal turning up at two shelters is a real case to design for there. **Schema half done 2026-09-29 (0113, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-microchip-number-on-residents-15-digits-staff-only.md`):** `microchip_number` (exactly 15 digits, unique where set) and `microchip_implanted_on` on `residents`, locked on death, no public view touched; `check-public-views.mjs` now refuses anon both columns. **The check is 15-digit only** (Lutan, 2026-09-29: no 9/10-digit chips), so ignore the "9/10-digit" allowance above. **Entry, display and scanner search done 2026-09-29 (PR 221):** number and implant date on Edit resident and intake, shown on the hub with a no-chip nudge, and the Scan a chip box on `/residents` (exact match jumps to the hub, unknown chip offers a prefilled new resident). **Still to do:** see the follow-on item below (vet entry, procedure prompt, archive PDF, vet views, public boolean, dashboard count, No microchip filter).
- [x] **Microchip, second half: vet entry, procedure prompt, archive, public flag, dashboard nudge.** Done 2026-09-30 (0117, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-microchip-second-half-vet-form-public-boolean.md`): one Record chip / Correct form through `set_resident_microchip()` on the hub, the Vet appointments and Procedures tabs and a visit's Edit page, for admin, staff and vets in scope, with each refusal mapped to its own message; "Record the chip number?" after a Microchipping procedure; the chip in the deceased summary PDF and offline index; "Microchipped" on `/adopt/[id]` from `public_resident_profiles.is_microchipped` (boolean only); an in-care "no microchip" count on the management dashboard; a No microchip filter on `/residents`; the Scanning a microchip manual topic (English only; no Thai manual yet). The optional intake duplicate-scan box was not built. Lutan, 2026-09-29. The rest of the item above after PR 221. **Vet entry (asked for 2026-09-29):** a vet must be able to add or correct a chip from their own interface. Vets can read residents in their clinic's scope (0108) but have no update on `residents`, so this needs a **schema PR first** (`claude/microchip-vet-schema`, next free number after `0114`): a security-definer function, e.g. `set_resident_microchip(resident_id, number, implanted_on)`, that allows staff and a vet whose resident is in `current_vet_resident_ids()`, refuses a deceased resident, strips nothing (the app strips; the 15-digit check and unique index still apply) and does not widen any other column. Then the feature stream: a vet form on the resident hub and vet views, using that function. **Also:** offer "Record the chip number?" straight after a Microchipping procedure is logged; the chip in the deceased archive PDF and index; on the vet-visit and procedure views; **"Microchipped: yes" on `/adopt/[id]`** as a boolean derived from the column (a `public_*` view exposes the boolean only, never the number, and `check-public-views.mjs` must still refuse anon the number); a count on the management dashboard (residents with a chip vs without); a "No microchip" filter on `/residents`; the Scanning a microchip manual topic and Thai text if a Thai manual exists by then. The duplicate-scan box on the intake wizard's first step is optional. **Vet-entry schema done 2026-09-29 (0116, see `docs/decisions/2026-09-29-vet-microchip-write-via-definer-function.md`):** `set_resident_microchip(p_resident_id uuid, p_number text, p_implanted_on date default null) returns void`, security definer, for admin, staff and a vet whose resident is in `current_vet_resident_ids()`. It refuses a deceased resident (`restrict_violation`) and a vet outside scope (`insufficient_privilege`), and leaves the 0113 check and unique index to refuse malformed or duplicate numbers (`check_violation` / `unique_violation`), so the app must strip first. Null clears the chip, and the date is overwritten with what is passed, so the form should always send both. The vet form and the rest are the feature stream.

## Medical records

- [ ] **Clinics and doctors: remove the word "Vet" from the system — DECIDED 2026-10-03, now a rename (schema PR, then the sweep).** **PARKED 2026-10-03 (Lutan): do not start.** Lutan has further clarity from the Director and will raise it as a new item, which may change or replace parts of this one; read that item first and reconcile the two before building anything here. Lutan, 2026-10-03: "We seem to have a disconnect between Clinic, vets and Doctors." **The model, decided by Lutan the same day:** (1) **A Clinic is what the system used to call a Vet.** It is the place a resident is taken to and a visit is booked with. **"Vet" goes from the system entirely** (data, screens, menus, both languages, manual) so the word cannot mean two things. (2) **A Doctor is a person who works at a clinic.** Doctors and clinics are **many-to-many**: one or more doctors, each connected to one or more clinics where they work. (3) **A doctor may or may not have a login.** The login is attached to the doctor record (at most one login per doctor, at most one doctor per login), and most doctors will never have one. **How far today's system already matches:** the shape is mostly right and the names are wrong. `vet_doctors` are people, linked to clinics through `vet_doctor_clinics` (0125); a login is linked to a doctor by `vet_doctors.user_id` and gets its clinics from that doctor (0127); a visit points at a clinic and optionally a doctor, and the composite key keeps the doctor at that clinic. **What is wrong:** (a) the clinic table is called **`vets`**, and its rows carry both a `name` and a `clinic_name` (0001), a leftover from when a row could be a person; (b) the UI says "Vets" in the nav (`/vets`), Management → Vets and the booking form, but "clinics" in the doctor screens and Settings → Security; (c) `vet_doctors.vet_id` is still there, deprecated, as "first-listed clinic" (0125); (d) the **`vet` role** names a login after the old word. **The rename:** `vets` → `clinics` (fold `name` / `clinic_name` into one clinic name plus the contact fields; check every row on dev and production first and agree with Lutan any row where the two differ, rather than guessing which to keep); `vet_doctors` → `doctors`; `vet_doctor_clinics` → `doctor_clinics`; `vet_appointments` → `clinic_visits` or similar (choose with Lutan; it is the staff-facing "vet visit", so the screen label changes too); `vet_id` columns → `clinic_id`; drop `vet_doctors.vet_id`; functions `current_user_vet_ids()`, `current_vet_resident_ids()`, `merge_vet_doctors()`, `schedule_bulk_appointments()` and the vet-scoped `security definer` writers renamed or rewritten, with every RLS policy that names them; views (`vet_contacts` from 0126 and anything else with `vet` in it). **The `vet` role becomes `doctor`** (confirmed by Lutan, 2026-10-03: the vet login is the doctor's login): `alter type app_role rename value 'vet' to 'doctor'` keeps existing policies working, because they store the enum value, not the text, but every literal `'vet'` in `src/`, `worker/` and the check scripts has to change in the same release. Routes: `/vets` → `/clinics`, `/management/vets` → `/management/clinics`, `/vet-visits` → a clinic-visit route, each old URL kept as a redirect so bookmarks and printed links survive. **Order and safety:** it is a large rename across live tables, so do it as **one schema PR, re-runnable, with compatibility views** under the old table names for one release if anything outside the repo reads them (backups, `import-appsheet.mjs`, the assistant's lookups), dry-run on dev first, and land the app rename in the PR straight after, so `main` never carries schema the code does not know. Rewrite `scripts/check-doctor-multi-clinic.mjs`, `check-vet-resident-scope.mjs`, `check-vets-readonly.mjs`, `check-contact-visibility.mjs` and `check-public-views.mjs` with it. **Then the screens:** the nav and Management tile read **Clinics**; a clinic's page lists its doctors; booking asks for a clinic and offers its doctors; Settings → Security links a login to a doctor (as now) under the role name **Doctor**; the assistant's vet intent understands "clinic" and "doctor" (keep "vet" and "หมอ" as words people type, since users will still say them, but nothing the app shows says Vet). Manual (rename the Vets topics, Security, booking a visit), both dictionaries, the role walkthrough's Vet pass becomes the Doctor pass, release notes by role, and a releases line, since every staff member will see the labels change. **Mobile vets, decided by Lutan 2026-10-03:** a vet who works without premises is still running a business, and **the business is the clinic**. So they are entered as a clinic like any other, just with **no address or map link**. Every clinic field except the name is therefore optional, and the clinic page, booking form and any map or directions link must show nothing (no empty map, no broken link) when there is no address. **A visit's doctor stays optional, always** (Lutan, 2026-10-03): a clinic visit requires the clinic and never the doctor, so the rename must not add a not-null or a required field anywhere a doctor is chosen. **Decide while renaming: what a doctor login sees.** Lutan (2026-10-03) expected it to be every resident *that doctor* has cared for, whichever clinic it was at. **That is not what is built.** `current_vet_resident_ids()` (0108, last redefined in 0125, its clinic set from 0127) is **clinic-scoped**: a login sees every resident with a live visit, prescription, procedure or blood test at **any clinic its doctor currently works at**, whoever the doctor on that visit was, and nothing tied to a clinic the doctor has left (an inactive link drops it). It never looks at `doctor_id`. That was the rule Lutan chose on 2026-09-28 ("any clinical record, clinic-level", so a colleague covering a shift sees the same list), before doctors could have logins. Options: (a) keep clinic-scoped; (b) **doctor-scoped**: residents on visits where this doctor is the doctor, at any clinic, past clinics included; (c) both together (the union). (b) alone hides residents seen by a colleague at the same clinic, and a visit with no doctor recorded (optional, always) is then seen by no doctor login at all, so (c) is the likely answer. Also decide whether write rights follow the same rule (0110 limits writes to the login's own clinics). Ask Lutan, record it, and change the function in the rename's schema PR, with `check-vet-resident-scope.mjs` covering each case. **A doctor's own page already exists:** `/appointments` (#227) is the doctor login's landing page, so the rename only relabels it. **Still open, small:** for **staff**, is a list of doctors needed across all clinics (e.g. Management → Doctors, to find one by name and see every clinic they work at), or is each clinic's Doctors list enough? **Read with:** the per-shelter roles and permissions item (Architecture), which turns role names into data anyway, so the `doctor` rename should be done to fit it; and the walkthrough patch item (Documentation). A `docs/decisions/` file records the model in the three sentences above.
- [x] **Stocktake page: count everything in one go.** Lutan, 2026-09-25. Stock on hand shipped in 0.5.0 (0083) as one editable cell per row on Management → Medications and → Diets (`updateMedicationStock` and its diet twin), so a stocktake of 100 medications means 100 separate edits. Add one count sheet, e.g. `/management/stocktake` with Medications and Diets tabs: every item in a list with its unit, the last count and when it was taken, and an input for today's count, plus search and a sort that follows how the cupboard is laid out if the shelter can tell us (name for now). **Save once:** one Save sends every changed row in a single server call that writes them in one transaction (an RPC), so a stocktake is all-or-nothing and every row gets the same counted-at time. Show a summary of what changed before saving (old → new, and the big differences highlighted). **Blank vs unchanged:** in today's cell a blank clears the count back to "not counted" and re-saving the same figure confirms it (0083). On the sheet, a blank input must mean "not counted this time, leave it alone", with a per-row "same as last time" tick to confirm an unchanged figure. Don't let the two meanings mix. **For phones:** staff will do this walking the shelves, so numeric keypad, Enter moves to the next row, big tap targets, and a warning before leaving with unsaved counts. Same roles as today's stock edit. Link it from both Management tables, and later from the "stocktake every Monday" recurring job (Resident operations) as its screen. Manual: the stock section of the Management topic in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts`. **Done 2026-09-26** at `/stocktake` rather than under `/management`: Lutan widened it to staff and volunteers, so `0091` made `record_stocktake` security definer and the page sits with the daily pages in the menu. Name order still; the cupboard order is a follow-up.
- [x] **Actual usage from stocktakes, compared with planned usage.** [done 2026-09-26 as count-to-count change against the plan, not "actual usage": count history is `0093`, the page is Management → Stock between counts (`src/app/management/stock-usage`). Stock received is **not** recorded, so part (2) — `stock_receipts` and "Record a delivery" — is split out as its own backlog item; CSV download and the percentage column were left for it, since both only mean something once receipts exist. See docs/decisions.md 2026-09-26.] Lutan, 2026-09-26. Work out what was really used of each medication and diet between two stocktakes, and set it beside what the prescriptions and diets said would be used over the same dates, so big gaps stand out (waste, spillage, doses not given, theft, or a forecast that is simply wrong). **The gap in today's data:** `stock_on_hand` (0083) is one figure per item, overwritten at every count, so the previous count is gone. The formula `actual used = previous count + stock received − new count` needs two things that are not recorded: (1) **count history**, a `stock_counts` row per item per stocktake (item kind medication/diet, item id, counted quantity in the item's unit, `counted_at`, `counted_by`, stocktake id so one session's rows group together), written by the stocktake save call. `0088_record_stocktake.sql` has merged without it, and the stocktake sheet is in review (#143, with schema PR #140 for 0091). So add the table in the next schema PR: fold it into #140 if that is still open, otherwise its own small PR. Then have the stocktake save call write the history rows, so every stocktake from then on counts. (2) **stock received**, because without deliveries every restock looks like negative usage: a `stock_receipts` row (item, quantity, received on, supplier contact optional, cost optional, note), entered from a "Record a delivery" action on Medications / Diets. It later meets the "order and buy medication" recurring job and the Cashflow actuals follow-up. **Planned usage** already exists: `medication_forecast(from, to)` (0044) and `diet_forecast(from, to)` (0051) over the dates between the two counts, living residents only. **Page:** Management → Usage (or a tab on Medications / Diets). Pick two stocktakes (default: the last two). One row per item: planned, received, actual, difference, and difference as a percentage. Rows over a threshold are flagged (e.g. ±20% and more than a minimum quantity, so tiny items don't shout). Sort by the biggest gap, with a link to the item and a CSV download like Cashflow's. **Cautions to show on the page:** an item counted in only one of the two stocktakes has no result (say so, don't show zero). "As needed" prescriptions and per-resident diet overrides make the plan approximate. A resident who arrived, left or died mid-period is already handled by the forecast functions, but check it. Receipts that were forgotten will look like overuse, so the first line of any big gap should say "check deliveries were recorded". **Depends on** the Stocktake page (Medical records) and should follow it in the plan. Manual topic, both dictionaries, and a `docs/decisions.md` entry for the formula and the threshold.
- [x] **Record stock deliveries, so Stock between counts can state usage.** [done 2026-09-27 as `claude/stock-deliveries`: /deliveries, and Stock between counts shows received and used from `stock_count_intervals`; the CSV / percentage / floor extras split out as their own item] Split out 2026-09-26 from "Actual usage from stocktakes" (done as `claude/stock-usage`). Management → Stock between counts shows count-to-count change beside the plan, but cannot say what was used: `used = previous count + received − new count` and nothing records **received**. Add `stock_receipts` (item kind + medication_id / diet_type_id as `stock_counts` does, quantity in the item unit, unit at receipt, received_on, supplier contact optional, cost optional, note, recorded_by), entered from a "Record a delivery" action on Management → Medications / Diets (and perhaps the Stocktake sheet). Schema PR first. Then the page can show received and actual used, and its rise / "less than planned" wording changes: a rise stops being "a delivery nobody logged" once deliveries are logged (though forgotten ones still look like overuse — keep a "check deliveries were recorded" line on big gaps). Carried over from the original item: a CSV download like Cashflow, a difference-as-percentage column, and a minimum-quantity floor so tiny items do not shout. Related: the plan reads low for residents who have since left (forecasts filter by status today) — a forecast over placement history would fix that; see docs/decisions.md 2026-09-26. Later meets the "order and buy medication" recurring job and Cashflow actuals.
- [x] **Stock between counts: CSV download, difference as a percentage, and a floor so tiny items do not shout.** [done 2026-09-27 as `claude/stock-counts-followups`: a Difference column with the percentage (a dash when nothing was planned), Download CSV through Cashflow's writer moved to `src/lib/csv.ts`, the floor as what two counts can get wrong per item (one of a counted unit, 5% of the stock for one read by eye), and a Before / After tag on the recent-deliveries list. See docs/decisions.md 2026-09-27.] Split out 2026-09-27 from "Record stock deliveries" (done as `claude/stock-deliveries`), which carried these over from the original usage item. Now that the page shows *used* from recorded deliveries: a CSV of the table like Cashflow's, the used-vs-planned gap as a percentage beside the quantity, and a way to stop tiny items being marked — decisions.md 2026-09-26 explains why a single absolute floor does not fit (units differ per item), so this is probably per-unit or per-item, or a minimum on the planned figure. Also worth a look while there: the recent-deliveries list shows two deliveries recorded before and after a same-day stocktake at the same minute, so it cannot show which side of the count each fell — a "before / after the stocktake" tag would.
- [ ] **Medication label photos, a card-by-card stocktake on phones, and Stocktake only for the people who do it.** Lutan, 2026-09-27. Three parts, one theme: make the count quicker and harder to get wrong. **(1) Label photo on medication setup.** On Management → Medications each medication gets an "Upload label" action (take a photo on a phone or pick a file; replace; remove), and the photo shows as a thumbnail in the table and on the stocktake. It is there so a person holding a box can match it at a glance, when counting and when a delivery comes in (the "Record a delivery" form from the Stock deliveries item should show it too). Store it on Drive like the other uploads (Shelter Friend logos, resident photos), as a `label_drive_file_id` on `medication`, served through the existing image route and resized for mobile. Schema PR first (next number is one past the highest on `main` — check open `*-schema` PRs before claiming it). Worth deciding in the same PR whether `diet_types` gets one too, so the Diets tab can use the same card later; the schema costs one column. Return `{ok, error}` from the upload action rather than throwing (the #441 lesson), with the size limit and file type checked up front. **(2) Card-by-card medication stocktake on phones.** On a narrow screen the Medications tab of `/stocktake` becomes one card at a time, like a dating app: the label photo large at the top (a placeholder with the name when there is none), and below it the name, strength/form, unit, the last count and when it was taken. Under that a big numeric input with the keypad already open, a **Save** button, and **Same as last time** and **Skip** as secondary actions. Saving moves the card on to the next medication with a short slide; a progress line says "23 of 100", and there is a way back to the previous card and to the list. Swipe gestures are a nice extra but buttons must do everything (one-handed use, accessibility). The desktop sheet stays as it is. **Saving:** the sheet today saves once at the end through `record_stocktake()` (0088, 0091, history in 0093), all-or-nothing. On the cards a phone could lose a half-done count, so decide between saving each card as it is entered (one stocktake id shared across the session so `stock_counts` rows still group as one stocktake) and keeping the counts on the device and sending them together at the end with the summary; record the choice in `docs/decisions.md`. Either way keep the sheet's rule that blank means "not counted this time", and that "same as last time" is an explicit confirmation, not an empty save. Skipped cards come round again at the end ("5 skipped — count them now?") before the summary. **(3) Stocktake in the menu only for the people who do it.** Today `canStocktake(role)` (`src/lib/management/stocktake.ts`, `STOCKTAKE_ROLES` = admin, management, staff, volunteer) shows Stocktake to everyone. Change it to admin and management always, plus any staff member or volunteer who is an assignee of an active recurring job whose `link_path` starts with `/stocktake` — on the job itself (`recurring_job_assignees`) or through a one-date hand-over (`recurring_job_occurrence_assignees`, so someone covering a sick week gets it for that week). Apply the same check in three places, not only the menu: `NavLinks.tsx`, the page guard in `src/app/stocktake/page.tsx`, and the save (`record_stocktake()` is security definer since 0091, so it must check this itself, which probably means a small SQL function such as `can_stocktake(uid)` in the schema PR). Decide whether a hand-over grants access only on its date or for the week around it, and whether a team assignee counts every member. Test with a staff login that is not on the job (no menu entry, the URL redirects, a direct save is refused) and one that is. Manual: the stocktake section in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` / `th.ts` (who can see it, the phone cards, how to add a label photo), both dictionaries, and a line in `src/lib/releases.ts`. Related: "Stocktake in cupboard order" (Management) — the cards should follow that order once it exists. **Status 2026-10-02: schema half of part (1) only, as `claude/schema-medication-label` (0129; see `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-medication-label-column.md`):** `label_drive_file_id text` (nullable) on **both** `medication` and `diet_types`; nothing reads the `diet_types` one yet. The feature half must add `medication` to the photo proxy's table list in `src/app/api/photos/[fileId]/route.ts`. Parts (2) and (3) and the UI are untouched; item stays open.
  **Status 2026-10-02 (feature half of part 1, `claude/medication-label-feature`):** Upload / Replace / Remove label on Management → Medications, with a thumbnail there, on the stocktake sheet and on the Record a delivery form; `medication` added to the photo proxy; the Diets tab is not wired (see `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-medication-label-photo.md`). Parts (2) and (3) remain; item stays open.
  **Status 2026-10-03 (part 2, `claude/stocktake-cards-phone`, PR #331):** the card-by-card medication stocktake on phones is built — one card at a time under 640 px with the label photo, keypad open, Save / Same as last time / Skip, "N of M", Previous and a way to the list, skipped cards offered again at the end, and the count kept on the phone between saves (see `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-stocktake-cards-phone.md`). Swipe is not built; buttons do everything. Part (3), Stocktake in the menu only for the people who do it, remains; item stays open.
- [x] **Units of measure: buy and count in one unit, feed or dose in another, with a conversion per item.** [done 2026-10-01 as `claude/units-of-measure-feature`: Units panel on Management → Medications / Diets, units on Record a delivery and the stocktake with the factor stamped server-side into `entered`, stock shown in the purchase unit, price per purchase unit; see docs/decisions/2026-10-01-units-of-measure-feature.md. The 2-decimal cost column refuses sub-1% precision, e.g. per-gram prices: follow-up on the backlog branch] From the backlog page's suggestion box (2026-09-26), confirmed by Lutan 2026-09-29. Dry kibble is bought in 20 kg bags but given to dogs by the cup; medicines arrive by the box or bottle but are given by the tablet or ml. **Today each item has exactly one unit**, used for everything: `diet_types.unit` (0051: "daily_qty_* and cost_per_unit are in this unit") and `medication.dose_unit` (0027). Stock on hand (0083), stock counts (0093) and deliveries (`stock_receipts`, 0096, which records the unit at receipt and expects "2 boxes of 50" to be converted by hand before saving) all use that same unit. So staff either count kibble in cups or feed in kilograms, and the forecast, stock and cost figures are only as right as that mental arithmetic. **Shape:** keep the item's existing unit as its **base unit** (the one feeding or dosing is recorded in, and what the forecasts already compute), and add a small per-item **conversion table**: other units it is bought, received or counted in, each with how many base units one of it holds (e.g. kibble: base `cup`; `kg` = 10 cups, `bag (20 kg)` = 200 cups; a tablet medicine: `box` = 50 tablets, `strip` = 10). Per item, not global, because a cup of one kibble weighs differently from another. Mark one conversion as the **purchase unit** and one as the **count unit** (can be the same) so forms default sensibly. **Where it is used:** "Record a delivery" takes a quantity in any of the item's units and stores both what was entered (quantity + unit) and the base-unit equivalent. The stocktake sheet (and the phone cards in the item above) counts in the count unit, e.g. "3 bags and 4 kg" if mixed entry is worth it, and saves base units through `record_stocktake()`. Stock on hand and days-of-stock can be shown in the purchase unit ("2.4 bags, about 11 days"). Cost moves to a **price per purchase unit** (what the receipt says: 850 baht a bag), with cost per base unit derived for the diet and medication forecasts and Cashflow. Doses, prescriptions, diets and forecasts stay in the base unit, so nothing there changes. **History must not shift:** existing counts and receipts are in today's single unit, which becomes the base unit, so they need no rewrite. Editing a conversion factor later must not silently re-value past rows, so each row keeps the base-unit amount it was saved with (0096 already snapshots the unit for the same reason). **Schema PR first** (a conversions table keyed on medication or diet type, like `stock_counts`' item-kind pair, plus the entered quantity/unit columns on `stock_receipts` and `stock_counts`); admin and management edit conversions on Management → Medications / Diets. Decide while building: whether a conversion factor can be approximate (a cup of kibble) and whether the page should say so; mixed-unit entry on the stocktake or one unit per line; and whether this settles the related open item "A single-cell stock edit writes no history". Check Stock between counts and Cashflow arithmetic end to end with a real kibble example. Manual (Medications, Diets, Stocktake, Deliveries topics), both dictionaries, a releases line and a `docs/decisions.md` entry for the base-unit rule. **Schema half done 2026-09-30 as `claude/schema-units-of-measure` (0118; see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-units-of-measure-schema.md`) — the feature half builds against this:** table `item_unit_conversions` (`item_kind` + `medication_id` / `diet_type_id` as in `stock_counts`; `unit`, `base_units_per` > 0, `is_purchase_unit`, `is_count_unit` with at most one of each per item, `note`; unique per item by lower-cased unit; admin and management write, admin / management / staff / volunteer read). The base unit is the item's existing `dose_unit` / `unit`, unchanged. **The point-in-time factor** is `entered jsonb` on both `stock_receipts` and `stock_counts`: `[{"quantity","unit","factor"}]`, a copy of the factors in force when the row was saved, so editing a conversion never re-values history; `quantity` / `counted_quantity` stay the base-unit total and a CHECK requires sum(quantity × factor) to equal it. Null `entered` = entered in the base unit (all existing rows). `record_stocktake(jsonb, jsonb)` keeps its signature and now accepts an optional `entered` per element beside `id` and `count` (count in base units). Deliveries are written straight to `stock_receipts`: set `quantity` (base) and `entered` together. No cost column: price per purchase unit ÷ `base_units_per`. Still to do in the feature half: the forms and pages, refusing a conversion named like the base unit, stamping `factor` from the live conversion at save time, and the open decisions above.
- [x] **Stock on hand and days-of-stock.** Neither medications nor diets record what's actually in the cupboard, so the forecasts say what will be used but not when it runs out. If the shelter wants that: a `stock_on_hand` (and maybe `reorder_at`) figure on `medication` and `diet_types`, edited in place on the two management pages, and a "days of stock" column computed from the 30-day rate — with a flag when it's inside the reorder lead time. Split out of the medication requirement page item when that closed (2026-09-21).

- [x] **Doctors belong to a vet: investigate a managed list rather than free text.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, during Pass 0 of the role walkthrough. A clinic has many doctors — Novel has around 20 on staff — and the concern raised was that the relationship is modelled the wrong way round. It is not: `doctor_name` sits on `vet_appointments`, not on `vets`, so one vet already has many doctors. What is missing is anywhere to **manage** them. The column is free text by Lutan's own call on 2026-09-24 (`0074`, `docs/decisions.md`), with the cost written down at the time — "Dr Somchai" and "Somchai" stay two strings, so counting visits per doctor means grouping by hand — and that migration closed with "Promoting it to a lookup is its own item if that is ever wanted". This is that item. Today the only affordance is `loadDoctorNamesByVet` (`src/lib/vets/doctors.ts`), which harvests the distinct names already typed against each vet and offers them as a `datalist` on the vet-visit form. That stops a name being typed three ways, but a doctor nobody has recorded yet is not suggested, a typo becomes a permanent suggestion, and there is no screen where a clinic's roster can be seen or corrected. **To investigate:** whether a `vet_doctors` table (vet_id, name, active) with a nullable `vet_appointments.doctor_id` beside the existing text column earns its keep; how the names already recorded would be reconciled into it; and whether the shelter will actually maintain a 20-name roster per clinic or would rather keep typing with better suggestions. Schema-first as its own PR if it goes ahead, and the free-text column stays for historical rows either way. Note for whoever picks this up: the walkthrough's Pass 0 line "Management → Vets has at least one vet, and at least one has a doctor name" was wrong — the vet record has no doctor field — and has been corrected in `docs/role-walkthrough.md`. **Decided 2026-09-27 (Lutan): build both.** Schema half is #176 (`0102_vet_doctors_and_vet_accounts.sql`, applied to dev): a `vet_doctors` list per clinic that fills itself from typed names by trigger, `doctor_name` kept and following the list, `merge_vet_doctors`, `p_doctor_name` on `schedule_bulk_appointments`, and `user_roles.vet_id` + `current_user_vet_id()` for the vet-account→clinic link items 4 and 8 need. **Feature half still to do:** the list on the clinic hub (view, rename, deactivate, merge), the booking form passing `p_doctor_name` instead of its second write, suggestions from the list instead of `loadDoctorNamesByVet`, choosing a vet account's clinic in `/admin/security`, manual and release-notes line; tick this item then. **Done 2026-09-28** (`claude/vet-doctors-roster-screen`): Management → Vets → Doctors lists each clinic's doctors with their visit counts, and renames (saying how many visits the new spelling reaches), merges (pointing out look-alike spellings such as "Somchai" / "Dr Somchai"), marks a doctor as left, and adds or deletes an unused one; the clinic hub shows the list read-only; booking passes `p_doctor_name` in the one RPC call; the visit forms suggest the clinic's active doctors. The `/admin/security` clinic choice had already shipped with the vet-scope work.

- [x] **A single-cell stock edit writes no history, but the `0093` back-fill treated past ones as counts.** [done 2026-09-29 as `claude/stock-edit-history`, migration `0112`: Lutan chose one way in — `record_stock_correction()` writes a `source = correction` row, kept out of usage intervals; see docs/decisions.md 2026-09-29] Found 2026-09-27 while clearing UAT's stock figures. `record_stocktake()` writes a `stock_counts` row per item (`0093`), and the stocktake sheet goes through it. `updateMedicationStock` and its diet twin (`src/app/management/medications/actions.ts`) do not: they `update` the column directly, so `0083`'s trigger stamps `stock_counted_at` and nothing lands in the history. The result is that **Stock between counts cannot see a figure typed on the Management page** — four such edits on UAT this morning left no trace in `stock_counts` at all. What makes it more than a design choice is that `0093`'s back-fill *did* capture exactly those edits, one synthetic stocktake each, because all it had to go on was `stock_on_hand` and `stock_counted_at`. So a cell edit made before `0093` is history and the same edit made after it is not, which is the kind of split nobody will reconstruct later from the data. **To decide:** either route the single-cell edit through `record_stocktake()` so there is one way in, or leave it out of history on purpose and say so on the page — and either way `0093`'s comment should stop implying the two agree. Worth settling before the first real count, since that is when the history starts being worth something. **The display half is now done too (2026-10-01, `claude/stock-count-source`):** Lutan chose Stock between counts only, so a row there says when a figure was typed by hand during the dates being compared, in the table and in the CSV. No figure changes — a correction is still never paired into an interval. Labelling back-fill rows so they stop passing as real stocktakes was considered and declined for now, and is noted as still open in `docs/decisions/2026-10-01-corrections-on-stock-between-counts.md`.

- [x] **A vet's photo upload should be limited to the Medical folder.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. **Done 2026-09-27** (`claude/medical-photos-profile`): no picker for a vet, and the upload route answers 403 to any other folder. Confirmed a vet can add photos from the resident hub, which is intended (`assertPhotoWriteAccess` allows every app role), but the folder picker offers all of them. A vet has no reason to file into the shelter's general or project folders, and a misfiled clinical photo is awkward to find later. Restrict the choice to **Medical** for the vet role — ideally by not showing the picker at all when there is only one valid destination, so it reads as "this goes in Medical" rather than a one-item dropdown. Enforce it on the server too, not only in the form: the upload route is the thing that has to refuse.
