A small schema PR: revoke from anon (and probably authenticated: the app no longer calls it), and move it out of `check-public-views.mjs`'s allow-list in the same PR, or that check fails. `check-shelter-friends.mjs` and `check-public-drive-file.mjs` call it as the owner and should be unaffected. **Only after the route change is deployed to production**: until then production's proxy still calls it as anon, and revoking would break every public photo.

- [ ] **Roles and permissions each shelter configures: a matrix of activities × roles in Settings, and roles defined at setup — design first.** Lutan, 2026-10-03, thinking about multi-tenancy. Shelters are organised differently: one has a staff member who handles purchasing, another leaves that to Management. Today's roles are a fixed enum (`admin`, `management`, `staff`, `vet`, `volunteer`, `public_viewer`) with their rights hard-coded in about a dozen app predicates (`isShelterRole`, `canManage`, `canStocktake`, `canRecordDelivery`, `canArchiveMedical`, `canWriteMaintenance`, `canWriteProjects`, `canDoJob`, `canUseAssistant`, …) and in RLS across roughly 26 migrations. That cannot vary per shelter. **What is wanted:** (1) **A catalogue of activities with no grey areas.** Every action in the system is one named activity, each set per role to **Edit / Read / None** (or **Yes / No** where reading makes no sense, e.g. "record a death"). Where one screen hides two different powers (create vs delete, record vs approve, see a contact's name vs their phone), split it into two activities rather than adding levels, so every cell means exactly one thing. (2) **A matrix in Settings:** activities down the side, the shelter's roles across the top, admin-editable. (3) **Roles defined per shelter at setup:** a shelter can add a role ("Purchasing", "Senior Staff", "Kennel lead") and set its column, or keep the defaults. **Design points to settle in the paper:** (a) **Defaults reproduce today exactly.** Ship the six current roles as the starting template, filled in so that switching to the matrix changes nothing for Lanna, proved by a check that compares each default cell with today's behaviour. (b) **Some things are not configurable.** Admin always has everything and cannot be edited away; no change may leave a shelter with no one able to edit the matrix; Settings → Security keeps its 2-step rule; `public_viewer` and signed-out visitors stay fixed. (c) **Data scope is separate from capability.** A vet seeing only their clinic's residents (0108) is a *scope*, not a cell, so model scopes apart (or as a small fixed set of scope options per role) rather than forcing them into Edit/Read/None. (d) **Enforcement must be in the database, not only the menu.** One SQL function such as `has_permission('stock.delivery', 'edit')` used by every policy and `security definer` guard, and one `can()` in TypeScript replacing the predicates, both reading the same tables. Decide how it stays fast under RLS (a `stable` function over a small cached table, or the role's permissions placed in the JWT by Supabase's custom access token hook, which means a change applies at the next token refresh — say so in the UI). (e) **Everything that keys on a role name today** has to move to activities: the manual's and `/releases`' per-role filtering (`isForRole`, topic `roles`), recurring-job eligibility, the assistant, nav and route guards, the role walkthrough. (f) **Changes are audited** (who changed which cell, when) through `audit_log` (0121), and the matrix can be exported, since it is also what a shelter's acceptance sign-off is checked against. **Define the catalogue once:** the role-by-activity acceptance matrix (Documentation) needs the same list of activities, so it should be generated from this catalogue rather than kept as a second list. **Where it sits:** it is part of the multi-shelter work (the spike just below): `roles` and the matrix are per `shelter_id` once tenancy lands, but the catalogue and enforcement can be built for one shelter first, and it is cheaper before seven shelters' data exists than after. **The Senior Staff item (Auth) should be decided against this:** if the matrix is coming soon, Senior Staff is better as the first shelter-defined role than as a new enum value that later has to be migrated out. Settle the order with Lutan before either is built. **Deliverable:** `docs/roles-and-permissions.md` with the full activity catalogue (every page and action, from the manual's topics and the current predicates and policies), the default matrix, the non-configurable rules, the data model (`roles`, `role_permissions`, `user_roles.role_id`, tenancy columns), the enforcement and caching choice and a migration path from the enum. Agree it with Lutan, record it in `docs/decisions/`, then schema PRs and the build in stages: catalogue and enforcement first, behaviour unchanged; then the Settings matrix; then custom roles.
- [x] **Recurring-job eligibility asks about another person's role, which `can()` cannot answer: add `role_can(role_key, activity, level)` (schema), then convert `canDoJob`.** Found by `permissions-catalogue`, 2026-10-03. `src/lib/recurring-jobs/eligibility.ts` decides whether an *assignee* can do a job that links to `/stocktake` or `/deliveries`; `can()` reads the caller's own cells and `role_permissions` is admin-read-only (`0132`), so those two rules keep `STOCK_COUNT_ROLES` / `STOCK_DELIVERY_ROLES`, equal to the seed and held there by `scripts/check-permission-catalogue.mjs`. A `security definer` function that answers for any role (readable by Management, who set up rotas), an RPC the form and `my-tasks` can call, and `canDoJob` taking its answer instead of a role string would remove the last stock role list and is needed before any sweep touches `eligibility.ts` (its `/management`, `/admin` and `/maintenance` rules are the same shape). Two units: the function is a schema PR; the app side follows it. See `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-permissions-catalogue.md`.
  **Status 2026-10-03 (`claude/schema-role-can`): the function is in (`0133_role_can.sql`, applied to dev, `scripts/check-role-can.mjs`), not ticked.** Callable by a role holding `recurring.manage` (so Management and Admin), by anyone about their own role, and by the service role; anyone else is refused. Left: the app half, `role-can-app` (RPC wiring, `canDoJob`, the form picker), with or before the first sweep that touches `eligibility.ts`. See `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-role-can.md`.
  **Done 2026-10-04 (`claude/role-can-app`): the app half.** `canDoJob` takes an `Eligibility` built from `role_can()` by `loadEligibility`; the form, the save check, the stranded warning and My tasks read it; `STOCK_COUNT_ROLES` / `STOCK_DELIVERY_ROLES` are gone and the stock rules come from the route registry. Both units are in, nothing else in the item remains. The shape the other three rules copy is in `docs/decisions/2026-10-04-role-can-app.md`.
  **Status 2026-10-03 (`claude/roles-and-permissions-design`, PR #323): `docs/roles-and-permissions.md` is written; the design is proposed and not agreed; not ticked.** It has every part the deliverable lists: the catalogue (55 activities, seeded from the acceptance matrix's 93 rows and audited against the 243 policies, 15 functions and 10 views that name a role on dev, and every page guard), the default cells for today's roles, the rules nobody can configure, scopes kept apart from cells (now including "who and where" for a resident), the data model with its tenancy column, the enforcement choice (a live `has_permission()` lookup, not the token hook, because archiving a person must stay immediate), and Lutan's page-per-role steer answered (single pages with access or no access, keyed to a task rather than a role name). **The migration from the enum is role by role, as Lutan asked on 2026-10-03** (§12): a foundation, then the volunteer narrowed, then each new role borrowing that narrowest set of rights and granted only its own job through `has_permission()`, then today's roles converted, then the enum dropped, then the Settings matrix and roles a shelter adds. **The parity check is specified, not built** (§11): it needs the catalogue file and tables that belong to the next stream. **The audit found** eight powers the 93 rows do not describe, one the manual promises and the system refuses, and twelve groups of cases where the database allows more than any screen. **Decided by Lutan, 2026-10-03: configured roles** (`docs/decisions/2026-10-03-configured-roles-not-enum-values.md`), so this item's matrix goes ahead in its stages: enforcement first, then the Settings screen, then roles a shelter adds. **Asks of Lutan:** L8 (default cells are what the screens offer today, and the wider database grants are closed as tables are converted), L9 (live lookup), L10 (`0132`), L12 (the order of the roles). **No decision file records the remaining details** (L8, L9, L12), because nobody has agreed them; the fork and Lutan's answers on who does what at Lanna each have their own. Pieces for `/plan-day` are in §15.
- [ ] **Spike: can the app hold several shelters in one database? Measure it, do not estimate it.** Lutan, 2026-09-28: seven shelters have shown interest — five in Chiang Mai, two elsewhere in Thailand — and the system would be **a gift to all of them**, so the ongoing cost must be as close to zero as possible (Lutan carries his own time and Claude costs, the shelters carry their own domains). **That constraint decides the architecture.** A silo (one Supabase project per shelter) cannot meet it: the free tier allows two projects per organisation and pauses a project after a week idle, so seven shelters means seven paid projects, forever, for a gift. Pooling — one database, a `shelter_id` on every table — keeps the marginal cost of each shelter at zero, because it adds no new services: the same two projects, the same Worker, the same Pi origin, the same weekly backup. **So the decision is effectively made; what is not known is the cost of getting there, and that is what this item buys.** Do it on a throwaway branch and do not merge it: add `shelter_id`, a `current_user_shelter_id()` helper (shaped like `current_user_vet_id()` from `0102`), and tenancy as **restrictive** policies — the pattern `0100` already uses for the `aal2` check on `user_roles`, where an `as restrictive` policy is AND-ed with every existing permissive one. That means roughly **one restrictive policy per table (~45), not a rewrite of all 181**. Then push it through the parts restrictive policies do not save you: **53 `security definer` functions**, which bypass RLS by design and each need their own tenant check, and **58 views**. Report a real number of hours and a list of the places that fought back. **Design the spike around the anonymous tier specifically** — the `public_*` views are served to signed-out visitors, so a tenancy miss there publishes another shelter's animals to the internet. That exact class of miss has already happened twice in this codebase at a smaller scale: `0101` closed the public gallery and the photo proxy and profile photos still leaked through three other views until `0103`, and #182 gated five route trees while a vet could still write the `vets` table through the API until `0105`. **Not before Lanna is live** — go-live is still gated on the Pi origin, the domain cutover and the AppSheet cutover, and one running reference shelter is what turns seven interested into seven committed. But **pre-production is the cheapest this will ever be**: adding a tenant column when one shelter has data is far cheaper than when seven do. Record the outcome in `docs/decisions.md` whichever way it goes. Note what is being given up: one database means one blast radius, so a bad migration takes all seven down at once where a silo could roll shelter by shelter. **The product needs its own name and domain (Lutan, 2026-09-28).** Once more than one shelter uses it, the app is no longer Lanna's, so it should not live on `lannacare.org` — that stays Lanna's public site, and each shelter keeps its own domain for its own public pages. Choose a name for the application itself and register a matching **`.org`** to host the staff app on (e.g. `app.<name>.org`, with the shelters' domains or subdomains pointing at the same Worker). **The whole project is in honour of Cooper, Lutan's dog who died recently**, so the name should carry him — shortlist a few Cooper-based `.org` names, check which are free (Cloudflare Registrar, where `lannacare.org` already is, sells at cost), and let Lutan choose. *Shortlist checked 2026-09-28 against the .org registry (RDAP), all unregistered that day: cooperscare.org, coopercare.org, coopershaven.org, cooperskeeper.org, coopersheart.org, coopersden.org, cooperspaws.org, friendsofcooper.org, cooperslegacy.org, cooperscircle.org. Already taken: coopercares, coopersfriends, coopersrescue, coopersplace, teamcooper. Re-check at checkout; a registry premium price shows only there.* Register it through Cloudflare so DNS, Email Routing and the Worker routes sit alongside the existing domain. Decide the name before the tenancy build, because it goes into the Worker routes, auth redirect URLs (Supabase and Google OAuth), release mail's sender and the manual.
- [ ] **Per-shelter Drive: each shelter keeps its photos in its own Google account.** From the seven-shelter discussion, Lutan 2026-09-28. Do **not** pool the photos even if the database is pooled. Each shelter uses their own Google account — 15GB free each — so storage cost never lands on Lutan and never grows without bound, and the photos live in an account the shelter owns, which matters if they ever leave or the arrangement ends. That makes Drive **per-shelter configuration, not per-shelter infrastructure**: a folder id and OAuth credentials keyed by `shelter_id` rather than the single set of env vars `src/lib/google/drive.ts` reads today. **The risk this creates is the one that has already bitten twice, multiplied by seven.** A Drive refresh token dying breaks that shelter's uploads silently until someone tries to add a photo: on 2026-09-25 the `lanna-care-dev` consent screen was still in *Testing*, so the token expired after 7 days and every upload on lannacare.org failed; a 90-day expiry is set somewhere and still unlocated (see Deployment). With seven shelters this stops being an incident and becomes a recurring support load. **Most of the fix already exists** — `scripts/check-drive-token.mjs`, the Drive-connected indicator on `/admin`, and the status-alert mailer from `0098` (#174). Extend the alerting to check each shelter's token and mail before a shelter notices, and treat that as a **prerequisite of onboarding shelter #2, not a follow-up**. Also decide where per-shelter OAuth credentials live: Worker secrets do not scale to seven, so most likely the database, and then how they are protected.
- [ ] **Ask the Chiang Mai five whether they want one regional adoption listing or five separate sites.** From the seven-shelter discussion, Lutan 2026-09-28. This is a product question that decides architecture more firmly than hosting cost does, and it is cheap to ask now and expensive to discover later. Five of the seven interested shelters are in Chiang Mai. Either they want **five separate sites** — each its own brand, adoption page and donors — or they want **one regional network**: a combined "dogs available in Chiang Mai" listing, Shelter Friends shared across shelters, and vet clinics entered once rather than five times (they will largely use the same clinics). The second is the stronger mission outcome — more animals seen by more people — and under a pooled database it is **nearly free to build**, where under a silo it would need a data warehouse on top. If even two of the five want the combined listing, pooling wins outright and the spike above becomes urgent rather than exploratory. Ask before the spike reports, so its answer can shape what the spike measures. Record what they say in `docs/decisions.md`.

- [x] **Public website photos load slowly: serve sized copies, cached on the Pi, not the originals.** Lutan, 2026-10-01. **Done 2026-10-01** by `claude/public-photo-sizing` (decisions/2026-10-01-public-photo-sizing.md): widths 160/400/1200, Pi disk cache capped at 1 GiB; the phone-from-Thailand timing is Lutan's. Seen on project pages (`/our-work`, `/our-work/[id]`); **scope is every public-facing image** (Lutan, same day): project cards and galleries, resident photos on `/adopt`, `/adopt/[id]`, `/r/` and the home page cards, pet of the week, the hero and site gallery, recent adoptions, adoption updates shown publicly, and Shelter Friend logos. Staff-only photos inside the app stay as they are for now. **Likely cause, from the code:** every image is the full-size original. `driveImageUrl(fileId)` (`src/lib/google/drive-client.ts`) is just `/api/photos/<fileId>` with no size, the photo route streams Drive's `alt=media` bytes as uploaded, and `next.config.ts` sets `images.unoptimized: true`, so `next/image` does no resizing either. A project card (`ProjectCard.tsx`), the story gallery's main image and **every thumbnail in its strip** (`StoryGallery.tsx` → `ThumbnailStrip`) each download a phone-camera original, often 3–10 MB, so a story with twenty photos can be over 100 MB before anyone taps anything. Every other public image uses the same route, so the same fix covers them all. **Measure first** (a real phone, from Thailand): bytes and time for `/our-work` and one story, cold and warm, so the fix can be shown to work. **Fix:** let the photo route take a width (e.g. `/api/photos/<id>?w=400`, only from a small fixed set such as 160 / 400 / 1200 so the cache cannot be flooded with sizes), served from Drive's own rendition through the existing `drive.downloadThumbnail(fileId, size)` (`src/lib/google/drive.ts`, today used only by the deceased-archive PDF), which also turns phone HEIC into JPEG. Fall back to the original if Drive has no rendition yet. **Keep the sized copies on the Pi (Lutan, 2026-10-01):** now that the Pi serves the site, the route writes each generated size to a disk cache on its SSD (e.g. `<cache dir>/<fileId>/<width>.jpg`) and serves it from there next time, so Drive is asked for a given size once rather than on every edge-cache miss. Small files make the Pi's home upload a non-issue for thumbnails and cards. Rules: Cloudflare's edge cache stays in front (the Pi is the origin, not a replacement); only files `is_public_drive_file` approves are written to disk, so internal attachments never land there; the cache is disposable (no backup, safe to empty, size-capped with oldest-first eviction), and Drive stays the source of truth; when a photo is replaced or deleted in the app, its cached sizes are removed (or the cache key includes something that changes, such as Drive's `modifiedTime`). When the Worker is serving without the Pi (fallback), it skips the disk and goes to Drive as today. Cache each size under its own key with the same public/private rules the route already applies (`is_public_drive_file`, `Cache-Control`). Then give `driveImageUrl` a size argument and use it on every public page listed above: thumbnails ~160, cards ~400, the gallery's main image ~1200 with a `srcset`, and a "view original" only where someone actually wants it. Keep `loading="lazy"` off-screen and `priority` only on the first image. Check the edge and Pi caching still apply per size, and that a staff-only photo is still refused when signed out at every size. **This takes in most of the Pi spike below:** it answers the spike's question (2) and builds its pull-through cache, for sized copies. Afterwards the spike only needs to measure whether full-size originals are worth caching on the Pi too. A releases line (visitors and staff will notice faster pages). No schema.
- [x] **Spike: would serving the published photos from the Pi make them load faster? Measure before building.** **Subsumed 2026-10-01** by the sized-photos item above (sized copies + the Pi disk cache are built; no one should run this spike). Lutan, 2026-09-30, once the Pi was serving the site. Today `/api/photos/<fileId>` (`src/app/api/photos/[fileId]/route.ts`) fetches a public photo from Drive through the authenticated API and caches it 24 hours at Cloudflare's edge and in the browser; only a cold miss costs a Drive round trip. Under `next start` on the Pi there is no edge Cache API (the route feature-detects it), so the Worker's own caching is what covers repeat views. The idea is to hold public photos on the Pi's SSD so a miss never waits on Drive. **Questions the spike answers first:** (1) where the time goes today, on a cold photo and a warm one, from Thailand and from elsewhere: Drive fetch, the tunnel hop, or the image size itself (are photos resized, or served at full upload size?); (2) whether a smaller image or a resized variant fixes most of it without touching hosting. **The caution:** a photo served from the Pi leaves through the home connection's upload, which is likely slower than Cloudflare's edge, so the Pi should be the origin behind the edge cache and never a replacement for it. **If it goes ahead, keep the design small:** a disposable pull-through cache on the SSD (Drive stays the source of truth, nothing is uploaded to the Pi), only for files `is_public_drive_file` approves so internal attachments never land on disk, no backup of it, and safe to empty at any time. It should also fit the per-shelter Drive item above rather than assume one Drive. Success is a measured drop in photo load time on a real phone connection, not a Drive request count.
## Documentation

- [x] **A role-by-activity matrix that becomes the acceptance checklist the shelter signs.** [done 2026-10-02 as `claude/acceptance-matrix`: `node scripts/acceptance-matrix.mjs` generates it from the manual, the role walkthrough and the Admin-on-mobile decision (93 rows from 67 topics, 7 role columns, EN/TH × phone/desktop boxes per row, a sheet per role, failures list and sign-off lines) and `npm run lint` fails when a manual topic has no entry; the generated copy is not committed, the signed edition is; see `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-acceptance-matrix.md`. **Not built: the printable A4 PDF with cover page and Thai fonts, and the "agree who tests which role / blocker vs accepted" step before the first run** — follow-up filed on the `backlog` branch.] Lutan, 2026-10-02. **Purpose:** user acceptance testing where every feature is tried by a real user **in the role that uses it**, in **Thai and English**, on **phone and desktop** as each activity is really done, ending in one document the shelter signs to say the system does what they need. **Build it from what already exists, so it stays true as features land:** (a) the manual already lists every activity and which roles it is for (`src/lib/manual/en.ts`: 81 topics, 55 tagged with `roles`, the rest for everyone, read through `isForRole`), so **generate** the matrix from it with a script (e.g. `scripts/acceptance-matrix.mjs`) rather than typing it, and fail loudly when a topic has no matrix entry; (b) the role walkthrough (`docs/role-walkthrough.md`, Passes 0-6) supplies the "must **not** be able to" lines per role, which matter as much as the positive ones; (c) the "Admin on mobile" decision (`docs/decisions/2026-09-24-admin-on-mobile-which-settings-and-management-pages-belong.md`: field-needed / nice-to-have / desktop-only per screen) says which device each activity is meant for. The roles are the app's own: admin, management, staff, vet, volunteer, public viewer, and signed-out visitor for the public site. **Shape of the matrix:** one row per activity (e.g. "register a new resident", "record a vet visit", "count stock", "add a Shelter Friend"), one column per role marked **does** / **must not** / **n/a**, and for each "does" cell the cases to test: **language** (each activity once in the tester's working language, plus a full Thai pass and a full English pass across the whole matrix, so untranslated text and Thai layout problems are caught) and **device** (phone for field activities, desktop for setup and reporting, both where the decision says both). Each case has: what to do, the expected result in plain words, pass / fail, the tester's name and date, and a notes column for what they saw. Keep each case to one line a shelter worker can follow without a developer. **The sign-off document:** printable (a PDF generated from the same source, A4, Thai and English side by side or one edition each; the summary PDF's Thai fonts in `src/lib/archive/fonts/` already cover Thai), with a cover page (system version from `/releases`, environment tested on, dates), one section per role signed by the shelter person who tested it, a list of failures and how each was resolved or accepted, and a final sign-off line for the Director. Version it: a signed edition is kept as `docs/uat/acceptance-<date>.md` plus the PDF, and any later change to a signed activity starts a new edition rather than editing the old one. Distinct from `docs/test-plans/` (per feature, developer-facing) and `docs/release-smoke-test.md` (per release, ten minutes): this is per milestone, shelter-facing, and is what go-live waits on. **Before the first run:** agree with Lutan and the Director who tests which role (the walkthrough's two-account role-switching works for one tester; real UAT wants a real person per role where possible), give each tester an account in that role on the test or UAT site with disposable data, and decide what counts as a blocker versus an accepted issue. Ties in with the Thai edition of the manual (Documentation), since Thai testers will read it, and with the multi-shelter work, where a new shelter would sign the same checklist.
- [ ] **Acceptance checklist: the printable sign-off edition (A4 PDF) and the pre-run agreements.** Split out of the matrix item on 2026-10-02, when `node scripts/acceptance-matrix.mjs` shipped the generated Markdown checklist (`docs/decisions/2026-10-02-acceptance-matrix.md`). **Still to build:** a PDF made from the same generator output, A4, with the cover page, one section per role, the failures list and the Director's sign-off line, Thai fonts from `src/lib/archive/fonts/` (the manual PDF in `src/lib/manual/manual-pdf.tsx` is the pattern); Thai-language instructions once the Thai manual exists, so a Thai tester reads Thai steps. **Still to agree before the first real run, with Lutan and the Director:** who tests which role (a real person per role where possible), an account in each role on the test or UAT site with disposable data, and what counts as a blocker versus an accepted issue. Not needed by `fable-dry-run`, which walks the Markdown.
- [ ] **Fable dry run of the acceptance checklist: functionality and ease of use, ending in a report.** Lutan, 2026-10-02. **Depends on** the acceptance matrix (the item just above): run it once the matrix exists, before real shelter users do, so they meet fewer problems and their time goes on judging the system rather than tripping over bugs. **Who and where:** a Claude session on the Fable model (`claude-fable-5-1`, as the architecture review item does), driving the app in the browser on **`test.lannacare.org` or a local dev server only** (dev database, disposable data; never production), signing in as each role in turn with test accounts (the walkthrough's role-switching mechanic: one admin account changes a test account's role between passes, then a hard refresh). Best done after test is served from the Pi (Deployment), since 1102 errors on the Worker would make the run report the platform rather than the app. **Two lenses on every checklist line:** (1) **Functionality:** did the activity work as the expected result says, did each "must not" stay refused (and refused politely, inside the app), did the data persist after a reload, in Thai and English, on a phone width and on desktop. Pass / fail with what happened. (2) **Ease of use:** could a non-technical shelter worker do it without help: how many steps and screens it took, whether they had to know where to look first (the Shelter Friend setup is the known example), whether labels and messages are plain (in both languages), whether the next step was obvious, whether anything on a phone was too small to tap or needed sideways scrolling, and whether errors said what to do. Rate each on a short scale (e.g. easy / some friction / hard) with one sentence of why. **The report:** `docs/uat/dry-run-<date>.md` (under `docs/uat/`, not `docs/test-plans/`), with an executive summary Lutan and the Director can read in five minutes, the matrix with each line's functionality result and ease-of-use rating, screenshots of every failure and every "hard" rating, and a **findings table** ranked by severity (blocks a role / wrong result / friction / polish), each finding written as a ready-to-paste backlog item (title, where, what happened, suggested fix). It also notes lines the run could not test (needs a real phone, real scanner, real Thai reader) so the shelter's run covers them. **After it:** Lutan reviews the report; the findings he accepts go onto the `backlog` branch as items under their headings (not raised automatically), and the matrix is corrected wherever the dry run shows an expected result was wrong. **The dry run never signs anything:** it is a rehearsal, the shelter's sign-off stays with the people who use the system.
  **Status 2026-10-03 (staff role only, `claude/dry-run-staff`, PR #314):** the staff sheet was run phone-first in English and Thai with a desktop sweep; report at `docs/uat/dry-run-2026-10-03-staff.md` with 22 findings ranked by severity (none raised yet; Lutan reviews) and the corrections the checklist needs. Volunteer, vet, management, admin, public viewer and signed-out visitor remain, one run each; item stays open.
- [ ] **Review the staff dry-run report and spawn the backlog items you accept.** Staged 2026-10-03 from PR #314 (`claude/dry-run-staff`), the staff role's run of the item above. The report is `docs/uat/dry-run-2026-10-03-staff.md` (a printable PDF sits beside it in that worktree, uncommitted); read its five-minute executive summary first. **How to use this item:** each line below is one finding, already written as a backlog item — title, where, what happened, suggested fix — with the heading it belongs under. Go through them in order; for each one you accept, cut the line and paste it as a top-level item under that heading (dating it and crediting the dry run); for each one you reject, delete the line and say why in a word so the next role's run does not raise it again. The checklist corrections at the end go into `scripts/lib/acceptance-matrix-entries.mjs` as one small PR. Nothing here has been raised yet, and the dry run signed nothing — the shelter's sign-off is still the shelter's. Tick this item when every line has been promoted or struck.
  - [x] **F-01 · blocks a role · Staff cannot log a blood test.** **Done 2026-10-03** (`0131_blood_test_write_policies.sql`, `scripts/check-role-write-policies.mjs`; see `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-blood-test-write-policies.md`): staff and management get insert and update on `blood_tests`, nothing else changes. Original finding: Resident → Blood Tests → Log blood test. Saving answers "You don't have permission to do that" with or without a file, though the form, the "+" and the visit's "Log blood test" link are all offered and the manual says staff do this. `blood_tests` has only a *select* policy for staff (`0001_initial_schema.sql:534`); `procedures` got its staff insert and update in `0031`, blood tests never did, and no management policy was seen either. **Fix:** a schema PR adding staff (and management) insert/update policies on `blood_tests` as `0031` did for `procedures`, plus a role check script that covers it. → *Medical records*
  - [x] **F-02 · wrong result · Scan a chip never finds a resident.** Residents → Scan a chip; `src/app/residents/page.tsx:119`. A chip recorded a minute earlier returns "0 residents — No residents match these filters", and the chip-specific "not found, register?" message never shows. The test reads `/^[ds-]+$/` — the backslashes are missing, so it matches the letters d and s, never digits; in the file since 2026-09-29 (`108415ac`). **Fix:** `/^[\d\s-]+$/`, and a check that looks a chip up through the page rather than only the database. → *Quick wins* **Done 2026-10-03 (`claude/dry-run-bugs`):** the pattern, now `chipFromSearch()`; `scripts/check-chip-scan.mjs` looks a chip up through the page.
  - [x] **F-03 · wrong result · Before 07:00 Thai time today's date is refused as "in the future".** Log immunization and Log blood test. At 01:00 on 3 Oct both refused their own default date (3 Oct) — "Date administered can't be in the future", "Test date can't be in the future" — while weight, vet visits, death, stocktake and deliveries accepted it in the same hour. Early-shift staff meet this every morning until 07:00. Seen at one hour only; the UTC cause is inferred. **Fix:** use the shelter's "today" in these validations; grep for every other "can't be in the future". → *Medical records* **Done 2026-10-03 (`claude/dry-run-bugs`):** not two forms but three — procedures had it too — and the cause is proved, not inferred; see `docs/decisions/2026-10-03-dry-run-bugs.md`.
  - [x] **F-04 · wrong result · A resident cannot be moved on the day it arrived.** Resident → Move enclosure. Intake today, move today: "The move date must be after the current placement started." The only way round is to wait a day, when the record carries the wrong date; the same rule would refuse a hospital admission and return on one day. **Fix:** allow a same-day change (on or after), or say what to do instead. → *Resident operations* **Done 2026-10-03 (`claude/dry-run-bugs`):** same window as F-03 (intake is stamped 00:00 UTC), fixed for every placement change; see the decision.
  - [x] **F-05 · friction · The resident page is wider than a phone, so Edit and Record death are off-screen.** Resident hub header at 375 px: 423 px of content for every resident tried; the pencil is 8 px visible, the heart not at all; "Record chip", the intake date and "Copy link" are cut; the assistant's Close button goes off-screen with it. **Fix:** let the header's text column shrink (`min-w-0`) and wrap the icon row under the name at phone width. → *Mobile*

Card titles and pickers neither wrap nor shrink — "Blue Enclosure 3 (Hallway Small Dogs Only)" stretches every enclosure card to 468 px and pushes the capacity pill off the edge; the Enclosure picker grows to its longest name; Deliveries cuts its Note box; Contacts cuts the fourth button. The checklist's own R046 ("does not scroll sideways") fails. **Fix:** truncate or wrap titles, cap pickers at the column width, stack Cost and Note; add a 375 px overflow check to the gates. → *Mobile* **Done (claude/phone-width-fixes):** one base-layer rule fixed the grids; the "375 px overflow check in the gates" half was not built — see the backlog item for it.

A vet should see no Remove on any of the four, which is a *Must not* line. (2) **`/admin/recent-changes`** (#277, `requireAdminUser()`) joins the refused-route list. the *Can do* lines "`/vets` and `/contacts` open and read", "`/enclosures` opens" and "`/projects` opens and reads" now land on `/no-access`; and the `/maintenance` rough-edge line ("empty rather than forbidden … write down which it is") is answered — the page calls `requireRole(isShelterRole)` and a vet is refused, so the question should go and a plain refusal line replace it. **Pass 1 — what is missing:** the five *Must not be able to* routes are the test for finding 6 and should say so explicitly (refused **inside** the app at `/no-access`, not on the public home page); (b) the "create a recurring job assigned to the test account" step can no longer be done for a vet — `claude/recurring-jobs-eligible-assignees` removed vets from the picker, so the seed that exposed findings 5 and 6 is impossible by design. Rewrite it to seed the job for the Staff and Volunteer passes and to confirm, as a vet line, that the vet is *not* offered. **Not in scope:** Passes 2–6 were never run, so leave them as they are; this is the Vet pass and the Pass 0 lines it depends on. **Also worth doing while here:** every vet fix's `docs/test-plans/*.md` reads `Manual verification by: pending`, and pending on precisely these lines, so the rewritten pass should name which plans a completed run lets someone sign (`vet-scope-navigation`, `vet-clinic-resident-scope`, `vet-cross-clinic-writes`, `vet-visit-form-scoping`, `vet-doctors-roster-screen`, `recurring-jobs-eligible-assignees`, `prescription-no-future-visit`, `one-weight-per-visit`, `role-based-manual`, `release-notes-by-role`, `medical-photos-profile`, `mobile-date-clear`, `file-type-icon`). Docs only, no schema, no `src/` change. **Still not fixed and not this item's job:** finding 17, Cloudflare 1102 on the test site — `ORIGIN_HOST` is still `""` in all three environments of `wrangler.jsonc` and the Pi is deferred to 2026-10-02, so a full run may still be cut short. Finding 6's fix reduces the load (a refusal no longer renders the public home page) but does not remove the mechanism. **Done 2026-09-30** (`claude/walkthrough-vet-pass-update`): most of it landed in #225; this stream added what #227 changed (Appointments replaces My tasks, `/my` redirects) and a marked-pending note for the microchip feature.

The desktop `<aside>` in `src/app/manual/page.tsx` is already `lg:sticky lg:top-6` but has no height limit, so once the list is taller than the window it just scrolls away with the page. Cap it to the viewport (e.g. `lg:max-h-[calc(100dvh-…)]` allowing for the app header, plus `lg:overflow-y-auto` and `overscroll-contain`) so the contents stay put with their own scroll bar while the sections scroll beside them. Check sticky actually engages — if the app shell scrolls an inner container rather than the window, the offset and height must be measured against that — and consider scrolling the active entry into view in the list. The phone `<details>` version is unaffected. Update the manual's own description of the Contents panel in `src/lib/manual/en.ts` if it has one.

The manual's screenshots are `loading="lazy"` `<img>`s with no `width`/`height` (`Screenshot` in `src/app/manual/page.tsx`), so they load after the browser's jump to the anchor and push the text down. Give each screenshot its intrinsic size (store `width`/`height` in `ManualScreenshot`, or read them in `scripts/manual-screenshots.mjs` when capturing) so the space is reserved, then recheck a deep link cold. Closing it means blank space under the manual (e.g. bottom padding of about a screen height minus the last topic), which is a design call. The image-shift half of the old deep-link item is fixed.
- [x] **Release notes as an accordion: version and title first, details on click.** Done 2026-09-24: each release is a `<details>`, newest open; `OpenReleaseFromHash` opens the release a `#v<version>` link targets; no Expand all yet (three releases; `docs/decisions.md`). Customer request 2026-09-24. `/releases` (`src/app/releases/page.tsx`) renders every release in full — version, title, date, Major and environment badges, then every note — so it grows by a screenful with each release (0.2.0 alone has seven long notes) and older releases are only reached by scrolling past everything newer. Make each release a collapsed row showing **version · title**, the date and the badges, which expands to its notes on click. Native `<details>`/`<summary>` is enough — keyboard and screen-reader accessible with no client JavaScript, so the page stays a server component; style the marker as a chevron that turns when open. Decide while building: (a) whether the **newest release starts expanded** (probably yes — it is the one people come to read) with the rest collapsed; (b) the existing `id="v<version>"` anchors must still land on an **open** release, since links such as `/releases#v0.2.0` would otherwise point at a closed `<details>` with its notes hidden — open the targeted one (a small client effect reading `location.hash`, including on `hashchange`); (c) the dev-only "Not released yet" block stays expanded as it is; (d) optionally an Expand all / Collapse all control once there are enough releases to want it. Check it on a phone, where the badges already wrap under the title. The manual's release-notes topic gets a line saying to click a release to see what changed, and the PR adds its own line to `unreleased`.

- [x] **A role-based manual: show each role the manual for what it can do.** Done 2026-09-27 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/manual` opens on the reader's role; other roles' topics are hidden until found (Find on page and #anchors reveal them, greyed), a section with none of the reader's topics goes with its intro, and Show everything (`?view=all`) brings the rest back greyed. Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. The manual already knows the answer — every topic carries `roles` in `src/lib/manual/en.ts`, and the page shows them as badges — but it shows everybody everything, so a vet reads intake, stocktake, cashflow and Settings to find the seven medical topics that apply to them. Filter `/manual` to the signed-in role by default. **Keep a way to see the rest**, a "show everything" toggle, because "can I do this?" is a real question and an empty search result is a worse answer than a greyed one. Two things to get right: topics with no `roles` mean *everyone* and must not vanish; and the section intros describe whole sections ("For the management and admin roles…") so a filtered view needs the section to disappear too when nothing in it survives. Worth pairing with the vet-scope item — as the vet's menu shrinks, a manual that still explains Enclosures is actively wrong.
- [x] **Release notes segmented by role.** Done 2026-09-28 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `/releases` opens on the reader's role with the manual's `isForRole`; every release stays listed with its number, and one with nothing for the role carries a "Nothing for Vet" badge and says so; `?view=all` shows the rest greyed. A note may now be `{ text, roles }`; released history stays untagged, which reads as everyone. The release mail stays unfiltered. Raised by Lutan, 2026-09-27, Pass 1 (Vet) of the role walkthrough. Nice to have, and Lutan flagged the difficulty in raising it. A vet reading `/releases` gets stocktake, cashflow and deploy-tooling entries that will never mean anything to them. Tagging each note with the roles it affects and filtering the page the same way the manual would is the obvious shape. **The hard part is versions, not tags:** releases are numbered whole-app, so a vet filtering the list would see `0.7.0` followed by `0.4.0` with gaps where nothing touched them, which reads like a broken page. Options are showing every version with "nothing for you in this one", or dropping version numbers from the filtered view and showing dated changes. Also the release mail: it goes to admins, so it is not affected, but any per-role notes work should decide whether that stays true. Low priority — do the manual first, it has the same shape and more value.

## Public website

- [ ] **Clickable links in the text of the website's pages (e.g. DonorBox on Donate).** Lutan, 2026-10-04. The shelter takes donations through **DonorBox** (donorbox.org) among other methods, and the Donate page's text names the DonorBox address, but it shows as plain text a visitor has to copy into the address bar. On a phone, which is how most visitors arrive, that usually means they don't. **Why it happens:** page bodies typed on Settings → Website are rendered by `SiteBody` (`src/components/SiteBody.tsx`) through `parseBody()` (`src/lib/site/body.ts`), which understands paragraphs, `## ` sub-headings and `- ` bullet lists only. Everything else, a URL included, is printed as text. **Build:** (1) **Bare URLs become links on their own.** Any `https://…` or `www.…` in a body is linked, so what is already typed (the DonorBox address) starts working without anyone re-editing it. Strip trailing punctuation the way a reader expects ("…donorbox.org/lanna." links without the full stop). (2) **Links with words**, so the page can say "Give monthly on DonorBox" rather than show the raw address. Use Markdown's `[Give monthly on DonorBox](https://donorbox.org/…)`, since `parseBody` already borrows Markdown's `## ` and `- `. Also allow an email address (`mailto:`) and a phone number (`tel:`), which are just as useful on a phone. (3) **Safe by construction:** build the link as a React element from the parsed parts, never as HTML from the text; accept only `https:`, `http:`, `mailto:`, `tel:` and paths on this site (`/adopt`, `/friends/join`), and print anything else (`javascript:`, `data:`) as plain text. A link to another site opens in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"` and a visible "opens in a new tab" cue for screen readers. Style it so it reads as a link on the cream background, with enough contrast in both themes and a tap target of at least 44 px on a phone. (4) **Everywhere `SiteBody` is used:** the information pages (Donate, Foster, Volunteer, How to adopt, International adoption, Become a Shelter Friend) and the home page's story, so one change covers them all. Decide while building whether the same rule should reach other public text that staff type: project stories on `/our-work/[id]`, resident bios on `/adopt/[id]` and Shelter Friend blurbs. Probably yes for project stories and friends, which are where an outside link is natural, but check each renderer and say which in the decision file. (5) **Translations:** a Thai version goes through the translation queue, so a translator must keep the address part of `[…](…)` unchanged. Say so in the translation editor's hint, and have the translation check refuse a Thai version whose links differ from the English. (6) **The editor:** a one-line hint under each body field on Settings → Website ("Paste a web address and it becomes a link. For your own words: [words](https://…)"), and a preview that shows the link working before Save. **Also, separately and worth asking:** a proper **Donate on DonorBox** button on `/donate` (and perhaps the header's Donate button pointing there), with the address as a Website setting rather than typed in the text, so the most important donation link is a big button and not a line in a paragraph. That overlaps the Fundraising items ("Make `/donate` ask for money", the per-donation QR paper), which should mention DonorBox now that the shelter is known to use it. No schema for the links themselves. Manual (the Website topic), both dictionaries for the hints, a decision file, a releases line (visitors will notice links that work), and a test plan covering a bare link, a worded link, an email, a phone number, a refused `javascript:` link and a Thai page.
- [x] **Settings → Website: let the shelter choose its preferred contact channel for the public site.** Lutan, 2026-09-28. The public pages lead with **LINE** wherever they offer one way to get in touch: the sticky "Ask on LINE" bar at the bottom of each animal's profile (`src/app/adopt/[id]/page.tsx`), the "get in touch" card on site pages (`SitePageView.tsx`, "Email us or message us on LINE"), the footer's Contact us (`PublicFooter.tsx`) and the order of the phone menu's "Talk to us" buttons (`PublicHeader.tsx` / `PublicNav.tsx`, "LINE first"). But Lanna actually use **Facebook Messenger, Instagram and WhatsApp**, and with the multi-shelter plan each shelter will have its own habits, so this belongs in a setting, not the code. **The links are already settings:** `site_content` holds `contact_line`, `messenger_url`, `whatsapp_number` and `instagram_url` (0080), edited and validated on Settings → Website (`SiteSettingsForm.tsx`, `src/lib/links/validate.ts`). What is missing is the choice. **Add:** a "Preferred way to contact us" picker on Settings → Website (LINE / Messenger / WhatsApp / Instagram / Phone / Email), offering only channels that have a value, stored as e.g. `site_content.preferred_channel` (a small `-schema` PR first; default `line` so nothing changes until someone picks). Consider an ordered list instead of one choice, so the second and third buttons follow the shelter's order too. **Use it everywhere LINE is hard-coded first:** the sticky bar's label and link ("Message us on Messenger", `m.me` link; WhatsApp opens `wa.me/<number>` with the message pre-filled as the email subject is today; Instagram opens the profile, since it has no pre-filled DM link), the get-in-touch card and its hint text, the footer and the Talk to us order. One helper (e.g. `preferredChannel(content)` returning label, href and icon) so every page asks the same question. Fall back to the next channel set, then phone, then email, if the chosen one is cleared. Text in en and th for each channel's call to action. **Multi-tenancy:** `site_content` is one row today; the tenancy spike's `shelter_id` makes it one per shelter, so a setting here is already per-tenant — no extra design needed, but list it among the tables the spike covers. Manual: the Website settings topic; a releases line (visitors see the button change). **Schema half done 2026-09-29 (migration `0111`, `claude/schema-contact-channel`):** an ordered list, `site_content.preferred_channels text[]` default `{line}` (first = preferred, the rest = button order and fallback), not a single `preferred_channel`; see `docs/decisions.md`. **Feature half done 2026-09-29 (`claude/contact-channel-picker`):** the picker on Settings → Website, `preferredChannels(content)` in `src/lib/site/channels.ts`, and the four public surfaces all converted; see `docs/decisions.md`.

We can arrange the journey"). **Content to get from the Director** (the page ships with her text, not placeholder copy): what she offers (international and domestic moves, and which parts she handles: paperwork, vet checks and vaccinations, export permit, flights, road transport), her licence details (licence number and issuing body, and any wording the licence requires), typical timelines, whether it is for shelter adopters, the public or both, and how to get in touch. `generateMetadata` with Open Graph tags like the other public pages. A releases line. **Unblocked 2026-09-27 (Lutan): ship generic copy now, modelled on what a Thai pet-relocation service page covers** — e.g. Boonma (boonma.com), Thailand's oldest mover, whose pet service is one-stop: documents, vet checks and vaccinations, microchip, export permit, crate, flight booking, customs clearance at both ends, and door-to-door delivery, international and domestic. So the page no longer waits on the Director's copy, and the body is admin-editable anyway — she edits it herself when she has time. **Two things that do NOT become generic.** (a) **Write original text.** Do not copy Boonma's wording or anyone else's; describe what the service involves in our own words. (b) **Licence claims still come from her.** The generic "what's involved" content is safe, but a statement that *this* shelter or Director is licensed — licence number, issuing body, any wording the licence requires — is a factual claim about a real person and must not be invented or implied. Out of scope until the core app is wired up, but a natural later public feature: a public form (species, description, photo, location, contact) writing to a `lost_pet_reports` table that staff review from an admin list, with a link to the intake flow if the resident arrives at the shelter.
- [ ] **Publish to Facebook from the app (low priority, discuss first).** Raised 2026-09-22: could a resident profile, a project story or a "pet of the week" be posted to the shelter's Facebook Page (feed post, photo, or a 24-hour Story) from inside the app? Technically yes — the Meta Graph API publishes to a Page the app has been granted (`pages_manage_posts` + a long-lived Page token held as a Worker secret; Page Stories have their own photo/video endpoint; Instagram Business publishing is a sibling if the Page has an IG account). Personal profiles are not publishable by API. The cost is not code but Meta's App Review (screencast, business verification of the Page; `/privacy` already exists) and a token that needs refreshing. Before building anything, decide between: (a) no API at all — a **Share on Facebook** button on `/adopt/[id]` and `/our-work/[id]` that opens the share dialog pre-filled with the public URL (the OG tags are already there, so the card renders well) and staff post from their own account; (b) Meta Business Suite scheduling straight from the Page, no app changes; (c) the full integration — an Admin → Website action that composes a post from a resident or project (photo, bilingual caption from `translations`, link back) and publishes to the Page, with a log of what was posted. Start with (a) if anything; only go to (c) if staff are posting several times a week and the copy-paste is the pain.

Going from the home page to the dogs up for adoption (`/adopt`, linked from `src/app/page.tsx`) is usually fast enough that the puppy-at-a-laptop loader (`src/components/PuppyLoader.tsx`, root `src/app/loading.tsx`) never shows: it waits 300 ms before appearing so quick loads don't flash it. Add a short, deliberate pause on that page so people see the animation, e.g. 1.5–2 s. **How:** an `await` pause at the top of the `/adopt` server page (before its data fetch), so the route's `loading.tsx` streams the puppy during it. Only on that page's first load from navigation, not when changing filters or paging within it. **Keep it switchable, not hard-coded:** read the length from an env var (e.g. `SHOWCASE_LOADER_MS`, `0` or unset = no pause), set in `wrangler.jsonc` per environment, so it can be on for demos on test/UAT and turned off (or shortened) for real visitors in production without a code change — a slower page costs adopters and search ranking. Clamp it (say, at most 3 s) so a typo cannot hang the page. Decide with Lutan whether production gets it at all at launch; record the choice and the reason in `docs/decisions.md`. Check it on a phone: the animation plays through, the page arrives cleanly after it, and the back button does not replay the pause. No manual change (public page); a `src/lib/releases.ts` line only if production gets the pause.

- [ ] **Desexing drives under Services on the public site.** Lutan, 2026-09-27, while placing Pet relocation: the public menu got a **Services** group (`PublicHeader.tsx`) for what the shelter offers the public, as opposed to ways to help it. Pet relocation has since come off the site (International adoption replaced it, under Adopt), so Services is empty and hidden in the header and footer until this item gives it an entry. Desexing drives — days when people bring their own pets to be desexed — belong there too. **Scope decided by Lutan, 2026-09-28: dated events with sign-up, not an information page.** So this is **not** a `site_pages` slug and not a tiny schema PR — that was the alternative and it was turned down. It is **two streams in sequence**: `claude/schema-desexing-drives` (a real `desexing_drives` table plus a sign-ups table, RLS, and a `public_desexing_drives` view granted to `anon`, added to `scripts/check-public-views.mjs`), then `claude/desexing-drives-page` (the public route, the **Services** entry in `PublicHeader.tsx` / `PublicNav.tsx` and the footer, and a management list to create drives and read sign-ups). Services stays empty and hidden until the second one lands. **Do not hand the schema half to a session as a one-liner — it needs a scoping pass with Lutan first,** because the table shape follows answers nobody has yet: what a drive record holds (date, venue, species, capacity, whether a drive can be cancelled or full), what a sign-up holds (owner name and contact, pet name / species / sex, a slot or just the day), and above all **whether the public sign-up writes to the database at all or is a "message us on LINE" handoff**. That last one is the real decision: a public write path is a personal-data question before it is a table. If it does write, the sign-up list is never public (anon gets `insert` only, never `select`, and the public view exposes drives, not the people who signed up), it needs rate limiting, and there is no CAPTCHA option here — so decide what abuse is acceptable rather than assuming it can be blocked. Also: whether drives appear on the home page. No dates, prices or eligibility rules invented — those come from the shelter. Manual topic and a `src/lib/releases.ts` line with the page.
- [x] **Check the website's LINE link opens the shelter's LINE Official Account.** Noticed 2026-09-27 while building `/friends/join` (#175), not yet tested on a phone. `lineLink()` in `src/lib/site/content.ts` strips the `@` from an id like `@lannacare` and builds `https://line.me/R/ti/p/~lannacare`. As LINE documents it, `~` is the add-friend form for a **personal** LINE id, and an **Official Account** uses `https://line.me/R/ti/p/@lannacare` (the `@` kept, URL-encoded as `%40`). If the shelter's LINE is an Official Account, every LINE button on the site may be opening the wrong thing or nothing: the footer, the phone menu's Talk to us, the Get in touch cards, Ask on LINE on resident pages, and Shelter Friends cards (check `FriendCard` too, in case it builds its own). **First, on a phone with LINE:** tap the LINE button on test and see where it lands. Also find out from the Director whether the shelter's LINE is an Official Account or a personal id. **If it is broken:** keep `~` for an id typed without `@` and use `@` for one typed with it, so that both kinds work. Leave pasted URLs as they are. Keep `lineMessageLink()` (the prefilled message on `/friends/join`, which already uses the `oaMessage/@id` form) consistent with it. Update the Settings → Website hint if it says how to type the id. No schema. Add a releases line if it was broken for visitors.
- [ ] **Later: let the public register their own pet's microchip, as a start towards a national register.** Lutan, 2026-09-29. Microchipping is not standard in Thailand and there is no national register that most owners use, so once residents carry chip numbers (the Resident operations item), open a public page where an owner records their own pet's chip against their contact details, so a shelter that scans a found animal can reach them. **Depends on:** the resident microchip item first, and the multi-shelter spike (Architecture), since a register is only useful across shelters. **Scope a paper before building, like the merch and B2B items:** who runs and is accountable for the data (the shelter, a foundation, or the app's owner), PDPA consent and retention for owners' personal details, how an owner proves the animal is theirs and how a disputed or transferred chip is handled, what a scanning shelter is shown (a way to contact the owner through the system, not the owner's phone number and address), abuse and spam on a public form, and whether this should join an existing register rather than become another one (find out first whether the Department of Livestock Development or a local authority keeps one, and which international lookup services Thai-issued chips are listed with). No schema and no public form until that is agreed; the privacy stakes are close to those that took payroll off this backlog.

## Fundraising

- [ ] **Make `/donate` ask for money and record what arrives.** Customer request 2026-09-23. Today `/donate` is just one of the five `site_pages` slugs (0059) — a title and a body the admin types on `/admin/website`, rendered by `SitePageView` (`src/lib/site/pages.ts`) and translated through the manager's queue — so the shelter can *describe* how to give, but the site asks for nothing and the system records nothing. Note also that `contact_type` lost its `Donor` value in 0067 as unused, so a donor is currently not modelled at all. **Ask the customer before any schema:** which channels people actually use today — Thai bank transfer / PromptPay QR, cash at the shelter, a Facebook fundraiser, PayPal or card from overseas supporters — and who thanks them and keeps the list now. Then build to that answer, in this order. (a) **A give block on `/donate`** — a PromptPay QR image (Drive-hosted like the hero photo) plus bank account name and number and whatever overseas option they use, as nullable columns on `site_content` edited on `/admin/website` beside the contact block; no integration at all, covers most Thai giving, and worth shipping on its own. (b) **Record donations** — schema first, its own PR: a `donations` table (`amount numeric(12,2)`, `received_on date`, method, optional `contact_id` plus a free-text donor name for one-offs, optional designation to a resident or a project, note, `recorded_by`), an admin/management RLS pair, and a **Management → Donations** page to enter one, list a window and total it. That total is the income half the Cashflow forecast item above has no source for, so design the two to meet. (c) **Appeals with a target** — a campaign row (title, body, goal, deadline, cover photo) with a progress bar on `/our-work/[id]` and a "sponsor {name}" block on `/adopt/[id]`, summing the donations designated to it; the translation plumbing and the `site_pages` body renderer already cover the prose. (d) **Card / online payment**, only if the customer says they need it — a hosted checkout (Opn/Omise covers PromptPay and Thai cards, Stripe for overseas), keys as Worker secrets, a webhook route under `src/app/api` writing the donation row, and no card data ever touching the app or the Pi. Don't skip to (d): (a) and (b) are most of the value for none of the compliance. Manual topic, dictionary strings in both languages and a `docs/decisions.md` entry once the shape is settled.
- [ ] **A unique QR code per donation that records itself and links to the donor, the way TrueMoney and Lazada top-ups do — options paper first, then build.** Lutan, 2026-10-02. **How those apps do it:** each top-up gets its own **dynamic** Thai QR carrying the amount and a reference; when the payer's bank app pays it, the payment provider tells the merchant's system (a webhook), and the reference says exactly which order it was. A plain PromptPay QR (to a phone number or tax ID) has neither: no reference, and nothing tells the system the money arrived, so a donation can only be matched later from a bank statement. This item is that automatic path, and it builds on "Make `/donate` ask for money and record what arrives" just above (its step (b) `donations` table is the record this fills in; its step (d) named a hosted gateway). **The three ways to get there, for the paper to compare honestly:** (1) **a payment gateway** that issues a PromptPay QR per payment and sends a webhook when it is paid (Opn Payments, formerly Omise, which the `/donate` item already names; GB Prime Pay and 2C2P are other Thai options): least paperwork and quickest to build, at a per-transaction fee; (2) **the shelter's own bank** offering Thai QR bill payment with reference fields and a payment notification API (most Thai banks offer a corporate version): no middleman, but it needs a corporate account set up for it, a biller ID and the bank's onboarding; (3) **no integration**: a static PromptPay QR plus importing the bank statement and matching by amount and time, the fallback when (1) and (2) are not available, and fragile (two people giving 500 baht the same morning). For each, record: fees, what the foundation must provide to sign up (registration documents, a juristic-person account), settlement time, sandbox/test mode, and whether overseas donors can pay too (cards, which PromptPay does not cover). **Shape once chosen:** `/donate` (and a "sponsor {name}" button on a resident, and an appeal's page once those exist) asks for an amount, and optionally a name and contact for a receipt, then shows a QR made for that donation with a short reference and a countdown; the page turns to "Thank you, received" when the webhook arrives. The webhook route (`src/app/api/...`) verifies the provider's signature, is idempotent (the same notification twice records one donation), records the amount the **provider** confirms (never the amount the page asked for), and links the row to the donation it was created for: the donor's contact if they gave one, a resident or appeal if designated. Anonymous giving stays possible. Staff see it on Management → Donations from the `/donate` item. **Linking to the person:** a donor who leaves a name and contact becomes (or is matched to) a `contacts` row, by email or phone, with their consent recorded, so repeat donors build up a history; nothing personal is asked for to make a donation. **Tax receipts:** check whether the foundation is registered for the Revenue Department's e-Donation system; if donors want a tax deduction it may need their Thai ID number, which is sensitive personal data (PDPA), so collect it only when they ask for a receipt, store it apart and decide retention in the paper. **Must hold:** no card or bank details ever touch the app or the Pi; the webhook must be reachable even if the Pi is down (the Worker fallback answers it, and providers retry); keys are Worker secrets; money goes straight to the shelter's own account, never through the platform, which also keeps each shelter separate when there are several. **Deliverable:** `docs/donations-payments.md` comparing the options with real fees and sign-up requirements, a `docs/decisions/` entry once Lutan and the Director choose, then the build (schema PR first) in the provider's test mode end to end before any real money.

- [ ] **Scope an online merchandise shop — the deliverable is a scope, not a build.** Customer request 2026-09-23. The ask is selling shelter merchandise online (t-shirts, calendars, tote bags and the like); this item ends with `docs/merch-shop.md` plus a `docs/decisions.md` entry and a recommendation the customer can say yes or no to — no schema and no routes until they have. **Questions to answer with them:** what is actually for sale and at what price; who packs and posts a parcel (shelter staff in Suphan Buri? Thailand Post / Kerry / J&T, who pays the postage, domestic only or overseas too?), or whether print-on-demand removes stock entirely; whether collection at the shelter is the common case; how many orders a month realistically — that number decides everything below; who answers "where is my order"; stock counts, sizes and variants, and what a sold-out item does; receipts, refunds, and whether the shelter's tax position cares that this is trading income rather than donations. **The real decision is build vs. link:** (a) point the public site at a hosted storefront — LINE MyShop or a Facebook/Instagram Shop, which is where their supporters already are, or Shopify — so the app adds a nav link and nothing else, and the shelter gets payments, stock and shipping labels for free; (b) build `/shop` into this app, reusing the Drive photo plumbing, the `site_pages` copy pattern, the translation queue and the Worker, with `products` / `orders` tables and a hosted checkout; (c) a middle path — a catalogue in the app that hands off to (a) for payment. Weigh it honestly: (b) is only worth the work if an order needs to tie back to something the system already knows (a resident, an appeal, a supporter record shared with donations), and it puts payments, stock and customer support on infrastructure that is about to move onto a Raspberry Pi in Suphan Buri (`docs/pi-hosting.md`), where an hour of downtime is a lost sale rather than a staff member waiting. Read it next to the Fundraising item above: if both happen, a buyer and a donor are usually the same person and should not end up as two unrelated tables.

- [ ] **Investigate options for a drop-shipment B2B offering on the system — the deliverable is an options paper, not a build.** Requested 2026-09-24 by Lutan. The idea: sell goods through the system without the shelter holding stock or posting parcels, a supplier shipping direct to the buyer, with businesses as the buyers or the partners. **Blocked: do not start until the Director has answered the prerequisites below** (Lutan is taking them to the Director); record the answers here, dated, before the work begins. **Prerequisites (answers from the Director):** (1) **What "B2B" means here**, because the options differ completely. Is it (i) the shelter *sourcing* from a B2B drop-ship supplier (print-on-demand merch, or pet food and supplies from a Thai wholesaler) and selling on to supporters; (ii) the shelter *selling to businesses* (vets, pet shops, cafés, the companies on the "Shelter Friends" list) buying branded or bulk goods shipped direct by the supplier; (iii) businesses selling *through* the shelter's site with a cut going to the shelter (affiliate or marketplace); or (iv) something else, e.g. offering this system itself to other shelters? (2) **What would be sold**, roughly at what price, and to whom: supporters in Thailand only, or overseas too? (3) **Suppliers already in mind**: any wholesaler, printer or partner business the Director has spoken to, and any known margins or minimum orders. (4) **Who handles the customer side**: payment, VAT invoices / tax receipts for business buyers, returns and "where is my order". Shelter staff, the supplier, or nobody yet? (5) **Tax and legal position**: whether trading income (rather than donations) changes the shelter's status, and who can confirm that (their accountant?). (6) **Expected volume**: realistic orders a month, which decides whether anything is worth building. (7) **How it relates to the merch shop and donations**: one effort or separate ones? **Once answered, the work:** research which suppliers actually drop-ship inside Thailand (and overseas, if it matters), their integration (API, CSV feed, email order, or a manual order form), margins and minimums; work out what the system would have to hold for the chosen model (products, business accounts, orders, supplier routing); and **weigh build vs. link honestly**, as the merch item does: a hosted storefront or the supplier's own white-label shop may give most of the value for none of the payments, support and Pi-uptime risk (`docs/pi-hosting.md`). The deliverable is `docs/drop-shipping-b2b.md` plus a `docs/decisions.md` entry and a recommendation Lutan and the customer can say yes or no to. No schema and no routes until they have. Read it next to **Scope an online merchandise shop** and **Make `/donate` ask for money** above, and **"Shelter Friends"** on the public site: if more than one happens they should share one catalogue, one checkout and one record of who the buyer, donor or business is. Folding this into the merch scope as one of its options is a fine outcome.

## Completed

Done items, kept under the same headings as the open work above so follow-ups can be traced back to what they build on.

### Security

- [x] **Close the sign-in open redirect (WEB-1).** Done 2026-09-30: `safeNextPath` parses the value against a dummy origin, keeps it only if the origin is unchanged, refuses backslashes and control characters, and returns `pathname + search`; `scripts/check-next-path.mjs` holds the cases (see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-signin-next-parsed-not-prefix-checked.md`). Original brief: `safeNextPath` in `src/lib/auth/next-path.ts` rejects `//` but not `/\`, which browsers treat as `/`, so `?next=/\evil.com` sends a user off-site straight after a genuine sign-in. Parse with `new URL(next, "http://x")`, return null unless the origin is unchanged, reject backslashes and control characters, and return `pathname + search`. Five lines plus a test. **Do first.**

### Process

- [x] **Run several workstreams at once.** Done 2026-09-22: one git worktree per feature (`scripts/worktree.mjs new|dev|sync|list|done`), the main checkout stays on `main`, `/plan-day` syncs `main` and proposes the day's two or three non-overlapping streams from this file, schema changes land first as their own PR, CI type-checks/lints/builds every PR, and `docs/decisions.md` merges by union. `CLAUDE.md` "Workstreams", README "Day-to-day workflow", `docs/decisions.md`.

### Deployment

- [x] **Set the Pi up: production origin and weekly backup, one box.** **Done 2026-09-30, guided session** (PRs #237 and #238, then `claude/pi-setup`; see `docs/decisions/2026-09-30-one-pi-hosting-and-backup.md` and `docs/pi-hosting.md`). **Hosting:** the Pi (Debian 13, built from a micro SD then copied to the NVMe drive) serves lannacare.org (`x-lanna-served-by: pi`), `pi.lannacare.org` returns 403 without the WAF key, and the failover drill passed both ways (app stopped, tunnel stopped: `worker`, still 200; restarted: `pi`). Release 0.10.1 carried the switch. **Backup:** `backup.mjs --local-copy` added; two real production dumps taken on the Pi (one by hand, one under cron's minimal environment), uploaded to Drive and kept in `~/backups/lannacare` (`0700`/`0600`, outside the repo); Sunday 03:00 cron installed. **Still open (follow-ups on the `backlog` branch):** the first scheduled Sunday run (2026-10-04) has not fired, and the laptop task stays until it is confirmed; the backups are not encrypted (DB-1). Text below is the original brief. Lutan is home in Suphan Buri and ready to set it up (2026-09-30). **There is only one Pi** — corrected 2026-09-30. This item replaces the former pair ("Bring the Suphan Buri Pi up as the production origin" and "Move the weekly backup to the Raspberry Pi 5 at home"), which assumed two machines: a hosting box in Suphan Buri and a separate backup Pi 5 "at home". Lutan's home **is** in Suphan Buri and there is one Raspberry Pi 5, so both roles land on it. Treat anything written on the two-box assumption as wrong, including `docs/pi-hosting.md`'s "Second Pi (later)" section, which stays genuinely later. **Do it as a guided session, not a handover document:** Lutan is at the box and runs the commands, the session reads the output and decides the next step, and writes up what actually worked afterwards. **(1) Hosting.** The Worker is already deployed with the tunnel fallback logic and `ORIGIN_HOST` empty (2026-09-22). Follow `docs/pi-hosting.md` end to end: clone, copy the two env files, create the tunnel, `scripts/pi/setup.sh <tunnel-id>`, the WAF rule and `ORIGIN_KEY`, set `ORIGIN_HOST` and `npm run deploy:prod -- --secrets`, then the failover drill. `curl -sI https://lannacare.org/` should report `x-lanna-served-by: pi`. This also clears the Cloudflare 1102s on the test site, which were always the environment rather than a defect. **(2) Backup.** An always-on Linux box with a 2 TB SSD beats a laptop that has to be awake on Sunday mornings, and the SSD gives a second copy next to Drive. `scripts/backup.mjs` already runs there unchanged: it looks for `pg_dump` on `PATH` first. Install Node 22+ and `postgresql-client-17` from the PGDG apt repo (`apt.postgresql.org` has arm64 builds; Debian bookworm's own package is 15, which pg_dump refuses against a 17 server); copy `.env.local` + `.env.deploy.production` across (never commit them); run `node scripts/backup.mjs --env production` once by hand; then a cron line such as `0 3 * * 0 cd ~/Animal_Shelter_App && node scripts/backup.mjs --env production >> backup.log 2>&1`. Add a `--local-copy <dir>` flag (upload *and* keep a copy on the SSD, unlike `--local`) so the Pi holds the last N too. Then unregister the laptop task (`scripts\backup-schedule.ps1 -Remove`) and update README "Backups". **(3) The two roles now share a box, which the old split avoided.** Hosting was designed to hold no data ("The data is never on the Pi", `docs/pi-hosting.md`) while the backup role puts a production dump on the SSD — so the machine holding the backups now also runs an internet-reachable render service. The tunnel is outbound-only so the exposure is modest, but it is no longer zero: keep the dumps readable only by the backup user, never serve them from the Next.js app, and **Drive stays the off-site copy** — a Pi in the house is one flood or theft away from the data it protects. Record the single-Pi correction and this trade-off in `docs/decisions/`.

- [x] **Lock UAT (lannacare.org) and test.lannacare.org behind sign-in — no public access until go-live.** Done 2026-09-25 (PR #123): `"PUBLIC_SITE": "locked"` on the test, uat and production Workers; signed-out `/` is a sign-in landing page, everything else but `/login`, `/login/forgot`, `/auth/callback`, `/privacy` and `/robots.txt` redirects to sign-in and back, all responses noindex. **The cutover must drop the var from the production block.** Original brief: Lutan, 2026-09-25: lannacare.org is UAT for good (the live site moves to lannacareforanimals.org at the cutover, see `wrangler.jsonc`), so strangers should not land on the public pages there yet. Signed-out visitors to any page get a sign-in landing page instead of the public site; staff sign in as now. Keep the exceptions that must stay open: `/login`, `/login/forgot`, `/auth/callback`, `/privacy` (the Google consent screen links to it and to the homepage), `/api/photos/` only as far as signed-in use needs. Switch it on with a per-Worker var in `wrangler.jsonc`, not `getAppEnv()` (lannacare.org still reports "production" until the cutover sets `UAT_PROJECT_REF`), so the cutover can turn it off for the real live site. Lock test.lannacare.org too (Lutan, 2026-09-25). Add `noindex` while it's on.

- [x] **A third environment, `uat`, in the app and the deploy tooling.** Done 2026-09-23, plumbing only: `src/lib/app-env.ts` returns `dev | uat | production`, with `UAT_PROJECT_REF` left empty until the cutover sets it (it will be `dbkodyyxxhtygxcxmfcu`, production's today). UAT keeps production's colours; a **UAT** badge in `AppHeader` and a diagonal watermark on the archive PDF are the only differences (dev builds get a `DEV` watermark too, since they share UAT's Drive). `wrangler.jsonc` has a `uat` block (`lanna-animal-care-uat`, no routes yet), `--env uat` works in every `scripts/lib/env.mjs` script with `.env.deploy.uat`, `npm run deploy:uat` exists, and `deploy-pi.sh` knows its service and host. `deploy.mjs` now refuses any environment whose project app-env would badge as another, so `deploy:uat` refuses until the cutover, and UAT gets production's clean-main and written-release guards. No thin top strip: the badge was judged enough. README "Environments" lists what the cutover changes.
- [x] **Builds no longer type-check the dev server's `.next/dev/types`.** Done 2026-09-22: `tsconfig.build.json` (`tsconfig.json` minus `.next/dev`) is what `next build` type-checks with (`typescript.tsconfigPath` in `next.config.ts`, keyed on NODE_ENV so `next dev` and the editor keep `tsconfig.json`) and what `npm run typecheck` runs `tsc` against. Reproduced with truncated `routes.d.ts` / `validator.ts` under a running dev server: `next build` failed before, and `npm run opennext:build` (the deploy script's build step) completed after with the same files in place. Root cause is Next 16's `experimental.useTypeScriptCli` default, which hands the tsconfig straight to `tsc --project`; Next's own checker filters `.next/dev/types` out and the CLI path doesn't. Original brief: **A production build can fail type-checking on half-written `.next/dev/types/*.ts`.** Seen 2026-09-22: `tsconfig.json` includes `.next/dev/types/**/*.ts`, which a running `next dev` rewrites; when `next build` type-checks while that is mid-write (or after an interrupted build wiped `.next`), it fails with a wall of `TS1005 ';' expected` in `routes.d.ts` / `validator.ts` and the deploy stops.
