### Public website

- [x] **Thai translation of home page content.** Done 2026-09-21 (see `docs/decisions.md`, "Public site pages"): the story moved into `site_pages` (0059) so its title and body go through the translations table and queue; tagline, hero alt and visiting hours are paired `_th` columns edited side by side on `/admin/website`. Original brief: `site_content` (tagline, story heading/body, hero alt) in Thai, picked by locale with English fallback on `/`. Since 2026-09-21 the way to do this is the translations table, not paired columns: add the columns to `translatable_fields`, attach the `queue_translations` / `drop_translations` triggers to `site_content`, expose `approved_translations('site_content', id)` wherever `/` reads it, and give the admin Website page a `TranslationPanel` per field — the manager queue then covers it for free. Short labels like the tagline could arguably stay paired columns (see the decision on `name_th`); decide per field.
- [x] **Visiting hours + adoption process on public pages.** Done 2026-09-21: `visiting_hours` / `_th`, `contact_phone`, `contact_line`, `contact_map_url` on `site_content` (0059); the `how-to-adopt` page in `site_pages` renders as a section at the foot of `/adopt` (`#how-to-adopt`); `/adopt/[id]` has the "Where to meet {name}" card with address, hours, email, LINE, phone and the can't-hold note linking to it. Original brief: Add `visiting_hours` (per-day text or JSON) and `adoption_process` (markdown/long text) to `site_content`, editable from `/admin/website`. On `/adopt/[id]` replace the bare mailto footer with a "Where to meet {name}" block (address from `contact_address`, hours by day, email/LINE contact) plus an honest note that residents may be adopted before a visitor arrives and can't be held over the phone, linking to a short "Our adoption process" section/page. Modelled on RSPCA ACT's pet profile footer.
- [x] **Filters on the public adoption listing.** Done 2026-09-21: species chips, size chips and a "Ready for adoption" toggle on `/adopt`, all URL search params, chips shown only for values present. Original brief: `/adopt` is a flat grid sorted by name. Add a species filter (Dog / Cat / Other, from the values in `public_resident_profiles`) and a "Ready for adoption" toggle, driven by URL search params so links are shareable and the page stays server-rendered. Keep it a simple chip/select row above the grid; no JS-heavy filter panel needed.
- [x] **"Similar residents" on the public resident page.** Done 2026-09-21: `loadSimilarResidents` (`src/lib/residents/public.ts`) — up to four same-species residents, ready first, shuffled — rendered with the shared `ResidentCard`. Original brief: At the bottom of `/adopt/[id]`, query up to four other rows from `public_resident_profiles` with the same species (excluding the current one, `ready_for_adoption` first, then random or by name) and render them as the same cards used on `/adopt`. Mirrors the "Similar animals to Harry" strip on RSPCA ACT.
- [x] **Adoption Recommendation fields on residents.** Done 2026-09-21 (0060, see `docs/decisions.md`): `good_with_dogs` / `_cats` / `_children` (Yes / No / Unknown), `energy_level`, `colour`, `is_desexed` on residents via one shared `AdoptionProfileFields` fieldset on intake and edit, summarised on the hub, and the "Is {name} right for you?" block plus Desexed / Vaccinated ticks on `/adopt/[id]`. Vaccinated is derived from `immunization_records`; no microchip field (not done in Thailand). Original brief: RSPCA ACT shows a separate "Adoption Recommendation" block (dog friendly / cat friendly / energy level / household activity / child recommendation) plus size and colour. Migration adding to `residents`: `good_with_dogs`, `good_with_cats`, `good_with_children` (nullable enum yes/no/unknown), `energy_level` (low/medium/high), `size` (small/medium/large), `colour` text. Expose the non-sensitive ones through `public_resident_profiles` (CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW — append columns only), add them to `/residents/[id]/edit` and the resident info tab, and render an "Is {name} right for you?" block on `/adopt/[id]`. Also surface Desexed / Vaccinated / Microchipped flags publicly if the data exists in the medical tables (otherwise add booleans on `residents`). These fields later enable richer `/adopt` filters.
- [x] **Foster, Volunteer and Donate public pages.** Done 2026-09-21: `/foster`, `/volunteer`, `/donate` from `site_pages` (0059) through one `SitePageView`, in `PublicHeader` (Donate as a button) and a "How you can help" strip on `/`; "Happy endings" strip on `/adopt` from `public_recent_adoptions` (0061). Also added while here: a shared `PublicFooter`, the "In vet care" tile now counts residents on medication or in hospital (0062) and a fourth tile for foster care. Also consider a **"Recently adopted"** page or strip (happy-ending stories): adopted residents are deliberately hidden from `/adopt` (0025), so this would read `placement_history` `Adopt` rows (say the last 90 days) joined to a public-safe resident view — name, photo, adoption month, no carer details — rather than the adoption listing views. Content lives in `site_content` (or a small `site_pages` table keyed by slug) so admins edit it from `/admin/website`; English + Thai columns following the Thai-translation item above. Donate page holds bank transfer / PromptPay details and what donations fund.
- [x] **Share button and Open Graph tags on public resident pages.** Done 2026-09-21: `generateMetadata` on `/adopt/[id]` (photo as `og:image`, first sentence of the bio), `/adopt`, `/` (hero + tagline) and the three new pages; `ShareButton` uses the Web Share API with a clipboard fallback. Do the same OG treatment for `/` and `/adopt`.
- [x] **Shelter impact stats on the home page.** Done 2026-09-21 with the management dashboard (see `docs/decisions.md`): `public_shelter_stats` (0039) and a three-tile strip under the hero on `/` — in care, adopted this year (with last-7-days), in vet care. Create a `public_shelter_stats` view (granted to `anon`) that derives counts from `resident_current_state` (Resident + Fostered + Hospitalised = in care; Hospitalised = under vet care) and from `placement_history` (`Adopt` placements with `start_date` in the last 7 days). Render three stat tiles with a one-line explainer each near the top of `/`. Public-safe: counts only, no names or IDs.
- [x] **Pet of the Week on the home page.** Done 2026-09-21 (see `docs/decisions.md`): `site_content.featured_resident_id` (0041), a single-select `ResidentPicker` on `/admin/website` fed from `public_resident_profiles`, and a card on `/` between the gallery and "Ready to meet everyone?" that is looked up through the same view, so it hides itself when the resident stops being public. On `/` render a featured card (profile photo, name, species/breed, first paragraph of `bio`) linking to `/adopt/[id]`. Hide the section when unset or the resident is no longer public. Mirrors RSPCA ACT's "Dog/Cat of the week".
- [x] **"Our work" — project stories on the public site.** Done 2026-09-21 (see `docs/decisions.md`): 0042 adds the `public_projects` / `public_project_photos` views (anon SELECT only, checked by `check-public-views.mjs`), `/our-work` lists published folders as cards with URL-driven category chips, `/our-work/[id]` has the captioned gallery, story and Open Graph tags (cover as `og:image`), `PublicHeader` links Adopt / Our work, `/` shows the three newest under Pet of the week, and `/admin/website` lists what is published with a one-click "Remove from website". Original brief: Depends on the Project work item under Admin. Create a `public_projects` + `public_project_photos` view pair (granted SELECT only to `anon` / `authenticated`, following 0025: filtered on `is_public`, exposing title, summary, date, category, cover photo and captioned photos — never `uploaded_by` or Drive folder IDs). Public `/our-work` page listing projects as cards (cover photo, title, date, category, first line of summary; category filter via search params like the `/adopt` filters item) and `/our-work/[id]` with the story and photo gallery, linking from `PublicHeader` and a "What we do" strip on `/` showing the three most recent public projects. Pick title/summary/captions by locale with English fallback. Add `generateMetadata` (OG image = cover photo) so stories share well on Facebook and LINE, same as the resident-page item below. Once live, project stories are also the obvious content for the Foster / Volunteer / Donate pages ("here's what your donation funded").
- [x] **Rethink the public site's navigation as a whole — mega menu, hamburger, or both.** Superseded 2026-09-25 by "Public site redesign" (Next up): Lutan's mockups settle the grouping and the phone menu. Raised by Lutan 2026-09-24 after Shelter Friends added a sixth section link. The public header (`src/app/adopt/PublicHeader.tsx`) is a flat row that wraps: Home, Adopt, Our work, Foster, Volunteer, Shelter Friends (only once someone is published), the Donate button, the language switch and Open the app / Login — and each feature adds one more. On a phone it already wraps to three lines before the page starts (see `/friends` at 375px), and the Facebook-link item wants to add an icon there too. Look at it holistically rather than one link at a time: group the sections (e.g. **Adopt** · **Get involved** → Foster, Volunteer, Donate · **About us** → Our work, Shelter Friends, our story), then choose the pattern — a hamburger / slide-out on phones, and on desktop either the same grouped row or a mega menu with a line of description and an image per section (RSPCA ACT and similar shelter sites are the references). Keep Donate a button visible at every width and the language switch reachable without opening a menu. Decide the footer's "How to help" columns to match, so header and footer tell the same story. Deliver a short proposal (a mock-up or two) to agree with Lutan before building; accessibility (keyboard, `aria-expanded`, focus trap in the drawer) is part of the build, not a follow-up.



- [x] **Database check on `contact_map_url` (WEB-6 follow-up).** `site_content.contact_map_url` is now checked with `checkHttpsUrl` in `admin/website/actions.ts`, but unlike the social links (0080) the column has no database check, so a write that skips the action is unconstrained. Add the same `~* '^https?://'` check as a small schema PR (additive, `not valid` then validate if any existing row is non-conforming). Done 2026-10-01: `0120_contact_map_url_check.sql`; the one existing row was NULL, so a plain validated check, no `not valid` step.

- [x] **`apply-migrations.mjs` should warn when it applies a file whose consumer is not yet deployed.** Split from the `deploy.mjs` schema check (2026-10-01), which guards the deploy end; this is the same information read from the apply end. Needs a way to know a migration's consumer (e.g. a `-- consumer: <path>` header line, or checking whether the column is selected under `src/` on a commit newer than the live release); the repo holds no such map, so it is not a few lines. **Done:** header-line version (`-- consumer:`), warn-only, live release assumed from `package.json`; inference rejected — see `docs/decisions/2026-10-02-migration-consumer-header.md`.

- [x] **The app should answer "what version am I?" without a sign-in, from the origin rather than the Worker.** Raised 2026-10-02 by `apply-migrations-consumer-warning`: `/api/releases/current` is answered by `worker/index.mjs` before the origin is consulted, so nothing can ask what the Pi is running. `apply-migrations.mjs` has to *assume* the newest cut release is live, so an undeployed cut reads as live. An unauthenticated version route served by the app itself (version plus git sha) would let that check, and `deploy.mjs`, be exact.



### Moved out of test plans, 2026-10-04

These were written as rows in `Left for manual verification` tables and sat there undone — some for over a week, one written twice in two days. They are tasks, not checks, and `docs/manual-verification-queue.md` is the argument for why that is how they stayed undone. Moved here on Lutan's instruction; the plans they came from keep a pointer to this section rather than the row.
